No second "surface" layer. `outcome` is a **4-value** enum (`closed` / `follow_up` / `lost` / `no_show`) with `outcome_source` (`inferred`/`manual`/`crm`) — no confidence/evidence fields.

#### 2026-07-21 — Auth diagnostics shipped; Issue-1 root cause identified (fix pending overnight data)
- **Overnight logout RECURRED despite the scout-auth.js hardening.** Root cause found: **dashboard.html carries its OWN parallel auth layer that overrides scout-auth.js** — a naive `refreshSessionIfNeeded()` (treats transient failures as fatal, bypasses the cross-tab lock via a raw `/auth/refresh`) used at boot, plus ~15 inline `if (res.status === 401) { clearSession(); location.replace('/login') }` handlers that nuke the session on any 401 — including the ones scout-auth deliberately returns WITHOUT clearing on a transient failure. So the transient-tolerance + cross-tab lock are neutralized by the page. The same page-level pattern exists in admin.html. **Fix (deferred until overnight beacon data confirms):** delete the page's naive refresh + 401 handlers and route everything through `window.ScoutAuth`.
- **Persistent auth diagnostics (shipped, NOT the fix).** `scout-auth.js`: a localStorage RING BUFFER `scout_auth_diag_v1` (≤200 events, survives reloads; read via `scoutAuthDump()`), a BEACON to **`POST /auth/diag`** (unauthenticated; logs `[auth-diag]` to Railway) on failures + logout triggers (`refresh-fatal-cleared`, `refresh-transient-kept`, `redirect-to-login`, `boot-no-session`), and a **boot snapshot** logging UA + which auth keys were present BEFORE any refresh — distinguishes "refresh failed" from "storage already empty" (Safari-ITP idle eviction). dashboard.html: its `clearSession()` beacons `page-clearSession` with a stack trace (identifies which of the ~15 handlers fired), `refreshSessionIfNeeded()` beacons its failure mode, and `init()` redirects beacon `page-init-redirect` with a reason. `ScoutAuth.diag = {push,beacon,snapshot,dump,clear,tab}`. **Cron ruled out** (touches only `fathom_connections`, never Supabase auth). `auth.audit_log_entries` is empty (GoTrue audit logging off) — check Supabase Dashboard → Logs → Auth for server-side revocation evidence. Known suspect to confirm via UA in beacons: **Safari ITP** clears localStorage after ~7 days idle (and is more aggressive on some setups).

#### Shipped 2026-07-21 — verdict extended to follow_up (grader v5)
- **`ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION='v5-2026-07-21'`.** The review-page verdict now covers `follow_up` calls too (the majority — ~60% of Josh's corpus). The grader produces `why_outcome` for `closed`/`lost`/`follow_up`; only `no_show`/null stay null. For `follow_up` the reason is the **primary BLOCKER** keeping it open (deal alive, not lost), paired with `one_thing` as the **next move** that advances it. Persist gate `isWinLoss` → `producesVerdict` (adds follow_up). Frontend `whyOutcomeState()` adds `follow_up → {kind:'pending'}`; renders an amber "Why this call hasn't closed yet" section (`.review-why.pending`, `--mid`) with "The move / One thing to do next". `no_show` still hidden. **Root-cause note:** the earlier "Towana Joseph shows no verdict" was NOT a bug — it was a `follow_up` correctly getting a null verdict under v4; v5 is the product change that gives follow_ups a verdict. The v5 bump makes all 38 v4 calls outdated → the "Update analyses (38 outdated)" button returns → re-grade populates follow_up verdicts.

#### Shipped 2026-07-20
- **Persistent web login — overnight-logout fix (`scout-auth.js`).** Confirmed Supabase auth settings: single-session OFF, time-box never, inactivity never, refresh-token rotation ON with **compromised-token (reuse) detection ON**, reuse interval ~10s; access-token TTL 60 min. Two root causes fixed: **(1) transient failures were treated as fatal** — `refreshSession` now only clears the session on a definitive HTTP 400/401; network errors / 5xx / 429 keep the session so a wake-time blip can't log you out. **(2) cross-tab refresh race** (the prime suspect given reuse detection) — two tabs waking overnight both replayed the same rotating refresh token; the one landing outside the 10s window tripped reuse detection and revoked the whole family. Refreshes are now serialized ACROSS tabs via `navigator.locks` (falls back to a localStorage lock with stale-takeover); every waiter re-reads localStorage first (adopts a token another tab already rotated) so only ONE network refresh happens per rotation. On a 401 the interceptor passes the stale access token so a sibling-tab rotation is adopted with no network call; a final localStorage re-read happens before any redirect to `/login`. Wake triggers (`visibilitychange`/`focus`/`online`) supplement the 4-min timer (frozen during sleep). Always-on console instrumentation prefixed `[scout-auth <ISO>]` for morning diagnosis. **Repro/test:** leave the dashboard open in TWO tabs overnight.
- **Fathom auto-sync cron + always-available re-grade (Phase 1.5).** The per-user sync core is factored into `syncUserCalls(admin, userId, conn)`, reused by the authed `GET /fathom/sync` and a new **`POST /fathom/sync-all`** (shared-secret `X-Cron-Secret: CRON_SECRET`, NOT requireAuth — cron has no session). `sync-all` iterates every `fathom_connections` row with `fathom_email` set, syncs each, and logs+continues on per-user failure. The `synced_unanalyzed` holdback is respected for free (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING → held rows never re-insert → never dispatched; NEW calls arrive `pending` and analyze). **Cron mechanism LIVE: GitHub Actions scheduled workflow** — `.github/workflows/fathom-sync.yml` runs `0 */2 * * *` (+ `workflow_dispatch`), POSTing `/fathom/sync-all` with the `CRON_SECRET` repo secret. Chosen over Railway-native cron because the Railway CLI was unauthenticated at setup and GH Actions needs no second Railway service; `gh secret set CRON_SECRET` is done, secret also stored in gitignored `API Keys.md` under "## Cron / Auto-sync". **STILL REQUIRED:** set the SAME `CRON_SECRET` value on the Railway **web service** env — until then `/fathom/sync-all` returns 503 and the workflow fails (that failure is the signal). Alternative trigger kept: Railway native cron on a dedicated service running `node backend/scripts/cron-sync.js` (same schedule) — either hits the same endpoint. **Re-grade availability:** `GET /fathom/status` now returns `outdated_count` = **all `call_analyses.status='done'` rows for the user whose `prompt_version != CURRENT`** (NULL counts; held calls carry `status='synced_unanalyzed'` so they're excluded for free — `outdatedCallIds()` no longer joins `fathom_calls.sync_status`, which under-counted after a deploy-race; see Session Learnings 2026-07-20), and **`POST /fathom/update-analyses`** resets that batch (`fathom_calls.sync_status` + `call_analyses.status` → `pending`) and fires the analyze loop. Dashboard shows **"⟳ Update analyses (N outdated)"** when `pending_count===0 && outdated_count>0`. `ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION` is now exported from the worker so the route can compare. **Requires:** set `CRON_SECRET` in Railway (web + cron service) and configure the cron service.
- **First-sync analysis cap = newest 20 — FIRST SYNC ONLY (ruling 2026-07-31, FD-4 Option B; scope corrected 2026-07-31).** On the FIRST sync (connection has no `last_sync_at` yet) auto-analysis is capped to the newest `FIRST_SYNC_ANALYZE_CAP` (=20) of the backlog; the rest insert as `sync_status='pending'` for the Update-analyses backfill. On EVERY subsequent (steady-state) sync, ALL new calls are auto-analyzed. `fathom.js` `callIdsToAnalyze(newRows, lastSyncAt, cap)` is the selector (TDD'd in `first-sync-cap.test.js`); Zoom imports the SAME function. **Why first-sync-only:** "analyze everything on connect" is unbounded — a customer with years of history would auto-fire hundreds of Claude analyses on connect. But capping EVERY run (the original FD-4 implementation, fixed 2026-07-31) was wrong: a single busy day / manual sync / cron-gap-recovery bringing >20 NEW calls in one run would silently leave the remainder ungraded (they'd sit `pending`, only reachable via backfill). Steady-state windows are naturally bounded by real call volume, so analyzing all of them is safe and correct. The **Update-analyses dropdown (7d/30d/all-time, dry-run count + confirm)** remains the deliberate path for the first-sync backlog tail. `orderBatchIds` stays ordering-only; batch SIZE is the caller's (first-sync cap 20 via `callIdsToAnalyze`, or re-grade `limit` default 10). **Zoom mirrors this exactly (sub-stage 2, shipped 2026-07-31).**
- **"Why this call closed / didn't close" on the call review page (grader v4).** `ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION='v4-2026-07-20'`. For win-class (`closed`) and loss-class (`lost`) calls the section grader now emits `why_outcome {reason, quote, timestamp_seconds}` — the single most decisive cause of the outcome anchored to one transcript moment — plus `one_thing_timestamp_seconds`. For a **lost** call, `one_thing` is required to be the direct antidote to `why_outcome`, so they render as **cause → correction**. `why_outcome` is forced null for `follow_up`/`no_show`; timestamps are clamped to the recording (`boundTs`, mirrors the highlight bound). **Schema:** migration 018 adds `why_outcome` / `why_quote` / `why_timestamp_seconds` / `one_thing_timestamp_seconds` to `call_analyses` — **one** `why_outcome` column (not `why_closed`+`why_not_closed`), interpreted by the call's outcome. **Review page:** new section between header and highlights — loss-class "Why this call didn't close" (red accent: cause + quoted moment + ▶ clip, then the paired "One thing to do differently" beneath it, footer duplicate suppressed); win-class "Why this call closed" (green accent + clip, `one_thing` stays in the highlights footer); other/no-outcome hidden. **Graceful degradation:** pre-v4 analyses have `why_outcome=null` → section hidden, `one_thing` renders standalone as before. **No forced re-analysis** — the 9 pending + future calls populate the full section naturally. `/fathom/calls/:id` select now returns `outcome` + the four new fields.
- **Live Sessions toggle RETIRED (DORMANT).** The Coaching / Live Sessions toggle is hidden in ALL ranges regardless of data — the Live Sessions view is desktop-teleprompter-era and fully retired from the dashboard until the teleprompter returns. `renderModeToggleHtml()` early-returns `''`; the former toggle markup is commented in place directly beneath it. The view logic (`renderOverview`'s `'sessions'` branch, `hasLiveSessions()`, `setOverviewMode()`) is kept intact but unreachable — nothing sets `overviewMode='sessions'` anymore. To revive: delete the early return in `renderModeToggleHtml()`. (Superseded the 2026-07-20 "auto-hide when zero sessions" behavior — now hidden unconditionally.)

#### v1.4 Team Layer — COMPLETE (2026-07-22: digest live + accepted; EOD Report arc accepted)
Design approved with 5 mods: (1) role migration has a **staged, tested rollback**; (2) manager daily digest is **CRON-generated** for everyone-with-reps after the morning sync (not on-first-view); (3) **owner-as-manager** — `managed_by` may point at a `manager` OR `owner`; **"has assigned reps" (not role)** grants the manager-view experience; owner-with-reps lands on their own team by default; owner team-picker = My team | each manager's team | All users; (4) a **hard post-data-flip verification checkpoint** (owner / manager / user / owner-with-reps) before any UI; (5) rest as designed — manual creation w/ temp password, pivot tightening (managers→own reps, owners→anyone), account-lock rules, permissions matrix (+ owner-with-reps row).
- **Stage 1 (migration 019, applied):** role `admin`→`manager` (backup table + tested `019_rollback.sql`; the test caught that `user_profiles_role_check` only allowed owner/admin/user → migration now drops→flips→re-adds `(owner,manager,user)`). Added `billing_status` (trial|active|past_due|canceled, default trial) + `no_self_manage` check. RLS 3 policies `admin`→`manager`. Prod flip was a no-op (0 admins).

- **Stage 6 (Manager daily digest) — LIVE + ACCEPTED (2026-07-22).** `lib/team-digest.js`: one digest per user-with-assigned-reps (has-reps NOT role — owner-with-reps included) covering the previous **America/New_York** calendar day (DST-safe via Intl; tested incl. both transition days). Rides `objection_synthesis_cache` as `synthesis_type='digest'` keyed `(manager, from_ts=date@00:00Z, to_ts=nextDate@00:00Z, set_hash)`; the hash folds in analyses + **call ids** (so synced-but-unanalyzed days don't collide with true zero-call days) + kbHash. Cache hit = no Claude call → the every-2h cron is idempotent per day. Zero-call day = deterministic "quiet day" digest, cached, zero Claude spend. Generation: fire-and-forget pass after the `/fathom/sync-all` loop (per-manager error-isolated — a digest failure can never fail or block sync, same degrade rule as KB) + owner-only **`POST /team/digest/run`** `{date?}` for on-demand testing. Read: `GET /team/digest?date=YYYY-MM-DD` (default ET-yesterday) now also returns `recent_dates` (last 7 stored days) which the panel renders as history chips. Panel renders date/quiet badge, stat tiles, summary, ≤3 notable moments (rep-attributed, clip links resolved from real rows only), and a "Today's focus" accent box. Grader untouched — ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION stays v6. Tests: `backend/test/team-digest.test.js` (ET math, cache-key convention, hash discipline, quiet shape).

- **Manual-outcome-override protection — IMPLEMENTED (confirmed 2026-07-31; was logged as a TODO 2026-07-22).** `analyzeCall` is NOT a blind upsert: before writing, it reads the existing row's `outcome_source`, and when `='manual'` it FREEZES `outcome` / `outcome_source` / `outcome_set_at` / `outcome_set_by` (read-before-write guard, `manualLocked` in analysis-worker.js ~L885). The frozen `effectiveOutcome` also drives `close_score` (Thread 2) + `payment_structure`. So a re-analysis / backfill can NEVER re-stamp a human-set outcome — this is why the v9→v10 30-day backfill is safe. (Confirmed against live data 2026-07-31: 0 rows have `outcome_source='manual'` — 182 null + 135 inferred — so nothing is even at risk yet, but the guard holds structurally for when an outcome-edit UI lands.) The EOD edit layer (`eod_edits`) separately shields the EOD view's outcome field. The v7 delta-gate note (borderline `lost→follow_up` flicker ~1-in-6 on re-analysis) is why the guard matters: inferred outcomes CAN move on re-grade — only manual ones are frozen.

- **Call Review Context — Part 1a (data) BUILT 2026-07-31 (LOCAL, unpushed; Justin's ruling: section-tag in-extractor, backfill last 30 days, inline two-group UI).** Spec: clicking a section score on the Call Review page breaks it into that section's own good/bad highlights (Part 1b UI; the Add-to-KB button inside is Part 2, not built). Part 1a ships the data layer: (1) the highlight extractor now emits a **`section`** per moment (intro/discovery/pitch/objection/close) — riding the EXISTING extractor call, no extra Claude call; (2) **migration 028** adds `call_highlights.section` text NULLABLE + CHECK (NULL or the five sections) — nullable because old rows / pre-backfill calls legitimately have none; (3) `lib/highlight-section.js` holds `VALID_HIGHLIGHT_SECTIONS` + `sanitizeSectionValue` (used by `sanitizeHighlights`, coerces bad/missing → null, never drops a moment) + `highlightGroup(h)` → 'good'|'bad' (the two-bucket split: strong/rapport/buying + handled-objection = good; missed/disqualify + partial/unhandled/unknown-objection = bad) — TDD'd in `highlight-section.test.js` (8). (4) **`ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION` v9→v10-2026-07-31** (additive extractor field; grader scoring/outcome prompts UNCHANGED, so no delta-gate). Highlights are DELETE+INSERT on re-analysis, so backfilled calls get fresh section-tagged rows. **BACKFILL PLAN (reviewed step, NOT auto): last 30 days only** via `POST /fathom/update-analyses` `scope:'30d'` — est. ~134 calls ≈ ~268 Claude calls (grader+extractor; extractor-only re-run isn't possible); exact count from a `dry_run:true` after deploy. Older calls stay on v9 with no section tags → Part-1b UI falls back to the section-notes prose (graceful, required). Manual outcomes survive (guard above; 0 exist anyway). **Sequence when approved: apply migration 028 → push → deploy-verify → backfill dry-run (report count) → confirm → execute. Part 1b (UI) is separate, after 1a approval.** **Also fixed here (data-safety, applies to ALL analyses Fathom+Zoom): highlight persistence was DELETE-then-INSERT (a failed/empty extract wiped a call's highlights). Now `persistHighlights` (analysis-worker.js, TDD'd in `persist-highlights.test.js`, 5) inserts the new set FIRST, then deletes exactly the prior rows — a failed/empty extract or a failed insert keeps the existing highlights untouched (critical for a 134-call backfill where a mid-run hiccup is realistic). If the final old-row cleanup fails, the new set is already saved and dupes self-heal next run — never a wipe.**
  - **BACKFILL DROPPED (Justin's ruling 2026-08-01) — not worth further time.** The 30-day retroactive re-grade stalled repeatedly at ~38/101 and was abandoned. **Rationale (accepted):** section tags land automatically on ALL new analyses at **zero marginal cost** (the extractor already emits `section` — proven on real data: on the 37 that did complete, all five values present, close 130 / discovery 41 / pitch 33 / objection 23 / intro 14, 36/37 calls tagged), and **untagged older calls fall back cleanly** to the section-notes prose in the Part-1b UI. The backfill was retroactive polish only. So older calls stay on v9/v8/v5 with no section tags — that's fine, by design. (Snapshot of pre-run per-call outcomes preserved at `~/Desktop/scout-backfill-snapshot-2026-07-31.csv` if a shift report is ever wanted; not pursued.)
  - **Why the backfill kept stalling — TWO causes, both LOGGED (2026-08-01, report-only, not fixed):** (1) **Deploys during the drain** killed the fire-and-forget analysis loop (see the standing "never push during a drain" rule) — a Part-1b API-fix push redeployed Railway mid-drain. (2) **UI mismatch — what shipped ≠ what was specified.** SPEC: a 7d/30d/all-time **scope dropdown with a dry-run count + confirm** as THE Update-analyses control. SHIPPED: that dropdown DOES exist (`updateOutdatedAnalysesNow` → `/fathom/update-analyses` scope, dashboard.html ~L2993) — but it renders **only when `outdated_count > 0`**. When `outdated===0 && pending>0`, the control that renders instead is **"⟳ Analyze next 10"** (`reanalyzeFathomNow` → `/fathom/reanalyze`, **newest 10, `limit:10`, NO scope, NO dry-run**, dashboard.html ~L2987). Because a scope run resets its batch to `status='pending'` (dropping them out of `outdated_count`), the visible control flips to "Analyze next 10" mid-backfill — which only ever re-analyzes the newest 10 pending per click and never sweeps the older pending tail. That's the mismatch that made completing the backfill by clicking impractical. **Not fixed — logged for a future UI pass** (e.g. always expose the scope dropdown whenever `outdated+pending>0`, or make "Analyze next N" honor a scope). **Stuck-row cleanup done 2026-08-01:** 2 rows were stranded at `status='processing'` from the dead drains → reset to `pending` (both `call_analyses` + `fathom_calls`); 0 remain stuck.

- **Call Review Context — Part 1b (UI) BUILT 2026-07-31 (LOCAL, unpushed).** Clicking a section grade card now expands into that section's highlights split into **"What worked"** / **"What to fix"** (dashboard.html `gradeCardHtml`, using `sectionBreakdown` + `highlightGroup` mirrored inline from `lib/highlight-section.js`). Each row REUSES the existing `highlightEntryHtml` (timestamp `?t=` jump — Fathom seeks, Zoom opens without seeking — speaker, quote, observation). The section **notes are kept, demoted** to a one-line summary above the groups. **GRACEFUL FALLBACK (the common case — only backfilled calls have tags):** a section with no tagged highlights shows today's notes prose cleanly (no empty state, no group headers); a section with tags but one empty group renders only the non-empty group (never an empty column). **Part 2's "Add to KB" button is NOT built** — each highlight row is the natural per-item slot for it. TDD: `sectionBreakdown` branch/group logic (`highlight-section.test.js`, 12), the inline-mirror-can't-drift guard (`section-breakdown-mirror.test.js`, 2), and the render assembly per branch (`grade-card-render.test.js`, 4). Suite 198. Live pixel render on a real tagged vs untagged review page is the post-deploy exercise step (needs a Josh-authenticated review page). **SHIPPED + live-verified 2026-07-31 (`9962bfc` + fix `838b192`):** verified on prod (throwaway user + seeded tagged/untagged calls) — tagged section shows both groups with working `?t=` links, untagged falls back to notes prose, collapsed unchanged. The live verify CAUGHT a gap: `GET /fathom/calls/:id` didn't SELECT `section`/`resolution`, so the client never got the tags — fixed + guarded (`review-highlights-select.test.js`). Backfill STALLED at 38/101 (fire-and-forget drain died — a push during the drain redeploys Railway and kills it; complete it with `update-analyses scope=30d` AFTER all pushes are done).

- **Coaching glance tiles — handle-rate + avg-score trend BUILT 2026-08-01 (LOCAL, unpushed; push AFTER the backfill completes — independent of it).** (1) The **"Objection moments" count tile → "Objection handle rate" %** over TRUE objections only (logistical + disqualifications excluded). **Recon ruling: the "true objection" split is the What-needs-work card's LLM bucket classification (`lib/team-needs-work.js`), NOT the stored `objection_category`** — so to make the tile and the card agree, the tile REUSES `state.needsWork` (already loaded on overview boot, cached, `loadNeedsWork()`), and **triggers NO LLM of its own** (boot-fetch trim intact). `objectionHandleRate(needsWork)` sums `detail.buckets` handled/total; **degrades to "—"** when the classification isn't available (loading/error) or there are zero true objections — never falls back to counting all moments. Subtext shows raw counts ("12 of 31 handled"); still drills to `goObjections()`. (2) **Avg-score tile gains a period-over-period trend** (green ▲ / red ▼ + delta %) vs the immediately-preceding equal window — **reuses the team view's prior-window machinery** (`team-analytics._aggregateWindow` over `[from - span, from]`), attached as `avg_score.prior_mean` in `computeCallAnalytics` (so `/me/analytics2` + the `/admin` pivot both get it). No prior data → **no arrow** (not a misleading 0%); prior ≤ 0 → division-guarded. Uses the existing `trendArrow` styling. TDD: `tile-metrics.test.js` (11 — handle-rate incl. div-by-zero/no-data, trend incl. no-prior/div-by-zero) + `tile-metrics-mirror.test.js` (2 — the inline dashboard copies can't drift from `lib/tile-metrics.js`). Suite 212. Live pixel verify is the post-deploy step.

- **KNOWLEDGE BASE Part 2 — five rulings set 2026-08-03; sub-stage 2a (team-scope ownership + promotion) BUILT, migration 029 APPLIED to prod, LOCAL/unpushed.**
  - **RULINGS (standing).** (1) **Harvested material NEVER reaches the grader in v1** — coaching/review surfaces only. If it ever does, it goes in a SEPARATE labeled block framed *"examples of this closer's past execution — evidence of capability, NOT the standard to grade against"*, **never inside SELLING CONTEXT** (whose prompt says *"do not penalize approaches this material explicitly endorses"* — correct for an offer doc, rubric-capture for harvested call moments). **The exclusion is enforced by TWO independent filters in `lib/selling-context.js`, and this is deliberate, not incidental: (a) `fetchSellingContext` filters `category='user_upload'`, which `learned_pattern` rows are not; (b) `GRADER_CATEGORIES = ['script','offer_document','objection_framework','case_study']` excludes `learned_pattern` (it isn't in `SYNTHESIS_CATEGORIES` either). Harvested entries therefore MUST be written with the `category` COLUMN = `'learned_pattern'` — that single choice is what keeps them out of grading. Do not "fix" either filter without re-opening ruling 1.** (2) The existing exclusion of a **managed rep's personal KB from their own grading STAYS**; offer/script governance is the manager's — ruling 1 means no conflict in v1. (3) **Per-section caps ~2/section/call** on the harvest so `close` can't swamp discovery+objection; **do NOT exclude close** — the extractor flags on merit, independent of the synthetic close score. (4) Auto-population gates on **`outcome='closed'` ALONE, NOT `cash_collected > 0`** — the grader records cash by payment structure, so a **payment-plan close with nothing due at signing legitimately shows zero cash**; that filter would drop real wins. Manual outcome tagging makes the closed tag trustworthy on its own. (5) A **MANAGER** clicking the Add-to-KB button on a rep's call **promotes into the TEAM KB — the button IS the promotion path, no separate curation queue**; a rep/owner clicking on their own call writes to their own KB; **auto-extraction always goes to the rep's OWN KB**.
  - **2a BUILT.** **Migration 029** adds `knowledge_base.team_owner_id uuid` + partial index + recreated `match_knowledge` (signature/returns unchanged). **Why:** migration 006 defined a team row as `scope='team' AND uploaded_by = manager`, conflating **provenance** with **visibility** — so promoting a REP's entry was impossible (flipping `scope` left `uploaded_by` on the rep → matched nobody; the only workaround rewrote `uploaded_by`, destroying "this came from Ava's Mar-14 call"). Now `uploaded_by` = provenance (never rewritten), `team_owner_id` = team key. `COALESCE(team_owner_id, uploaded_by)` keeps every pre-029 row's exact old meaning → non-breaking, backfill was a no-op (0 team rows existed).
  - **Four duplicated scope predicates collapsed into ONE** (`lib/kb-scope.js`): the RPC, `lib/kb-visibility.js` (now a re-export shim, old body commented in place), the inline filter in `routes/kb.js /kb/search`, and the team branch of `lib/selling-context.js` (now keys on `TEAM_KEY_COLUMN`). **The SQL copy can't import JS**, so `test/kb-scope-sql-mirror.test.js` guards it two ways: **(a) textual** — migration 029 must contain `KB_VISIBILITY_SQL` verbatim; **(b) semantic** — the SQL clause is transpiled to JS and evaluated against the same fixtures as the predicate, asserting row-for-row agreement (plus a non-vacuous-pass pin). Same pattern as `section-breakdown-mirror` / `tile-metrics-mirror`.
  - **Writers must stamp the key:** `/kb/upload` sets `team_owner_id` for manager uploads (identical to pre-2a there, since manager `p_admin_id` = own id); `/kb/list`'s managed-reader `.or()` gained a `team_owner_id` branch — **without it promoted rows were filtered out of the candidate set before the predicate ever saw them**; three `select()`s gained the column.
  - **`PATCH /kb/:source_label/scope`** `{scope:'team'|'personal'}` — `manage`-gated, `resolvePromotion` exported for unit test. Never writes `uploaded_by`; demote clears the team key (leaving it set would silently republish on a later re-promote); `'global'` is deliberately unreachable (would publish a rep's material platform-wide). Dashboard: **Promote to team / Team ✓** toggle on each KB row, manager+owner only (`kbCanPromote` — an unmanaged user can manage their own KB but has no team to promote INTO).
  - **§2 cost estimate CHALLENGED AND CONFIRMED — team scope had NEVER been exercised against a single live row** (0 team rows; org is 3 owners + 5 users + **no managers**, so every upload landed `global` via owner→global). Proven end-to-end on the real team (manager `8c952cc0` + 4 reps) with a genuinely promoted row (`uploaded_by`=rep, `team_owner_id`=manager): uploader sees it **1**, teammate sees it **1**, other team **0**, teamless caller **0**, legacy-shape row still visible to team **1**, legacy cross-team **0**. **Counterfactual proven: the same row scored 0 under the pre-029 predicate and 1 under the new one.** Test rows deleted (0 remain); KB total unchanged at 770. Suite **239**.
  - **2a SHIPPED + live-verified 2026-08-03 (`18631b3` pushed).** Post-deploy exercised, not just a 200: served `/dashboard` carries `kbCanPromote`/`setKbScope`/`kb-promote-btn`, and `PATCH /kb/:label/scope` returns **401 (mounted + gated)** where it returned 404 pre-deploy. Health 200; analysis counts untouched (no drain was running — 0 rows `processing`, last analysis 2 days prior).
  - **⚠⚠ SUPERSEDED 2026-08-18 — BOTH MANUAL ADD-TO-KB CONTROLS ARE DELETED. RULING 5 NO LONGER DESCRIBES A LIVE PATH. See "MANUAL ADD-TO-KB REMOVED" below before acting on ruling 5 or on the two-surfaces entry that follows it.**
  - **⚠ TWO SEPARATE SURFACES — DO NOT "CONSOLIDATE" THEM (ruling, 2026-08-03; the first of the two was DELETED 2026-08-18).** They are different actions on different objects and both were intentional:
    - **Call-review row button** (`POST /kb/from-highlight`, 2b) — **CREATED** a new KB entry from a call moment. Manager acting on a rep's call → team KB (ruling 5). **REMOVED 2026-08-18.** The route stays mounted; nothing calls it.
    - **KB-list toggle** (`PATCH /kb/:source_label/scope`, 2a) — **RE-SCOPES an entry that already exists**, personal ↔ team. **Still live.**
    The original warning stands for anyone reviving the create path: one is a create-from-source, the other is a scope change on existing rows, and collapsing them would either lose the create path or make re-scoping impossible for uploads that never came from a call.
  - **2b BUILT 2026-08-03 (migration 030 APPLIED to prod, LOCAL/unpushed) — the manual "Add to Knowledge Base" button in the Call Review section-breakdown rows.**
    - **`POST /kb/from-highlight` takes IDS ONLY** (`{fathom_call_id, highlight_id}`) and **re-reads the highlight server-side**; client-supplied quote/observation is never trusted into a store that may one day feed grading. Mismatched call/highlight pairs are rejected (they would skew the dedupe key onto the wrong call). Gate is **`protect`, not `manage`** — ruling 5 lets a MANAGED rep add from their own call, and `requireKbAccess` deliberately 403s managed reps; authority over *other* users' calls is enforced by `getVisibleUploaderIds`.
    - **RULING 1 enforcement is the entry shape:** category COLUMN `'learned_pattern'` (filter (a): `fetchSellingContext` only takes `category='user_upload'`) + `metadata.category='call_moment'` (filter (b): absent from `GRADER_CATEGORIES` **and** `SYNTHESIS_CATEGORIES`). **Both filters are deliberate and redundant on purpose** so that "fixing" one in isolation can't silently open the loop. Pinned by a test in `kb-entry.test.js` that reads the real category lists.
    - **Migration 030** adds `source_fathom_call_id` / `source_section` (CHECK'd to the five sections) / `source_quote_hash` + a **PARTIAL unique index** `(uploaded_by, source_fathom_call_id, source_section, source_quote_hash) WHERE source_quote_hash IS NOT NULL`. **Partial is load-bearing:** all 770 pre-existing rows have a NULL hash and a full index would have collapsed them into one conflicting bucket. `uploaded_by` (the KB owner) is the first key component, so a rep's personal copy and the team copy legitimately coexist while a second add into the SAME KB is refused — **this is what makes the button idempotent with 2d's auto-population by construction** (2d writes with `uploaded_by`=the rep; a rep re-adding the same moment produces an identical key). A `23505` is returned as `{ok:true, duplicate:true}`, not an error.
    - **NOT the highlight id, and this is the whole point:** `persistHighlights` is insert-new-then-delete-old, so **every re-analysis reissues fresh `call_highlights.id`s for the same moments**; an id-keyed index would review as correct and silently readmit duplicates on the next re-grade. **KNOWN LIMIT stated honestly:** the content hash survives id reissue but NOT the model *re-wording* a quote on re-extraction — `normalizeQuote` absorbs case/whitespace/wrapping-quote/trailing-punctuation drift, but a genuinely reworded quote is a new entry. Semantic near-duplicate detection would need embedding-similarity dedupe; out of scope for 2b.
    - **`GET /kb/saved-moments/:fathom_call_id`** returns already-saved highlight IDs so rows render as **In KB ✓**. Hashing stays server-side deliberately — returning hashes for the browser to recompute would have meant a **fifth mirrored copy of `normalizeQuote`**, the exact duplication 2a spent its budget retiring. Degrades to "everything unsaved" on any failure, which is safe because re-adding is idempotent.
    - **`routes/fathom.js` `/calls/:id` highlights select gained `id`** — without it the client has nothing to send and every button is inert. Same failure class as the Part-1b missing-`section` bug; pinned by a new assertion in `review-highlights-select.test.js`.
    - **`lib/kb-entry.js`** holds the pure pieces (`normalizeQuote`, `quoteHash`, `buildEntryContent`, `resolveEntryTarget`) — 2d reuses them verbatim. `quoteHash('')` returns **null**, never a hash of empty string: a blank hash would bucket every quote-less moment together and swallow legitimate adds.
    - **Live-verified** against the real DB: duplicate **blocked**, same moment into a different KB **allowed**, same quote in a different section **allowed**, two NULL-hash rows **allowed**. Test rows deleted; KB total unchanged at 770. Suite **256**.
  - **NO cap on the MANUAL button (ruling, 2026-08-03) — the ~2/section cap is a 2d concern ONLY.** Ruling 3's cap exists because **auto-population is indiscriminate**; a human clicking each individual moment has already made that judgment call, and capping the manual path would block a genuinely exceptional call from being fully captured. Do not "unify" the two paths under one counter.
  - **2c SETTLED 2026-08-03 (decision block — three rulings, one guard built).**
    - **(i) kbHash guard — BUILT.** `test/kb-hash-guard.test.js`. The cache-invalidation cost was previously avoided only as a **side effect** of ruling 1's category choices, and side effects don't survive future edits. Two layers: **structural** (`call_moment` absent from `GRADER_CATEGORIES` **and** `SYNTHESIS_CATEGORIES`; harvested rows' category COLUMN ≠ `user_upload`) and **behavioural** (drives the REAL `fetchSellingContext` against a fake Supabase holding a harvested row beside a genuine offer doc, asserting the harvested row lands in neither `contextText` **nor `kbHash`** — including at 25-row scale). Failure messages name ruling 1 and state the consequence. **Guard proven to actually fire** by deliberately breaking each filter: breaking either ALONE trips its structural test while the behavioural ones stay green (**the two filters are genuinely independent — one break does not open the loop**); breaking BOTH trips all five. **Why kbHash specifically matters: it invalidates `objection_synthesis_cache`, so if harvested rows ever entered the included set, every closed call would force a paid synthesis regeneration.**
    - **(ii) Write path — 2d calls `lib/kb-entry.js` DIRECTLY, no internal HTTP hop.** An HTTP call inside the analysis worker would add auth, network, and failure modes to a pipeline already fragile enough to die on a redeploy. `POST /kb/from-highlight` is now a **thin wrapper** over the same `buildMomentRow` + `insertMoment` — **ONE insert implementation, two callers.** Also extracted `getVoyageEmbedding` → **`lib/voyage.js`** (the worker needs embeddings; a second copy in the worker would have been new duplication — old body commented in place in `routes/kb.js`).
    - **(iii) Surfacing — DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT, and this is NOT an oversight.** Harvested moments currently land in the KB list under "My call moments" with an expand panel — **storage, not coaching**. Surfacing gets built **together with the queued section-drilldown** (clicking a Coach-summary section bar → aggregate breakdown for that section across the period), where *"Discovery is your weakest section — here are your best discovery moments"* is a clean section-level match needing **no new tagging**. **Do not bolt on a separate surface before then** — it would be thrown away when the drilldown lands.
  - **2d BUILT 2026-08-03 (LOCAL/unpushed) — auto-population of closed calls into the rep's OWN KB.** `lib/kb-harvest.js`, wired into `analysis-worker.js` as **Phase 7b**, immediately after `persistHighlights`.
    - **Fire-and-forget, NOT awaited** — the drain already dies on a Railway redeploy (stranded batches twice); adding ~2–10 sequential Voyage round-trips inline would lengthen the window where a deploy kills a call mid-analysis. **Zero new Claude calls** — it files moments the extractor already produced.
    - **Gate = `shouldHarvest(effectiveOutcome)`, i.e. `outcome === 'closed'` ALONE (ruling 4).** Uses the **manual-override-aware** `effectiveOutcome`, so a human-tagged outcome drives it exactly as it drives `close_score`/`payment_structure`. **NOT `cash_collected > 0`:** the grader records cash by payment structure (v8: `payment_plan` = only what was collected ON the call), so **a plan close with nothing due at signing legitimately shows zero** and that filter would systematically drop plan closes. `shouldHarvest` takes only the outcome so cash cannot leak in via a later edit (arity is test-pinned).
    - **Filter + cap (ruling 3):** good-group (`highlightGroup`) + section-tagged + non-blank quote, **capped at `HARVEST_SECTION_CAP = 2` per SECTION per call**. Live data motivating it: on section-tagged closed calls the good-group split is close 62 / pitch 14 / discovery 11 / objection 6 / intro 6 — **uncapped, close would be ~63% of every harvest**, and close is the section whose score is synthetically 100. **Close is CAPPED, never EXCLUDED** — the extractor flags on merit independent of that score. **The manual button stays UNCAPPED** (see the ruling above).
    - **Writes to the REP'S OWN KB** (`scope:'personal'`, `team_owner_id:null`, `uploaded_by` = the rep) — which is precisely what makes it **idempotent with the manual button by construction**: both paths build through `buildMomentRow`, producing an identical dedupe key, so whichever runs second is a no-op. **Order does not matter.** Test-pinned by comparing the two paths' keys directly.
    - **Same entry shape as 2b** (`learned_pattern` / `call_moment`) so **ruling 1 holds automatically with nothing extra to remember**; `metadata.source = 'auto_closed_call'` distinguishes it from `'manual_add'`.
    - **Degrades silently** — `insertMoment` swallows its own errors and the worker `.catch()`es the pass. A KB write can never fail or stall an analysis (same rule `fetchSellingContext` follows). A null embedding still writes the row (keyword-searchable).
    - Suite **277**. TDD'd: the gate, the cap, and idempotency-against-a-manual-add (`test/kb-harvest.test.js`, 16).
    - **IN-SITU VERIFIED 2026-08-03 (15/16) against a REAL closed call** (`06c8d9a5…`, Josh, "AF Sober Living Riches | Amanda Law"). Phase 7b **fired**: 5 rows harvested, per-section `{discovery:2, intro:1, close:2}` — **cap held** (close capped 4→2), every row `learned_pattern` + `call_moment` + `auto_closed_call` + `scope:personal` + `uploaded_by`=the rep, dedupe keys populated, **the analysis itself returned `{status:'done', overall_score:70, highlights_count:6}` undisturbed** by the non-awaited call, score unchanged 70→70, outcome still `closed`, `sync_status` back to `processed`. **Rows KEPT** — genuine material from a real closed call in the correct rep's KB, not test residue. **Why in-situ mattered: Phase 7b is fire-and-forget with a swallowed catch, so a broken 2d is INVISIBLE — no error, no log, no symptom, just an empty KB nobody questions for weeks. A silent-by-design path must be observed firing at least once.**
    - **VOYAGE 429 DEFECT — FIXED 2026-08-03 by BATCHING (local/unpushed).** `lib/voyage.js` gains **`getVoyageEmbeddings(texts)`** — ONE request for a whole call's moments, replacing N sequential singles; `harvestClosedCall` builds every row first, then embeds in a single batch. **Batching, not delays:** a delay would only slow the pass, still rate-limit at scale, and — the decisive point — **would not remove the systematic bias**, since `selectHarvestMoments` preserves chronological order so the dropouts are always the late-call sections. **Contract (test-pinned): output length ALWAYS equals input length; mapping uses the response's `index` field, NEVER array order** (trusting order would silently attach the wrong vector to the wrong moment — a corruption invisible until search results stopped making sense); partial failure degrades per-item to null, total failure to all-nulls, never throws, never returns a short array. **A batch error can never lose the harvest** — every row still writes, just unembedded and keyword-searchable. `summary.unembedded` is now logged per call. Tests: `voyage-batch.test.js` (11: single-request, index-mapping, missing/malformed/out-of-range indices, 429, network throw, malformed body, no-key, degenerate input) + 3 harvest-level wiring tests driving `harvestClosedCall` with a stubbed fetch (one-request, partial-failure, total-429). Suite **291**. **The 2 pre-fix null-embedding rows were BACKFILLED via the new batch path (both `close` moments — confirming the predicted bias); 5/5 harvested rows now embedded, 0 remaining.** **QUEUED (deliberately NOT folded into this stage): `/kb/upload` still embeds chunk-by-chunk sequentially** — a 20-chunk document is 20 single requests, exposed to the identical 429. `getVoyageEmbeddings` drops straight in.
      - **Why it is LOW URGENCY today:** embeddings are **not on the grading path at all**. `fetchSellingContext` is a bulk SQL dump filtered by category + scope under a 5000-char cap — **it never reads `embedding` and never calls `match_knowledge`**. So a chunk that failed to embed still reaches the grader normally. The ONLY thing that degrades is the **`match_knowledge` / `POST /kb/search` similarity path, which today serves the DORMANT desktop teleprompter** — i.e. nothing a live user touches.
      - **⚠ THE TRIGGER THAT MAKES IT LOAD-BEARING — the surfacing work using similarity search.** The moment the queued **section-drilldown** surfacing (see 2c(iii)) retrieves harvested moments or uploaded material by embedding similarity rather than by section/scope filters, an unembedded chunk becomes **permanently invisible to the feature that exists to find it** — silently, with no error. **Fix `/kb/upload` BEFORE that retrieval ships, not after.** This is a stated precondition, not a nice-to-have: the failure mode is invisible-by-design, exactly like the Phase 7b one that required an in-situ run to catch.
    - **⚠ ORIGINAL DEFECT REPORT (kept for context) — VOYAGE 429s under the harvest loop.** `harvestClosedCall` fires N **sequential** Voyage embeds in a tight loop. On the 5-moment run, **3 succeeded then #4 and #5 returned HTTP 429** → those rows stored with `embedding: null`. The degrade path worked as designed (row still written, still keyword-searchable), **but the bias is not random: `selectHarvestMoments` preserves chronological order, so the LATE-CALL sections — `close` above all — are systematically the ones left unembedded**, and those are exactly the moments a "how do I close" semantic search should surface. Live state: 5 harvested rows, **2 without embeddings**. Resolved by the batching fix above.
    - **STILL UNPROVEN INDEPENDENTLY: that the DEPLOYED Railway container runs Phase 7b.** The in-situ run invoked `analyzeCall` directly against prod (same committed code, clean tree) rather than over HTTP, because the only closed section-tagged calls belong to `josh@scoutsystems.io` and `/fathom/reanalyze` is `requireAuth` on `req.user.id` with no admin variant — an HTTP trigger would have meant minting a login session for another person's account. **That was the right call and should stay the norm.** The container-side proof is **the first genuinely new closed call**, which costs nothing to wait for — do not manufacture it. 2d gates on `outcome='closed'` alone (ruling 4) with ~2-per-section caps (ruling 3), writes to the rep's OWN KB, and reuses `lib/kb-entry.js` unchanged.

### ⚠⚠ MANUAL ADD-TO-KB REMOVED — AND WITH IT, THE ONLY PATH FROM A REP'S CALL MOMENT INTO THE TEAM KB (ruling 2026-08-18)
**Justin: *"Even kill the live one if it does so automatically."*** Both manual Add-to-KB controls are gone from the render path (archived in place, per convention):
- **A — the section-drilldown "+ Save"** (`.sec-kb-btn`, `addSectionMomentToKb`). **Had never rendered for anyone**: its `canSave = !state.viewingUserId` guard is always false, because `viewingUserId` is never null after boot.
- **B — the call-review "+ Add to KB"** (`.review-kb-btn`, `addMomentToKb`). Live and working. **⚠ It was NOT on the Call Highlights timeline** — that list has no button and never had one. B lived one level down, inside the **expanded section-grade cards** on the same review page, on both the "What worked" and "What to fix" groups. The framing "the button on Call Highlights" is wrong and cost a round of clarification.

**THE EVIDENCE, measured on production the day it was removed** — this is why the ruling was easy:
```
knowledge_base WHERE metadata->>'category' = 'call_moment'
  auto_closed_call · personal   313    2026-08-03 → 2026-08-17
  manual_add                      0
  scope = 'team'                  0
```
**Two weeks live, not one click.** Auto-harvest (`lib/kb-harvest.js`, Phase 7b) already files the good moments, which is what made the buttons redundant.

**⚠⚠ THE CONSEQUENCE — THERE IS NOW NO PATH FROM A REP'S CALL MOMENT INTO THE TEAM KB. Recorded here, in the code beside the deletion, and asserted in `test/add-to-kb-removed.test.js`, so a future session asking "how does a manager promote a rep's moment?" FINDS the answer rather than rediscovering the gap.**
- auto-harvest always writes `scope:'personal'`, `uploaded_by` = the rep (deliberate — it is what makes it idempotent with the old manual button by construction)
- the visibility rule (`lib/kb-scope.js`) is `scope='personal' AND uploaded_by = p_user_id`, so **a manager cannot SEE a rep's harvested rows in `/kb/list` at all**
- therefore `PATCH /kb/:source_label/scope` can never reach them — **you cannot re-scope a row you cannot see**

Control B was that path (ruling 5: *"the button IS the promotion path, no separate curation queue"*). **Zero rows ever took it, so nothing is lost today — but the CAPABILITY is gone.** Reopening it means either harvesting to team scope for managed reps, or giving managers read visibility of their reps' personal call moments. **Both are RULINGS, not tidy-ups.**

**⚠ `POST /kb/from-highlight` AND `GET /kb/saved-moments` STAY MOUNTED, AND THAT IS THE RULING — NOT AN OVERSIGHT.** `from-highlight` is a **thin wrapper over `buildMomentRow` + `insertMoment`**, the same insert auto-harvest calls, and **its tests are what pin KB ruling 1** (harvested material is `learned_pattern` + `call_moment`, the two independent filters keeping it out of the grader). Justin, 2026-08-18: *"deleting the route to tidy up would remove the enforcement of a ruling, which is a much worse trade than an unused endpoint."* **An unused endpoint whose tests enforce a ruling is not dead code.**

**Also removed as a consequence, not as separate scope:** the `saved_to_kb` marking in `routes/me.js` (a per-request `knowledge_base` query whose answer had no consumer left), its `quoteHash` import, the `saved_to_kb` defaults in `lib/section-breakdown.js`, `state.savedMomentIds`, and both button CSS blocks. **`sectionGroupHtml` lost its `canSave` parameter, which sat BETWEEN `moments` and `renderer`** — a stale 5-arg call site would have passed the renderer into a slot that no longer exists and silently fallen back to `sectionMomentHtml`, losing the 6d "You said" framing with no error. Both call sites were updated together and the arity is exercised at runtime by the render probe.

#### Roadmap update — Team & Account layer (design notes, set 2026-07-21)
These are **design notes, not specs** — a full design session happens per-feature before build. This re-sequences the numbering below: **v1.4.0 is now the Team & Account layer**; CRM integration (previously v1.4.0/v1.4.5) and Slack connect shift to **v1.5.0**.

**v1.4.0 — Team & Account layer:**
- **EOD Report (PULL FORWARD — build FIRST).** Per-call, editable report: prospect name, outcome, Fathom link, **cash collected**, brief summary. *Cash collected is NEW: the grader extracts the payment amount from the transcript ($0 default when none stated)* — needs a `call_analyses` column (e.g. `cash_collected numeric default 0`) + a prompt field + a bump. Output in a **copy-paste format for Slack**; the **one-click Slack send lands in v1.5**. Editable = the closer can correct fields before copying/sending.
- **Manager view** (for a role that has assigned reps — reuses the existing `user_profiles.managed_by`): team + individual stats; trends (weekly / monthly / quarterly); team-level recommendations; **"Call Highlights of the Week"** (best objection handling / challenger moments / pain excavation — clips + short summary, **reuses the synthesis + highlight infrastructure** already built for Performance Summary / Objections); a **daily manager digest** (in-app panel first; email/Slack later).
- **Admin (owner) console:** list all users with **email, role (user / manager / admin), billing status** — all editable and persisted.
- **User account page:** login info, billing, subscription, offer info. **Self-serve ONLY when the user is NOT assigned to a manager;** when managed, it's **locked (view-only) with a "managed by X" notice**.
- **Role naming decision (document + implement mapping):** today's `admin` role **with** `managed_by` assignments = **"manager"**; `owner` = **platform admin**. So the surfaced role set is **user / manager / admin(=owner)**, mapped onto the existing `role` + `managed_by` columns (no new enum unless a design session says otherwise).

**v1.5.0 (existing items stay):** CRM integration (GHL + Close.io — read then act, diff-then-confirm on writes); Slack connect (EOD one-click send + manager digests).

#### Revised roadmap (next milestones, in priority order — set 2026-07-18)
- **v1.3.1 — Dashboard rewire + UX fixes** (NEXT):
  1. **Coaching Dashboard becomes the default landing view for ALL roles** including admin/owner (the admin panel stays one click away, no longer the landing page for admins/owners).
  2. **Rewire the coaching dashboard data source** from the desktop-teleprompter era to the Fathom era. Donuts + Coach summary currently aggregate `call_sessions`; they must aggregate `call_analyses` + `call_highlights`:
     - Calls donut = analyzed calls count (`call_analyses`)
     - Objections donut = `call_highlights WHERE type='objection'`
     - Close rate = from analysis outcomes
     - Coach summary = aggregated from section grades + `one_thing` patterns
     - Drill-downs link to call review pages
  3. **Persistent login sessions (website + desktop):** extend Supabase session TTL + refresh-token persistence so users stay logged in indefinitely (already flagged high priority — see Future Plans).
- **v1.4.0 — CRM Integration Phase A (read):**
  - "Connect CRM" button: GHL and Close.io via OAuth popup (same pattern as Fathom)
  - Read + display: contacts, pipeline stages, deal values alongside call data
  - Match analyzed calls to CRM contacts
- **v1.4.5 — CRM Integration Phase B (act):**
  - Scout as assistant: send SMS/email follow-ups (using the generated follow-up drafts), add notes to contacts, update pipeline stages
  - **All write actions require user confirmation before executing** (diff-then-confirm — see CRM Integration Notes below)
- **v1.5.0 — Notifications & reporting:**
  - "Connect Slack" button — Scout posts EOD reports to a channel
  - EOD report generation (per the existing Phase 5 spec)
  - BOD prioritization + overnight recap (Layer 3 proactive intelligence)
- **Carried forward — slot under the milestones above where they fit:**
  - **Objection intelligence dashboard** — mostly SHIPPED as the Objections view (see "Shipped" above). Remaining: the grounded ISOLATE→REFRAME→OVERCOME synthesis and the re-analysis that populates categories/resolution — both credit-gated.
  - **Zoom / Google Meet as additional recording sources** — the pipeline is already source-agnostic (see Recording Source Architecture); do after CRM.
  - **Teleprompter section-mode** (prompt only during objection/close sections) — see Teleprompter (Future Architecture Note); unscheduled, architecture preserved.

### Fathom API Constraints (learned in production)
Hard-won during the v1.3.0 rollout against Josh's real workspace. The
fathom-typescript SDK (v0.0.41) is the source of truth for the API surface.
- **OAuth apps cannot use `include_transcript=true` / `include_summary=true` on `/meetings`** — the SDK documents them "Unavailable for OAuth connected apps" and they return no transcript (HTTP 400 territory). Fetch the transcript separately via **`GET /recordings/{recording_id}/transcript`** instead.
- **Transcript shape:** `/recordings/{id}/transcript` returns `{ transcript: [...] }`; each turn has `speaker.display_name`, `text`, and an `HH:MM:SS` `timestamp` string (the normalizer converts to numeric seconds).
- **No identity endpoint — `/me` does not exist.** The entire API is 8 endpoints: `/meetings`, `/meeting_types`, `/recordings/{id}/summary`, `/recordings/{id}/transcript`, `/team_members`, `/teams`, `/webhooks`, `/webhooks/{id}`. The OAuth grant uses `scope=public_api` (not OIDC), so the token response carries no identity either.
- **`recorded_by` identity ≠ login email.** Fathom stamps recordings with a workspace-assigned recorder email that can differ entirely from the user's login. Josh logs in as `josh@scoutsystems.io` but his recordings are `recorded_by = joshua@soberlivingriches.com`. The `fathom_connections.fathom_email` column (migration 011) holds the **`recorded_by` value**, confirmed by the user via the identity-options picker — which lists distinct `recorded_by` emails pulled from one unfiltered page of real meetings so the user *selects* theirs. **Never guess the login email** (it cost several wrong-value cycles here).
- **`recorded_by[]=<email>` is a server-side owner filter** (literal brackets, one per email, exact-match on email). WITHOUT it, `/meetings` returns the ENTIRE team workspace's recordings — there is no implicit per-user scoping on an OAuth token. Note: `recorded_by=` WITHOUT brackets is silently ignored by Fathom (returns the default unfiltered page), so the brackets are load-bearing.
- **`created_after` with an epoch/1970 value returns zero** regardless of other filters. OMIT the param entirely when `last_sync_at` is NULL (first sync / post-reset) — never default to `new Date(0)`. `_fetchMeetingsPage` already appends `created_after` only when truthy.
- **Single-use refresh tokens rotate on every refresh** — each refresh returns a NEW refresh_token that MUST be persisted immediately in the same UPDATE, or the connection bricks on the next refresh.
- **Errored calls are recoverable:** reset `fathom_calls.sync_status='pending'` (+ `call_analyses.status='pending'`) and re-run via **`POST /fathom/reanalyze`** (batch-limited: default 10, max 50, most-recent first). It does NOT re-sync from Fathom — it re-analyzes already-stored rows.

### Session Learnings (Fathom analysis rollout, 2026-07)
- **Sequential analysis is ~30–60s per call.** The post-sync analysis loop is fire-and-forget and **does not survive Railway restarts/redeploys** — an interrupted drain leaves rows stuck at `status='processing'` that need a manual reset back to `'pending'`. Keep batches bounded (that's what the reanalyze `limit` is for).
- **A prompt change MUST ship with its version-constant bump in the SAME commit.** If `ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION` lags the prompt content it stamps, every downstream system lies *coherently*: the DB stamp, the outdated-count, and the "Update analyses (N)" button all agree with each other and all agree with the wrong answer, so nothing looks broken. Rule: editing `buildSectionGraderPrompt` (or any dispatched prompt) and bumping `ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION` are one atomic change. When verifying a bump, probe the *content that will run*, not just the constant (read the deployed grader prompt / confirm a re-graded call carries the new fields), and confirm the count query returns the expected N against live data.
- **Supabase `.or()` / `.neq()` null-logic must be verified against live data (2nd silent-null bug in a week).** In Postgres, `.neq('prompt_version','v4')` is NULL (excluded) for NULL rows, so a naive `.neq` count silently drops pre-versioning rows; `.or('prompt_version.neq.v4,prompt_version.is.null')` is the correct-but-fragile form. `outdatedCallIds()` deliberately SIDESTEPS this — it fetches `status='done'` rows and filters in JS (`prompt_version !== currentVersion`, where JS `!==` treats NULL as outdated), provably correct with no `.or()`. If the count ever moves back into a SQL filter, verify it against a dataset containing NULL prompt_versions first.
- **Deploy-race — SECOND occurrence (2026-07-20).** A re-analysis batch dispatched during a redeploy runs against the *outgoing* server, so calls get graded under the OLD prompt version (or the loop dies mid-drain). It's insidious because the rows come back `status='done'` and look fine — only the `prompt_version` stamp reveals they're stale. **Working as designed: the `prompt_version` column made it detectable** (a "v3 done" row after a v4 deploy is unambiguous). **Mitigation for future prompt bumps — deploy FIRST, verify (`/health` 200 + a version probe such as `/fathom/status`.`prompt_version` or `/dashboard.html` carrying the new marker), THEN dispatch batches.** Never `git push` while a reanalyze/update-analyses drain is running. Corollary bug fixed same day: the "Update analyses (N outdated)" count must be defined on `call_analyses.status='done'` + `prompt_version != CURRENT` ALONE — an earlier version joined `fathom_calls.sync_status='processed'`, which under-counted because a deploy-race can leave a done+old analysis on a call whose `sync_status` drifted to `pending`/`error`, letting it escape the count (held calls are excluded for free since they carry `status='synced_unanalyzed'`, not `done`).
- **Diagnostic principle: replay the pipeline stage-by-stage with counts** (`fetched → mapped → upserted`) before assuming where data is lost. In the "sync returns zero" investigation, replaying the exact fetch with Josh's token showed `FETCHED=0` at stage 1 (wrong `recorded_by` email) vs `FETCHED=200 / MAPPED=200` for the correct email — proving there was no downstream "leak" and the only variable was the identity value. Instrument the failing stage; don't guess it.
- **Duplicate highlights root cause (2026-07-22) was CONCURRENT dispatch, not blind insert.** The Jeremiah Thompson Jul 14 call showed 15 highlights (two full sets) — but analyzeCall's delete-before-insert had existed since 2026-06-24, and only 1 of 62 highlight-bearing calls was affected. The tell: the two insert batches' `created_at` were **1 second apart** — two overlapping analyzeCall runs (the three fire-and-forget dispatch loops in routes/fathom.js share no lock), not serial re-analysis stacking (which delete-first already handles; that would also have hit every re-analyzed call). Diagnostic rule: group suspect rows by `created_at` batch before blaming the persist path — batch spacing distinguishes concurrency races (seconds apart) from serial stacking (dates apart).
- **Fix: atomic per-call claim in analyzeCall (`claimAnalysisRun`).** Replaces the blind `'processing'` upsert. One conditional UPDATE claims the row unless another run holds a *fresh* claim (row-level atomicity arbitrates concurrent updates); when no `call_analyses` row exists, an INSERT claims it and the `fathom_call_id` unique index makes the loser fail cleanly. `analyzed_at` doubles as the claim timestamp while processing. Staleness window `CLAIM_STALE_MS` = **10 min**, sized from measured drain data (per-call p50 50s / p95 64s / max 247s) — a live run is never preempted, and a crashed run (redeploy mid-drain) self-heals after the window instead of needing the manual `'processing'`→`'pending'` reset. A run that loses the claim returns `{status:'skipped', reason:'claim_held_by_another_run'}` — logged, no status change, no Claude spend (this also halves worst-case double-spend that a DB unique constraint alone would not have prevented). The `.or()` filter explicitly includes `status.is.null` and `analyzed_at.is.null` per the silent-null lesson above.
- **Backend now has a real test harness:** `npm test` in `backend/` runs `node --test 'test/*.test.js'` (Node's built-in runner, no new dependency). First suite: `test/claim-analysis.test.js` (claim/lose/insert-claim/fail-closed matrix against a faked supabase chain). Note: `node --test test/` (bare directory arg) misbehaves under the iCloud working dir — use the quoted glob form.
- **`knowledge_base` category schema (Item A, 2026-07-22):** the top-level `category` COLUMN is the row-type discriminator (`user_upload` / `learned_pattern` / seeded-framework values) and must never be rewritten to a content category; the *content* category lives in `metadata->>'category'` (allowlist in kb.js `KB_UPLOAD_CATEGORIES`). The "Josh Winning Call Jackie Ustache" upload (20 chunks, global) had `metadata.category='offer_document'`, so `fetchSellingContext` was injecting a raw call transcript into the grader's SELLING CONTEXT as offer material — fixed data-side (`metadata.category`→`'winning_call'`, column untouched); kbHash shifted so synthesis caches invalidate naturally. Platform scan found no other mislabeled winning-call uploads.
- **one_thing double-render on follow_up reviews (Item D, 2026-07-22)** — grader v5 (d87c97c) added follow_up/pending to the verdict section's one_thing pairing ("The move"), but the highlights-footer suppression still keyed on `kind === 'loss'` alone, so follow_up reviews rendered the identical one_thing twice. Fixed by extracting `verdictKindShowsOneThing(kind)` (dashboard.html) as the single predicate both the verdict pairing and the footer suppression key on — they can no longer drift. Win-class unchanged (footer only). Guarded by `backend/test/review-one-thing.test.js`, which extracts the predicate from the HTML and asserts both call sites use it (inline browser script — no module exports — so the test is textual-extraction by design).
- **follow_up_email greeting names are Claude's unguided inference (Item C, 2026-07-22).** The grader prompt never states the prospect's name and never includes the meeting title, so the greeting comes from names spoken in the transcript. Verified: Tasha Presberry's call greeting "Jamie" is CORRECT — the attendee self-identifies as "Jamie Ellis"; the meeting *title* is the misleading artifact (booked-name ≠ attendee). Spot-checks: transcript-grounded names correct (Rochelle, Dana); with no name signal the model emits junk ("Hey First" on the Fathom Demo call). Proposed prompt contract (NOT yet implemented — batch with the next ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION bump): *greet with the name the prospect is actually called in the transcript; if unclear, no name*. Do NOT switch to meeting-title names — that would have greeted the absent "Tasha".

### Analysis schema & outcome/objection pipeline (migrations 012–013)
- **Migration 012:** `call_analyses.outcome` (`closed`/`follow_up`/`lost`/`no_show`), `outcome_source` (`inferred`/`manual`/`crm`), `outcome_set_at` (+ `(user_id, outcome)` index); `call_highlights.objection_category` (`fear`/`logistical`/`timing`/`partner`), `objection_handled` (bool, superseded by `resolution`).
- **Migration 013:** `call_highlights.resolution` (`handled`/`partial`/`unhandled`), `closer_response` (the closer's verbatim handling line — the grounding quote for synthesis; the Fathom clip link is derived at render time from `recording_url` + `timestamp_seconds`).
- **Outcome is folded into the section grader** — it's one field in the grader's JSON output, so the pipeline stays **two** Claude calls (grader + highlight extractor), NOT three. `outcome_source='inferred'`. **TODO when the manual-override UI lands:** the blind `call_analyses` upsert must stop clobbering a `'manual'` outcome (read-before-write or a DB guard) — noted in `analyzeCall`.
- **Coaching synthesis caching (DESIGNED, not built):** the grounded ISOLATE→REFRAME→OVERCOME synthesis is to be cached per **(user, date range, analysis-set hash)** so it regenerates only when the underlying analyzed set changes. Currently a placeholder in the Objections view (credit-gated).
- **Objections-only re-extraction is NOT possible.** The highlight extractor runs inside the full `analyzeCall` pipeline (fetch transcript → grade → extract). Any taxonomy/category change (e.g. adding a category, adjusting the money→fear rule) requires a **full re-analysis** of the call, not a cheap objection-only re-pass. Budget re-analysis accordingly (2 Claude calls/call × N calls; Josh ≈ 200).

### Grading calibration + prompt-version discipline
- **Grader is calibrated, v3 (2026-07-19).** v1/v2 graded against a perfect-call ideal and scored demoralizingly low (Josh: ~51 overall, close 53, on a closer who closes ~31% — strong for high-ticket). v3 adds to `buildSectionGraderPrompt`: (a) an **anchored rubric** (85-100 exceptional / 70-84 strong = a good working closer's typical call / 55-69 adequate / 40-54 weak / <40 failed; reserve sub-50 for genuine failures), (b) **domain context** (25-35% close rate is strong; advancing or appropriately disqualifying a prospect is a success, not a failure), (c) **ranking-integrity** instruction (anchor shifts the scale, not the discrimination — stronger sections must still outscore weaker ones). **Empirical calibration is deferred** until 50+ outcome-tagged calls exist (then anchor the bands to this closer's own win/loss score distributions rather than a fixed rubric). **Shipped result** (Josh's 20 calls, v2→v3): blended overall 51→62; win-class (closed) 65→**71**, middle (follow_up) 46→60, loss-class (lost) 25→**49** — wins land in 65-75, losses in 40-50, ranking preserved (pitch/intro top, discovery bottom, same as v2). The Coaching Dashboard **Avg Score donut carries a "Wins X · Others Y" sub-stat** (`analytics2.avg_score.win_mean`/`other_mean`) so win quality is visible instead of buried in the blend — a low blended average is then legible as composition (many non-closes), not harshness.
- **Delta gate REDEFINED (Justin's ruling, 2026-07-22).** The gate measures **overall score only — median of 3 dry-runs per sample, 3 samples** (closed/follow_up/lost), threshold ~5 points, plus a **hard stop if the outcome flips on any run**. Per-section movement is REPORTED but does not gate — measured noise floor on byte-identical prompts was up to ±14/section while overall stayed within ±3 (2026-07-22 measurement). Tooling: scratchpad delta_check.js implements exactly this.
- **Validation methodology — 3-sample delta check before any batch re-analysis.** When changing the grader prompt, dry-run the new prompt against 3 representative calls (one win-class, one middle/follow_up, one loss-class) using their stored `transcript_stored`, and compare new vs old section+overall scores WITHOUT persisting. Acceptance: overall lift into sane ranges, win-class visibly above loss-class, section ranking preserved. Only then bump the version and re-analyze.
- **prompt_version discipline.** `ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION` (analysis-worker.js) is bumped on EVERY grader/extractor prompt change and stamped on every `call_analyses` row. `SELECT prompt_version, count(*) ... GROUP BY prompt_version` instantly separates current from stale analyses — the guard that makes "did the re-analysis actually run the new prompt?" a one-query answer. Any taxonomy/scale change requires a full re-analysis (the extractor + grader run together inside `analyzeCall`; there is no partial re-grade).



### ⚠ GRADER NOISE PROFILE — a standing constraint on what can be validated (measured 2026-08-10)
**Measured on ONE call, repeated runs of the SAME UNCHANGED prompt:**
- **Per-section noise floor: ±14 points.** (Already documented from the 2026-07-22 delta work; re-confirmed.)
- **Some sections are BIMODAL, not noisy-around-a-mean.** Live example — intro returned `[42,42,42,42,58]` on one prompt and `[58,58,42,58,58]` on another. Same two modes, different winner. **A median over a bimodal distribution reports a 16-point "shift" when all that changed is which mode won 3 of 5 runs.** Reading medians alone will manufacture effects that do not exist.
- **Baseline drift ~6 points BETWEEN ROUNDS on identical input.** The unchanged v11 prompt on the same call gave overall `[51,50,50,50,50]` med **50** in one round and `[57,57,53,56,53]` med **56** in the next. Tight WITHIN a round, unstable ACROSS rounds — so tight distributions are NOT evidence of a real effect.
- **THE RULE: any change whose intended effect is smaller than that CANNOT be validated by score deltas.** It needs a **directly checkable structured field** instead — have the grader answer a question you can verify by reading the transcript, rather than hoping a score moves.
- **How this was learned (3 failed attempts, do not repeat them):** trying to encode "credit any conversational route" as grader wording. Attempt 1 (one-sided "credit by ANY route") → +7 overall, read as general leniency. Attempts 2–3 (two-sided, with qualification enforcement) → targeted section moved **0** while an UNRELATED section moved +16. Three attempts, three different failure modes ⇒ the lever was wrong, not the wording. **A "+7 reproducible, not noise" claim made on tight within-round distributions was later shown to be partly baseline drift** — which is exactly the trap this constraint exists to prevent.

### SELLING CONTEXT — the orphaned onboarding material (wired 2026-08-10)
- **THE FINDING:** `user_profiles` held `offer`, `qualifications` and `script_raw`, written by the **old onboarding wizard** and the desktop script upload. `fetchSellingContext` reads `knowledge_base` ONLY, so **the grader had never seen any of it** — every grade to date was scored against generic standards while the closer's real criteria sat one table away. Josh's stored `qualifications`, verbatim: **"10k saved, not living paycheck to paycheck, 640 or above credit score"**, plus a **14,721-char** `script_raw`. (An earlier recon wrongly concluded "the criteria are not written down anywhere" — they were, in a column the grader doesn't read. The tell that should have caught it: the best closer-side moment in the corpus is *"correct me if I'm wrong, you're not living paycheck to paycheck"*, which maps word-for-word onto a stored criterion.)
- **NO PROMPT BUMP — deliberately.** `analysis-worker.js` is untouched and `ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION` stays **v11**. The SELLING CONTEXT block already existed; only its INPUT changed. Bumping would mark every analysis outdated for no reason under the **new-calls-only ruling**. **History keeps its generic grades; the corpus corrects itself as new calls arrive. Do NOT re-analyse to "fix" old grades.**
- **⚠ BUDGET SPLIT — `lib/selling-budget.js` (the part that had to be right).** The old selection was a single 5,000-char pool filled breadth-first with `break outer` on the first chunk that didn't fit. Measured on Josh's real material: the offer's first chunk took **3,348 of 5,000**, the script's first chunk needed ~3,000 and couldn't fit the remaining 1,652 — so **all 14,721 script characters were dropped silently**. Uploading a script would have appeared to do nothing. Replaced with **lanes + reserves + redistribution**: priority `qualifications(600) → offer(900) → script(1500) → kb uploads(1500)`, phase 1 fills reserves in priority order, phase 2 round-robins the remainder so long lanes absorb slack from short/empty ones. Whole chunks only. Profile material is chunked at **180 words** (not the KB's 500) because a 1,500-char reserve cannot accept a 3,000-char chunk — that granularity mismatch WAS the bug.
- **Result for Josh: 0 → 4,500 chars.** `qualifications 137 (1/1) · offer 544 (1/1) · script 3,809 (4/16 chunks)`. All three criteria visible to the grader.
- **Substance guard:** `usableProfileField` requires ≥15 chars AND more than one token. The demo accounts store `offer = "Ava"/"Ben"/"Cara"` (names left by seeding) — those must never reach the grader as an offer. Verified excluded; `justinschmidtsales`' genuine 26-char offer is kept.
- **`kbHash` now covers profile material too**, so editing the offer/criteria invalidates cached syntheses exactly as a KB upload does.
- **Only Josh has substantive material** (offer 506 / quals 69 / script 14,721). `justinschmidtsales` has a 26-char offer; everyone else has nothing.
- **Expected effect, measured before landing:** across 79 calls the criteria are covered unevenly — credit 72%, savings 78%, **paycheck-to-paycheck only 15%**, all three only **14%**, none **11%**. Discovery already tracks coverage *implicitly*: 0 criteria → avg **36**, 1 → **54**, 2 → **56**, 3 → **60**. With the bar explicit, expect **discovery to move DOWN on average** (34% of calls cover ≤1 criterion) and the spread to widen. That is grades becoming MORE accurate, not worse — the old ones flattered because the bar was unknown.

### PER-CALL COACHING INTELLIGENCE — 7a/7b/7c SHIPPED, 7d BUILT (2026-08-12)
- **The pattern: ask a direct question, store CHECKABLE evidence, wire it to nothing until it has been read.** This is the `qualification_covered` shape extended, adopted after three attempts to express the same intent as grader WORDING died against the ±14 noise floor. **None of these fields touch a score**, and guard tests fail if one ever does.
- **7a (gate)** — proved v14's verbatim contract fixed the evidence leg before anything was built on it: `qualification_covered.evidence` reconstruction **17% → 89%** in an A/B, and **3/3 vs v13's 0/3** on real cron-analysed production rows.
- **7b (`lib/coaching-areas.js`)** — derives each rep's discovery AREAS from their own offer/criteria/script. **Silence is the default path: 1 of 8 live profiles derives areas, 7 are silent and cost no model call.** See the two-bars ruling below.
  - **⚠ AREA KEYS ARE JOIN KEYS AND DERIVATION IS NON-DETERMINISTIC.** The same material produced `income_goal_and_gap` then `income_goal_and_motivation`, `prior_attempts` then `previous_attempts`. Since any material edit re-derives, drifting keys would silently orphan every tagged moment — surfacing as *"no examples yet"*, not as a bug. **Re-derivations are shown the keys already in use** (reuse measured 3/6 → 6/6). Cached on `materialHash` in `objection_synthesis_cache` (`synthesis_type='coaching_areas'`) with a **fixed sentinel** for from_ts/to_ts — those are NOT NULL and sit in the unique index, so a "now" value would mean the cache never hits. An EMPTY derivation is never cached (a transient 429 would otherwise pin the silence in place).
- **7c (migration 036)** — `coverage` (per area: established on this call, by ANY conversational route, with a verbatim line) + `prospect_context` (≤3 attributes the prospect stated about themselves). **Evidence is verified at WRITE TIME by `quote-locate`, never trusted from the model.**
- **7d (migration 037, v16)** — `what_mattered`: the highest-priority UNCOVERED area, naming a structured `area_key` with the prospect's own PROVEN quote as the reason. **Never free text** — free text can't be checked, can't be filtered against the 7e example library, and reads plausibly whether or not it's right. Accepted only when the area exists for this rep, is marked uncovered, AND the reason reconstructs as the PROSPECT's words; any failure stores NULL.
- **⚠ GRADER OUTPUT TOKEN BUDGET IS NOW THE BINDING CONSTRAINT.** `GRADER_MAX_TOKENS` 4500. Measured twice on the six LONGEST calls (1645-2123 turns): worst case **3,211/4,500 (71%)**, **0 unparseable in 24 runs**, ~1,289 spare. **Re-measure on the longest calls before adding ANY further grader field** — an unparseable response fails the ENTIRE analysis, and this cap was already raised once (3000→4500) for exactly that. **Beware the naive marginal-cost number: run-to-run variance on identical input is ~300 tokens, LARGER than 7d's measured +162**, so treat small deltas as noise.
- **⚠ ROLE-INVERTED CALLS (the recorded user is the one being SOLD TO).** Live case: the closer's own disclosures (*"I own a primary residence"*, *"I have cash on hand"*) were counted as covered PROSPECT ground. **The `role_inverted` detector is a WEAK FLAG, NOT THE PROTECTION** — it keys on the model citing closer lines as prospect attributes, and it fired on one run of a call and not the next. A deterministic alternative (closer question share: inverted 39% vs normal 51-63%) is promising but **only 2 corpus calls have role-labelled turns**, too few to set a threshold. **What actually protects the output is the verification chain**: `what_mattered` came out null on the inverted call in both runs, because closer words cannot pass a proven-prospect-quote test. Do not describe the flag as the safety mechanism.
  - **⚠ THIS IS A DATA PROBLEM, NOT A CODE PROBLEM — do not "fix" it by tuning the detector.** Reliable inversion detection needs labelled examples that do not exist yet: only calls analysed under **v13+** carry role-labelled turns, so the corpus of calibratable calls is 2 and grows only as new calls are analysed. **Justin's ruling 2026-08-12: do NOT ship a threshold justified by n=2.** Revisit when enough v13+ calls have accumulated to separate the distributions honestly — until then the verification chain carries it, and that is sufficient.

### ⚠⚠⚠ STANDING INSTRUCTION — STOP REPORTING COST (Justin, 2026-08-29)
**He does not want a running commentary on what things cost. Not in findings, not in reports, not as a caveat appended to a recommendation. When cost is genuinely a decision he needs to make, HE WILL RAISE IT.**
- **⚠⚠ THIS CHANGES THE REPORTING, NOT THE APPROVAL RULE. Spend still needs approval BEFORE it is spent** — the dry-run-then-confirm discipline, the six-loop ceiling, "do not run it, Justin rules" all stand exactly as they are. What stops is **narrating the price of everything**.
- **⚠ THE DISTINCTION IN ONE LINE: ASK BEFORE SPENDING, DON'T ANNOUNCE AFTER.** A request for approval names what it will do. A finished report does not need a price tag on every option, a per-call figure, or a "this would cost ~$X" aside on work nobody asked about.
- **⚠ IT APPLIES TO CLAUDE TOO** — the same instruction covers the assistant's own reports and summaries, not only the documents.
- **WHY IT IS WORTH RECORDING RATHER THAN JUST DOING:** cost commentary reads as diligence, so it accumulates without anyone asking for it — and every unrequested figure is one more thing between the reader and the finding. **The absence of a price is not an omission.**

### ⚠⚠ THE COMMENT-AS-CODE TRAP, IN DEPLOY VERIFICATION — AND IT LOOKED LIKE A PARTIAL DEPLOY (2026-08-29)
**A served-page check reported a state that contradicted itself: `Your Reports` PRESENT (from `efb89b3`) while `billed to the company` and `no inbox in Scout` — both removed by that SAME COMMIT — were also present. Two markers from one commit, only one behaving. That reads as a half-applied deploy.**
```
served /dashboard   raw 800,515 bytes   ->   470,384 after stripping comments
                    330,131 bytes (41%) IS COMMENT
```
