Any of it can be folded into a Call Highlights row later **with no re-analysis and no backfill** — the data will already be there.
- **WHAT SURVIVED, because it improves Call Highlights itself rather than adding to it:** the **handling badge inline with the type chip** (`Engaged with` / `Moved past` / `No response`), the **"You said"** line beneath the quote, the **missing type labels** (`risk_signal` and `barrier` were rendering as raw snake_case), **bigger full-strength type text**, and the **barrier tightening**.
- **Only `risk_signal`, `barrier`, `objection` are badged** (`HANDLING_TYPES`). Badging every type alike is what made the removed panel feel cluttered.
- **THE LESSON:** three separate stages each added a section rather than improving the one the user actually reads. **When a user asks for something to be better, improving the existing surface beats adding a new one — a new panel reads as more complexity even when its content is correct.**

### ⚠ HELD: "A CHALLENGE IS ENGAGEMENT" NEEDS A CONFRONTS-vs-STEERS DISTINCTION (held 2026-08-14, work recorded so it is not lost)
v23 broadened `handling='addressed'` to count a challenge as engagement — the mirror of the v18 warmth fix, prompted by Scout scoring *"It's taking you a year to finally reach out to us?"* as *"acknowledged, then moved past"* when Justin called it a phenomenal response.
- **⚠ IT IS UNVALIDATED AND MAY OVER-APPLY.** The moment Justin praised was **not picked in any of 3 runs**, so his case is untested. A regression check on a borderline case came back **`deflected | deflected | addressed`** — flipping in 1 of 3.
- **THE DISTINCTION THE WORDING IS MISSING — a question mark is not the discriminator:**
  - *"It's taking you a year to finally reach out to us?"* **CONFRONTS** → engagement.
  - *"But that's set aside for business, right?"* **STEERS THE PROSPECT TO AGREE** → not a dig. (Justin's own 9a missed-cue read treats this exact line as a **miss**.)
- **Held at Justin's call for stabilising, not more tuning.** When it resumes, both live examples above are the worked pair — the pattern that has settled every previous boundary here.

### ⚠ 9a MISSED CUE — PAIRED IN THE WORKER, WITH NO MODEL DECLINE STEP (rulings 2026-08-13)
**The feature:** an unaddressed cue early, a concrete obstacle later. *"You had a signal here and didn't qualify on it — this is where the time went."*
- **⚠ THE ARCHITECTURAL LESSON, WHICH GENERALISES.** This shape is DEFINED by the extractor's own output (`risk_signal` + `handling` + timestamps). I first put the field in the GRADER — because the extractor returns a bare JSON array and the grader is the only call with a call-level output slot. **An OUTPUT-SHAPE constraint drove an INFORMATION design decision, and put a judgement where its evidence wasn't.** Result: the grader re-derived it blind and **linked on 0 of 8 calls that demonstrably contain the shape**, while pairing the same 8 calls from stored moments yields **14 pairs across 5 of them**. **RULE: when a field needs data from a model's own output, the WORKER is the home — not the other model.**
- **⚠ NO MODEL "DECLINE" STEP, AND DO NOT RESTORE ONE AS A SAFETY MEASURE.** The claim is two FACTS, both already established: the prospect raised this (a typed `risk_signal` whose quote is proven as their words), and the closer did not dig (`handling` = deflected/ignored, **judged at CAPTURE with the transcript in view**). It never claims the obstacle was *caused* by the miss, and the panel refuses that reading outright (*"not proof the deal was winnable"*). **The judgement was already made where the evidence was; asking a second model to re-make it blind is precisely what produced 0/8.**
- **SETTLED CONSTANTS — do not re-litigate:**
  - **`MIN_CUE_GAP_SECONDS = 120`**, derived not rounded: degenerate pairs (the "obstacle" is the same moment restated) top out at **101s**, the next genuine pair is at **183s**, so any value in that empty band excludes exactly the degenerate cluster and no real pair.
  - **A SECTION constraint (cue in discovery, obstacle at close) was measured and REJECTED** — it halves eligible calls (20 → 10) and discards coachable pitch→close, intro→close and discovery→objection shapes.
  - **ONE pair per call: the EARLIEST cue with its FIRST qualifying obstacle.** Measured 44 pairs across 20 calls but only **24 distinct cues** — the multiplication is one cue against several later obstacles, so showing all is repetition, not coaching. The cue anchors the story; the *first* consequence proves it bit, the last would inflate the gap.
- **Both quotes must be `speaker_verified`** — the panel quotes both as the prospect's words.
- **Shape (b) — the closer DID dig and the prospect genuinely had nothing — is DEFERRED, not dropped.** `handling='addressed'` is far too weak a proxy: sampled, only 1 of 6 resembled a real dig; the rest were reframes and flattery (*"the whole thing is reverse engineering the concept behind risk"*, *"you are at least intelligent because…"*). It needs a purpose-built signal **plus** a ruling on the KB harvest gate, which admits `outcome='closed'` ALONE — and **5 of the 6 calls carrying shape (b) did not close.**

### ⚠ SPEAKER-ANCHORING GUARD — `violatesProspectAnchor` (shipped 2026-08-12)
**`barrier`, `risk_signal` and `objection` describe the PROSPECT'S position and cannot be anchored on a CLOSER-spoken line.** Enforced at WRITE TIME in `lib/analysis-worker.js`, **not** by a prompt instruction — a prompt line here would be the adjective-not-operation trap this project has hit three times.
- **THE LIVE BUG IT EXISTS FOR:** two moments typed `barrier` carried `speaker=CLOSER` and quoted the closer's OWN finances — *"Dude, I just burnt through $100,000… my dad, I'm going to talk to him about being a silent partner"* — while their observations read **"Prospect disclosed no liquid capital."** **Speaker attribution was CORRECT.** The extractor ignored it when interpreting, turning the closer's rapport-building into the prospect's barrier. Justin read it as a one-off caused by Josh relating to prospects; **the mechanism is systematic and would corrupt the coverage map, `what_mattered`, and any 8c/8d tracing**, all of which read these types as statements about the prospect.
- **⚠ APPLIED TWICE, AND BOTH ARE NECESSARY:** once in `sanitizeHighlights` on the model's CLAIMED speaker, and again **after write-time verification on the PROVEN speaker** — because verification can correct `PROSPECT → CLOSER` *after* sanitisation, so checking the claim alone lets a corrected row through. Removing either is a regression.
- **⚠ IT REJECTS RATHER THAN RECLASSIFIES — this is the ruling, not laziness.** **The observation is written from the same mistaken premise as the type.** Retyping the row (say to `rapport_moment`) would keep prose asserting the prospect said something they never said, and the sanitizer cannot rewrite an observation. Inventing a replacement label it cannot justify is the wrong-label-worse-than-none failure. **Dropping one moment out of the 5–8 the extractor emits is the cheap, safe action.**
- **⚠ THE SCOPE IS A DELIBERATE RULING, NOT AN OVERSIGHT.** Exactly three types are guarded. `buying_signal` and `disqualify_signal` are prospect-side too, but **a closer voicing a buying signal (*"so you'd want to start in January?"*) is normal reflection, not corruption** — guarding it would delete legitimate moments. **Widening the list is a DECISION, not a tidy-up.** Pinned by a test so it cannot be widened casually.
- **Proven in a live run:** the guard independently dropped a `barrier` anchored on *"We can do $5,000 with Clarity. Do you guys happen to have…"* — the closer talking.

### ⚠ A SEARCH PREFIX CAN SILENTLY REWRITE THE WRONG PROMPT BLOCK (self-caught 2026-08-12)
**The extractor prompt contains TWO `- closer_response:` instructions** — one in the objection block, one in the risk_signal/barrier block. A scripted edit matching the prefix `'- closer_response: the closer'` matched **both**, replaced the **first** (the objection one), and left the intended target untouched.
- **This class of error does not fail loudly.** The prompt still parses, the suite still passes, and the only symptom is objections quietly acquiring risk-signal wording. It was caught by reading the file after the edit, not by a test.
- **Both instructions are now pinned by a test** asserting there are exactly two `- closer_response:` lines and that the objection one still says *"answering this objection"*. **When scripting an edit against a prompt with repeated field names, match on the FULL distinguishing line or edit by index — and verify which one you changed.**

### ⚠⚠ SAME FAMILY — REMOVING A PARAMETER FROM THE MIDDLE OF A SIGNATURE SILENTLY SHIFTS EVERY LATER ARGUMENT INTO THE WRONG SLOT (2026-08-18)
**A stale call site does not error. It passes arguments that are all individually valid into positions that now mean something else, so the failure surfaces as MISSING BEHAVIOUR rather than a crash — and missing behaviour reads as a design choice, not a bug.**
- **The live case:** `sectionGroupHtml(key, title, moments, canSave, renderer)` lost `canSave` with the Add-to-KB button. `canSave` sat **BETWEEN `moments` and `renderer`**. A surviving 5-argument call would have passed the renderer into a slot that no longer exists, `renderer` would have been `undefined`, and the function's own `var renderMoment = renderer || sectionMomentHtml` fallback would have quietly rendered the "What worked" lane with the WRONG renderer — losing the 6d **"You said"** framing that is the entire point of that lane. **No exception, no failing test, no log line.** The graceful fallback is what makes it silent; without it there would at least have been a TypeError.
- **⚠⚠ THE DEFENSIVE DEFAULT IS WHAT MADE IT SILENT — AND THE TENSION IS WORTH STATING RATHER THAN RESOLVING.** `var renderMoment = renderer || sectionMomentHtml` was written to absorb a MISSING argument, which is a good instinct and should stay. But **the same `||` absorbs a WRONG one**, and only one of those is what it was written for. Without that default, the stale call site would have thrown a TypeError on the first render — loud, immediate, impossible to ship. With it, the lane renders perfectly using the wrong function. **A fallback converts a crash into a silent behaviour change, and it cannot tell the two cases apart because at the call boundary they are identical: `undefined`.** Do not read this as "remove your defaults" — read it as: **wherever a default absorbs an argument, the arity is no longer self-checking, so something else has to check it.** That is why the runtime probe below is not optional here.
- **THE RULE: when a parameter is removed from anywhere but the END of a signature, update every call site IN THE SAME COMMIT and EXERCISE THE ARITY AT RUNTIME.** Grepping the call sites proves they were edited; it does not prove the argument now lands where the function reads it. A render probe that calls the function with the renderer in its new position and asserts the renderer-specific output (`sec-moment-closer`) is what actually proves it.
- **Prefer removing from the END, or leaving a documented dead slot,** when a signature has many consumers. Both cost less than a silent renderer swap.
- **Shares its mechanism with the prefix-edit hazard above and with the shared-carrier family: the edit is correct, the thing that CONSUMES it is what breaks, and nothing announces the change.**

### ⚠⚠ A REMOVAL GUARD MUST BE WRITTEN AND SEEN TO FAIL *BEFORE* THE REMOVAL (2026-08-18)
**A guard written after the deletion can only prove the CURRENT state. It cannot prove it would have caught the thing you just removed — which is the only property that makes it a guard rather than a description.**
- **The practice:** write the absence assertions first, run them against the un-edited file, and confirm they fail naming every marker. The Add-to-KB removal listed nine markers and all nine were reported present before a single edit; after the edits the same nine came back empty. **That sequence is the proof; the green run on its own is not.**
- This is the non-vacuity discipline applied to deletions, and it is where the discipline is most needed: **an absence assertion passes trivially against an empty or mis-read string**, so `assert(!found)` is the easiest test in the codebase to write and have mean nothing.
- **Pair it with a "cut too deep" check.** The same test asserts the HOST surfaces still exist (`sectionMomentHtml`, `highlightEntryHtml`, `review-highlight-row1`), because a removal that took the panel with it would otherwise pass every absence assertion perfectly.
- **⚠ AND STRIP COMMENTS FIRST — this codebase archives removed code in place, so a raw grep reports a shipped removal as un-shipped.** Measured on the served page the day of the removal: **503,933 raw bytes vs 371,984 after stripping** — 132k of archive, containing all nine markers verbatim. A raw check would have been wrong on every one of them.

### ⚠ THERE ARE TWO TOKEN CAPS — CHECK WHICH ONE YOUR FIELD LANDS IN (recorded 2026-08-12)
**Every analysis makes TWO Claude calls with SEPARATE caps. Budgeting a new field against the wrong one means reasoning from a number that does not apply to it.**
- **`GRADER_MAX_TOKENS = 4500`** — the section grader. Carries the scores, notes, outcome, `one_thing`, `follow_up_email`, `why_outcome`, `qualification_covered`, and the 7c/7d coaching fields (`coverage`, `prospect_context`, `what_mattered`). **Measured worst case across the six longest calls: 3,211 / 4,500 (71%), ~1,289 spare, 0 unparseable in 24 runs.**
- **`HIGHLIGHT_MAX_TOK = 3000`** — the highlight extractor. Carries `quote`, `observation`, `section`, `type`, and the objection/risk/barrier sub-fields (`objection_surface`, `resolution`, `closer_response`, `handling`). **Measured worst case: 908 / 3,000 (30%), ~2,092 spare, 0 unparseable in 12 runs.**
- **⚠ THE EXTRACTOR CAP HAD NEVER BEEN MEASURED UNTIL 8a (2026-08-12).** Every truncation note before that — including the 3000→4500 bump — was about the GRADER. If you are adding an extractor field, none of those numbers apply to you.
- **⚠ THE CODE COMMENT ON `HIGHLIGHT_MAX_TOK` IS A ~3× OVERESTIMATE.** It says *"8 highlights × ~300 tokens each"* = 2,400, implying only 600 spare. Actual worst-case usage is **868**. **Do not budget against the comment; measure.** The extractor has far more room than it appears, and the grader far less.
- **The rule stays: measure on the SIX LONGEST CALLS before adding any field, never on the average.** A truncated response fails the whole analysis for that call — the grader loses everything, the extractor loses the entire highlight set. And beware small deltas: run-to-run variance on identical input is ~300 tokens on the grader, larger than most single-field costs.

### ⚠ 8c/8d (BARRIER → UNCOVERED-GROUND CAUSAL LINK) IS HELD ON DATA, NOT UNFINISHED (ruling 2026-08-12)
**The feature:** *"the lender only approved $5,000 — and financial qualification was never established in discovery, which is where that would have surfaced."* Closest thing yet to Justin's vision. **Designed, deliberately NOT built.**
- **WHY IT IS HELD — measured, not suspected.** Uncovered areas per call, on every call that currently has a coverage map: **0, 1, 1.** With exactly ONE uncovered area **the link is forced, not judged** — every barrier on that call gets attributed to the single available gap regardless of relevance. A spouse-approval barrier would trace to `financial_qualification` purely because nothing else was there, and it would read as a confident causal discovery. **A forced link that reads as a discovery is precisely the failure this project keeps designing against.**
- **UNBLOCK CONDITION:** coverage maps get richer as v15+ calls accumulate and reps' area lists exercise more ground. Revisit when a meaningful share of calls carry **2+ uncovered areas**, so the model is actually choosing between candidates rather than naming the only one. **This is a DATA problem with a due date, the same shape as the role-inversion detector — do NOT read it as unfinished work and do not "fix" it by building the link anyway.**
- **WHEN IT IS BUILT, THE MACHINERY ALREADY EXISTS.** Item 2 is structurally **7d with a different anchor quote**: `resolveWhatMattered` already enforces area-exists → area-uncovered → cited-quote-reconstructs. Swap the prospect's self-description for the barrier's quote (already `speaker_verified`) and the validation chain is unchanged. No new validator.
- **Also required at that point:** the model must be able to DECLINE, the prompt must say that an available gap is not the same as a causal one, and the wording must read as an observation the closer can judge — **never as a finding**. Causation is not provable here; only the scaffolding is.
- **Asymmetry worth remembering:** this needs derived areas, so it is silent for **7 of 8 live profiles**. Item 1 (risk-signal handling) needs none and works for every rep — which is why 8a/8b went first.

### ⚠ OBJECTION HANDLE RATE WILL DRIFT FOR WEEKS — WHAT IS HEALTHY vs WHAT IS BROKEN (recorded 2026-08-12)
**Under new-calls-only, any 30/90-day window blends v16-and-earlier (broad `objection`) with v17+ (narrow) rows. The number WILL fall. That is the metric becoming truer, not a regression. A future session reading a lower number must not chase it as a bug.**
- **BASELINE captured the day v17 shipped:** 30-day window = **303 objection-typed moments, 85 handled, 28% raw handled rate**. (The dashboard tile is computed from `team-needs-work` buckets — true-objection buckets only — so it is a related but not identical number; the raw figure above is the cheap proxy, computable with no Claude call.)
- **HEALTHY drift — expect all of these:**
  - Rate **falls toward the 21–32% post-price band**. Measured pre-v17 by section: discovery **45%**, intro 33%, close 32%, pitch 25%, objection **21%**. Early concerns are handled far more often because they are easier; removing them is the point.
  - **Denominator roughly HALVES.** 153 of 322 pre-v17 objection moments (48%) were post-price by section proxy.
  - The **What-needs-work** card hits its *insufficient* state **occasionally** — `MIN_BUCKET = 6`, and fewer objections per surface bucket means marginal buckets legitimately drop below it. That is the guardrail working.
- **BROKEN — these are real failures, investigate:**
  - The **tile blanks** (`objectionHandleRate` returns null → "—"). It only does that when total is 0. With ~150 objections expected per 30 days post-narrowing, a blank tile means objections stopped being captured at all — check that the extractor is still emitting `type='objection'` and that migration 038's CHECK did not reject writes.
  - The What-needs-work card sits at *insufficient* **persistently rather than occasionally** — that means the narrowing over-fired and almost nothing is being typed `objection`.
  - The rate falls **below ~20%** and keeps going. The post-price floor measured pre-v17 was 21%; materially below that suggests true objections are being mis-typed as `barrier`, i.e. the decision test has inverted somewhere.

### ⚠ `coverage` AND `qualification_covered` HANDLE A MISSING QUOTE DIFFERENTLY — ON PURPOSE (ruling 2026-08-12)
- **`sanitizeQualificationCovered` FLIPS `true` → `false`** when the model claims coverage without a supporting quote. Correct **there**, because that field **drives nothing** — it is measurement only, so under-reporting is free and over-reporting would corrupt the measurement.
- **`sanitizeCoverage` does NOT flip.** It keeps `covered: true` and withholds the unprovable quote (`evidence: null`, `evidence_verified: false`).
- **WHY THE DIFFERENCE IS LOAD-BEARING:** 7d ranks **UNCOVERED** areas into *"the question that mattered"*. Flipping an unsupported claim to `false` would **manufacture a gap and coach the rep on a fiction** — telling them they missed ground they actually covered. Claiming coverage without proof and asserting a miss are **both** assertions; make neither. Keep the judgement, withhold the quote.
- **Justin's ruling, 2026-08-12:** *"flipping an unsupported claim to false would invent a gap and coach the rep on a fiction."*
- **A future session will notice the two sanitizers disagree and try to harmonise them. Do not.** The rule follows the CONSEQUENCE, not the shape: a field that drives nothing may fail closed; a field that drives coaching must not invent the thing it coaches on.

### ⚠ TWO DIFFERENT BARS FOR PROFILE MATERIAL — DO NOT "FIX" THEM INTO ONE (ruling 2026-08-11)
- **`usableProfileField` (≥15 chars AND >1 token) is the SELLING-CONTEXT bar** — it decides what background material the grader may read. Thin is fine there: a short offer is still true, just brief, and it can only help.
- **`lib/coaching-areas.js usableMaterial` is the RUBRIC bar, and it is DELIBERATELY HIGHER.** A coverage rubric ASSERTS what a buyer must demonstrate. Asserting that from 26 characters is fabrication, not brevity. It requires knowing what the offer demands OF A BUYER — so either `qualifications` or `script_raw` must be present, or an offer of ≥`MIN_OFFER_ONLY_CHARS` (200).
- **The live case that forced the split:** one user's ENTIRE offer is `"6 month real estate course"` (26 chars). It clears `usableProfileField`, and from those five words the model confidently produced five areas — *"time availability"*, *"current experience level"* — which are **general knowledge about real estate courses wearing the costume of a personalised rubric**. Josh's real offer is 506 chars plus criteria plus a 14,721-char script.
- **Justin's ruling, upheld 2026-08-11:** *"Background material can be thin and still help the grader; a RUBRIC asserts what a buyer must demonstrate, and asserting that from 26 characters is fabrication."* Live shape: **1 user derives areas, 7 are silent** — that is CORRECT, not a coverage bug. **A future session will notice the two bars differ and try to unify them. Do not.**

### SPEAKER IDENTITY — 6a/6b/6c/6d SHIPPED, 6e BUILT (2026-08-11). Supersedes the "blocked" note below.
- **6a — deterministic labelling (`66becf8`, live, `ANALYSIS_PROMPT_VERSION` v12→v13).** The closer is identified by **EXACT EQUALITY** between the per-turn `speaker.matched_calendar_invitee_email` and `fathom_connections.fathom_email`. Settled by live fetch: **only the recorded_by user's turns carry the email** (closer 47/47, 551/551, 653/653; prospects 0/180, 0/257) — a perfect discriminator. **NO name fallback, NO talk-time heuristic, NO fuzzy matching** — each returned the CLOSER as the prospect on real calls; the legacy `recorded_by`-name path is kept but deliberately unwired.
  - **RULING 1 — the email is resolved to a role at normalize time and DISCARDED.** `transcript_stored` turns are exactly `text · speaker · display_name · start_seconds`. Writing a personal email onto every turn would spread PII for no gain. **Cost, accepted knowingly:** a future repair can never re-derive attribution from stored data — it would have to re-fetch from Fathom while the recording and connection still exist. One-way door.
  - **Why it needed a version bump even though the TEMPLATE is unedited:** the prompt STRING changes materially (the closer is named; every transcript line is prefixed `CLOSER:`/`PROSPECT:` instead of a raw display name). `prompt_version` is the only way to tell which analyses were graded with MATCHED speakers vs GUESSED ones. It marks all prior analyses outdated — **cosmetic under new-calls-only; do NOT mass re-analyze.**
  - Verified on the hardest live call (**two people named Joshua**): Josh (prospect) 1214 turns → PROSPECT, Joshua Pinner 549 → CLOSER. `speaker_closer_name` populated for the first time (was NULL on all 83 rows).
- **⚠ `speaker_confidence` IS NOT A COLUMN** — the worker only RETURNS it. The persisted equivalent is `speaker_closer_name`, non-null **iff** confidence is `matched` (both set in the same normalizer branch). That invariant is pinned by `speaker-identity.test.js`; **do not decouple them** — `routes/kb.js` depends on it.
- **6b/6c — backfills, three-valued verdicts.** `call_highlights.speaker_verified` (migration **034**) and `closer_response_verified` (migration **035**) are **NULL / false / true** with **no default, deliberately**: NULL = never assessed (no closer identity — e.g. the 235 demo rows), false = assessed and NOT provable, true = proven. A default would collapse the first two. **Closer-side features MUST require `= true`.** Live state: highlights **440 proven / 524 unprovable / 235 never assessed**; harvested KB moments 48 proven / 63 refused.
  - **Corrected corpus numbers** (the originals were artifacts of the unsound locator and were reported as fact): highlight speaker error rate **8% → 3%** (13 of 439 provable; intro worst at **19%**), KB misattribution **18% → 6% of provable**.
- **6d — the rep-only "What worked" lane (`59c19ee`, live).** The section drilldown leads with the CLOSER's line and shows the prospect's preceding line as context. Only **proven** moments appear; unprovable and never-assessed are excluded and **counted separately on screen** (they are different messages). `closer_moments` is a SEPARATE field, never a filter on `good` — the call-review breakdown still shows everything. **RULING: prospect-side buying signals stay on the call-review page only — do NOT re-mix them into this lane.**
- **6e — objection responses (BUILT, local).** An objection is the PROSPECT's row with the rep's reply in `closer_response`, invisible to a CLOSER filter (257 responses vs 1 usable moment). **The field NAME is not evidence: of 53 responses that reconstruct, 3 were actually the prospect speaking.** Only `closer_response_verified = true` + `resolution='handled'` reaches the lane; the objection quote on the same row becomes EXACT context.
  - **⚠ THE YIELD CAME IN AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE UNDER THE ESTIMATE — 1 → 3 moments, not the 30–80 predicted.** Three compounding reasons, all missed in the estimate: (1) only **24%** reconstruct (50 of 212 checkable), not the "third to a half" guessed; (2) only **12 of 50** are `handled` — `partial` dominates (38) and belongs in "what to fix"; (3) those 12 **scatter across sections** (NULL 5, close 3, discovery 2, **objection 2**) because an objection-TYPE highlight is tagged with the section where it OCCURRED. **Objection coaching is not viable on current data.**
  - **THE REAL BOTTLENECK, measured: the extractor paraphrases.** Of 88 sampled unreconstructible responses, **76 start verbatim then diverge mid-sentence**, 5 are wholly rewritten, and 7 ARE fully present (a small locator gap on responses split by an interjection). **Fixing this needs the extractor to emit VERBATIM `closer_response`, which is a prompt change + version bump and only helps NEW calls.** That single change is worth more than any further backfill.
- **v14 — VERBATIM QUOTING (built 2026-08-11). The single highest-yield change in this stage.** Every quoted field the models emit must be a **CONTIGUOUS character-for-character span of ONE transcript line**: extractor `quote` + `closer_response`, grader `why_outcome.quote` + `qualification_covered.evidence`. Scoped deliberately to ALL of them — fixing only `closer_response` would have repaired one section and left the identical defect capping stored highlights, the review page's decisive-moment quote, and harvested KB moments.
  - **WHAT ACTUALLY CAUSED THE DEFECT (both removed):** the prompt said **"trim filler"**, which licenses editing the words, and capped quotes at **5-30 words**, which forces compression of longer lines. Shortening is now **cut-from-the-ends only** — never stitching a beginning to a later ending.
  - **⚠ THE ADJECTIVE DOES NOT WORK — the rule must be MECHANICAL.** `closer_response` ALREADY said *"quoted verbatim from the transcript"* under v13 and still failed reconstruction **67% of the time**. Measured on 88 failures: **86% began verbatim and drifted mid-sentence**, 6% were wholly rewritten. Telling a model to be verbatim is not the same as telling it to COPY A SPAN. Do not "simplify" the HOW TO QUOTE block back into an adjective.
  - **MEASURED A/B on 8 real calls, both arms on the SAME call** (validated by RECONSTRUCTION RATE, never score deltas — the noise floor makes scores meaningless and reconstruction is directly checkable): **`quote` 67% → 93%**, **`closer_response` 33% → 91%**, **usable moments 35 → 51 (+46%)**.
  - **THE RISK THAT WAS MEASURED AND DID NOT MATERIALISE:** forcing verbatim could have made the extractor emit FEWER moments (no longer able to smooth a messy exchange by paraphrasing). It did the opposite — **52 → 55 moments, 6.5 → 6.9 per call**, and it emitted MORE `closer_response` values (6 → 11) because under v13 it often gave up and returned null. **If a future prompt change touches quoting, re-run this A/B rather than assuming the trade still holds.**
  - **Corpus baselines were re-measured with the interjection-tolerant locator FIRST**, so the prompt's gain is not confounded with the locator's: `quote` 45%→**48%**, `closer_response` 24%→**33%** from the locator alone.
- **`lib/quote-locate.js` tolerates interjections (2026-08-11).** Two accumulators: all turns in order, AND the anchor speaker's turns only. One person's continuous point interrupted by a short *"Right."* used to fail, because concatenating verbatim inserts the interjection INTO the candidate string — 7 of 88 unreconstructible responses failed for exactly that. Not a loosening: every word must still come from one speaker's own consecutive contributions, and a quote that only reconstructs by borrowing the other party's words is still reported `mixed`.
- **⚠ SUPERSEDED — the note below is kept for history only.**

### ⚠ CLOSER-SIDE FEATURES ARE BLOCKED ON SPEAKER LABELLING (recorded 2026-08-10 — RESOLVED by 6a above)
- **Root cause:** `analysis-worker.js` hardcodes `recorded_by: null` when building the meeting object, so the normalizer's fuzzy match never runs, `speaker_confidence` is `'unknown'` on **every** call, and `speaker_closer_name` is NULL on all 56 rows. **Speakers are INFERRED by the model from conversational cues, not matched.**
- **Consequence, measured:** of six real CLOSER-tagged discovery moments, **three are actually the prospect** (*"I'd definitely be interested to learn more about the role"*, *"I'm kind of sold on the actual work environment"*, *"I mentioned that in my Zoom video"*). A closer-only filter would present those to the closer as **"your good questions."**
- **Therefore: ANY closer-side feature waits on this.** The cheap display-only version **inherits the defect rather than avoiding it** — "start thin" is not a way around it.
- **The fix is the one that solved prospect names:** `fathom_connections.fathom_email` gives a reliable closer identity ("joshua@…" ↔ "Joshua Pinner"), and the raw transcript already carries `matched_calendar_invitee_email` per turn. Feed those into the normalizer so speaker assignment is deterministic. Prerequisite stage, not part of the feature.

### Recording Source Architecture
**ZOOM integration (2026-07-23, Zoom-only — Meet is OUT).** The pipeline is source-agnostic from `normalizeTranscript` onward (the `meeting` object at analysis-worker.js:710); a new source only reimplements OAuth + token store + sync + a transcript adapter that emits `[{speaker:{display_name}, text, timestamp:"HH:MM:SS"}]`.
- **Zoom app (Justin-created):** User-managed OAuth (General app, NOT Server-to-Server). Scopes `cloud_recording:read:recording` / `:list_recording_files` / `:meeting_transcript` + `user:read:user`. **OAuth Redirect URL = `https://www.scoutsystems.io/auth/zoom/callback`** (the www custom domain, NOT the Railway host — corrected 2026-07-23). Webhook (sub-stage 4): `recording.completed` + `recording.transcript_completed` → `https://www.scoutsystems.io/zoom/webhook`. **Env (Railway, set):** `ZOOM_CLIENT_ID`, `ZOOM_CLIENT_SECRET`, `ZOOM_REDIRECT_URI`, `ZOOM_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TOKEN` (sub-stage 4). State signing reuses `FATHOM_STATE_SECRET`. **PRODUCTION CREDENTIALS SWAP (beta-share, 2026-07-24 — submission is IN, ~72h review):** the beta auth flow runs on the app's PRODUCTION Client ID/Secret (Marketplace -> Production tab -> Basic Information), which are DIFFERENT values from the dev credentials Railway currently holds. The swap is a PURE ENV-VAR CHANGE — code verified to read ZOOM_CLIENT_ID/ZOOM_CLIENT_SECRET only via process.env (no hardcoded client id anywhere; token endpoint zoom.us/oauth/token is identical dev/prod). WHEN JUSTIN PASTES: after beta approval lands and BEFORE sharing the auth URL / before any tester connects. Sequence: (1) paste Production ZOOM_CLIENT_ID + ZOOM_CLIENT_SECRET into Railway Variables (triggers redeploy); (2) reconnect Zoom once from #account -> Connections (existing dev-minted tokens die when the client changes, so the connection must be re-established under Production creds); (3) verify boot + a fresh /users/me call succeeds; (4) dev credentials retired; (5) THEN distribute the beta auth URL to testers (they authorize under Production creds). ZOOM_REDIRECT_URI is unchanged. **External-user launch is gated on Zoom Marketplace beta-share (~100-install cap) then public review — build/test on Justin's own account first.** Cloud recording requires a PAID Zoom plan (Pro+); free tier has nothing to sync (sub-stage 4 detects+messages this). No native timestamp deep-link → clips are the parity mechanism.
- **Sub-stage 1 (BUILT 2026-07-23, local commits, awaiting approval):** migration 024 (`fathom_calls.source` fathom|zoom; unified `call_connections` table, composite PK (user_id,provider), RLS service-role-only) + `lib/zoom-client.js` (OAuth exchange/refresh — Basic-auth token endpoint, refresh tokens rotate like Fathom) + `lib/call-connections.js` (shared token store: `getValidAccessToken(provider)` refreshes + persists the rotated refresh_token) + `GET /auth/zoom/connect` + `GET /auth/zoom/callback` (mirror Fathom, write call_connections). **DEVIATION (justified): the approved sub-stage-1 scope included repointing Fathom's 8 `fathom_connections` sites into `call_connections`; the code argued against bundling that with the live Fathom token-refresh path, so the Fathom cutover is DEFERRED to its own contained step. Zoom is fully on `call_connections`; Fathom untouched; `call_connections` holds only zoom rows until the cutover.** **SCHEDULED DEBT (due date, not a resting state — Justin's ruling 2026-07-23): the Fathom cutover is its own contained step (own stop point) triggered AFTER sub-stage 2 proves the unified store against real Zoom traffic. It repoints Fathom's 8 `fathom_connections` sites (auth.js callback upsert, fathom.js ×8-incl. `_getValidAccessToken` refresh + `/sync-all` list + identity-picker `fathom_email`→`external_account_email`, analysis-worker.js Phase-3 token read) to `call_connections` filtered by provider='fathom', backfills existing Fathom rows, then drops `fathom_connections` after a rollback-safety release. Two parallel connection stores is DEBT WITH A DUE DATE — do not let it become permanent drift.** Tests: zoom-oauth.test.js (config gate, authorize-URL, Basic header, token-response validation, refresh-decision). **Sub-stage 2 (OPENED 2026-07-24: sync + VTT adapter → grade end-to-end).** MANDATORY FIRST TASK — token blocker: every authenticated Zoom API call in recon 401'd ('Invalid access token' 124) and a direct refresh 400'd ('Invalid Token'); we have NEVER confirmed a working Zoom API call from our flow (only ever tested REFRESHED tokens, all rejected; the connect token was never tested pre-refresh). Diagnostic discipline (ruling): after a FRESH reconnect, test the brand-new connect token against /users/me BEFORE any refresh (splits 'our tokens never worked' from 'rapid test refreshes broke the single-use chain'); then ONE controlled refresh + retest; root-cause before building sync. DESIGN RULING (adopted): Zoom token refresh is SERIALIZED per connection — single-flight, atomic-claim discipline, NO concurrent refreshes ever (Zoom's single-use rotating refresh tokens make this a correctness requirement, not an optimization); build it into lib/call-connections as part of sub-stage 2. NO scratchpad/test code touches live tokens outside that path from now on. Then per the approved design: zoom-client.listRecordings + lib/vtt-adapter (Zoom VTT: per-cue HH:MM:SS + inline 'Name:' speaker labels → the meeting.transcript shape) + POST /zoom/sync (mirror /fathom/sync, map → fathom_calls source='zoom') + the analyzeCall Phase-3/4 source-switch (call-connections.getValidAccessToken('zoom') + Zoom transcript → same meeting object at analysis-worker.js:711). First recon after the token fix: confirm Justin's Zoom account has a cloud recording + transcript to grade (couldn't verify in recon — API rejected auth). **Sub-stage 2 build progress (2026-07-24, local, unpushed):** (a) serialized single-flight refresh landed in lib/call-connections (getValidAccessToken joins one in-flight refresh per user+provider; double-checked re-read; TDD in call-connections.test.js) — the ruling fix for Zoom's single-use rotation. (b) lib/vtt-adapter.js: Zoom WebVTT → the meeting.transcript shape [{speaker:{display_name},text,timestamp:'HH:MM:SS'}] (supports 'Name: text' + <v Name> voice tags, ms→HH:MM:SS, merges consecutive same-speaker cues, skips NOTE/index lines; TDD in vtt-adapter.test.js against synthetic VTT — VALIDATE against a real Zoom recording once Josh connects post-approval). REMAINING for sub-stage 2: zoom-client.listRecordings + POST /zoom/sync (map → fathom_calls source='zoom') + the analyzeCall Phase-3/4 source-switch (call-connections.getValidAccessToken('zoom') + Zoom transcript via the adapter → same meeting object). **TOKEN BLOCKER RESOLVED (2026-07-24): our tokens + refresh WORK** — controlled diagnostic after a fresh reconnect: connect token → /users/me HTTP 200 (before any refresh), one controlled refresh → /users/me HTTP 200. The earlier 401s were the single-use refresh chain broken by rapid repeated test refreshes (confirms the serialized single-flight refresh ruling as a correctness requirement). **NEW BLOCKER — MISSING SCOPE: `cloud_recording:read:list_user_recordings`.** With a valid token, GET /users/me/recordings returns Zoom error 4711 naming exactly this missing scope. The app HAS list_recording_files/recording/meeting_transcript (fetch a KNOWN meeting's files) but NOT list_user_recordings (LIST the user's recordings — the polling-sync entry point). **Justin action before sub-stage-2 sync: add `cloud_recording:read:list_user_recordings` to the Zoom app scopes, then reconnect (re-consent to pick up the new scope).** (Webhook ingestion in sub-stage 4 delivers meeting ids directly and wouldn't need this, but sub-stage 2's polling sync does.) Gradeable-data check (recording + transcript present) is blocked until the scope lands. Feature env-dark until ZOOM_CLIENT_ID/SECRET present. **Connect-UI PULLED FORWARD from sub-stage 4 (product ruling 2026-07-23):** `GET /zoom/status` (routes/zoom.js, mounted /zoom) + a dashboard "Connect Zoom" section identical in shape to Fathom (same card/strip classes, connect+connected states, button → /auth/zoom/connect → popup → lands on ?zoom=connected → status reflects the call_connections row). Deeper polish (paid-plan detection messaging) stays in sub-stage 4. UI RESHAPE (ruling 2026-07-23): (1) the ONLY connection-management surface is a new #account 'Connections' section (all users, exempt from the managed lock like Password) — both providers with state (connected / email / since / not connected) + Connect/Reconnect/Disconnect. (2) Disconnect = DELETE /zoom/disconnect (call_connections) and DELETE /fathom/disconnect (fathom_connections): deletes ONLY the connection row, NEVER calls/analyses/highlights (history stays; new-call sync stops); confirm-before in the UI; sync degrades gracefully (Fathom /sync already 404s clean, cron skips absent connections). (3) the dashboard is FIRST-RUN-ONLY: connect cards render only when NO source is connected; once any source connects the dashboard shows only connected status strips and the other provider's connect button disappears (it lives in Connections). Pairing row from Justin's test was CLEARED (0 zoom rows; 250 calls/analyses intact) — he reconnects from his own login via the Connections section. ZOOM OAUTH TESTING GOTCHAS (2026-07-23, NOT bugs — don't chase): (1) AUTO-APPROVE — if the tester's browser has an active Zoom session AND the app was previously authorized, Zoom auto-approves instantly (popup closes immediately) and pairs the BROWSER's Zoom account regardless of which Scout login initiated. Standard OAuth. For multi-account testing use incognito or sign out of Zoom first. The already_linked guard (Zoom callback rejects a Zoom account already bound to a different Scout user -> ?zoom=already_linked) backstops the wrong-pairing case. (2) EXTERNAL ACCOUNTS BLOCKED — the Zoom app is unpublished (dev), so any Zoom account other than the developer's (Justin's) canNOT authorize it and Zoom shows its generic 'Something went wrong' page. Expected until Marketplace beta-share approval; build/test on Justin's own Zoom only. Correct current pairing after cleanup: Scout justin.schmidt23@yahoo.com <-> Zoom justinschmidtsales@gmail.com. **Deauthorization (2026-07-24, pre-submit, LOCAL/unpushed):** migration 025 adds call_connections.external_account_id (Zoom user id, captured at connect from /users/me); `POST /zoom/deauthorization` (unauth) verifies the app Secret/Verification Token (`ZOOM_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TOKEN`, Authorization header, timing-safe), answers the endpoint.url_validation CRC challenge, deletes the matching Zoom connection row(s) by external_account_id (tokens die; calls/analyses/history stay — same as in-app Disconnect), 200-fast, never 500. Responses arrive by email to justin@ + notes on the Publish page. **Post-approval choreography (STRICT ORDER):** (1) Justin manually activates (his click — activation is set to manual); (2) confirm the unlisted install link surfaced in-console; (3) Josh installs via link + records a cloud call with transcription; (4) validate the VTT adapter against his REAL recording; (5) finish sub-stage 2 — `zoom-client.listRecordings` + `POST /zoom/sync` + the `analyzeCall` source-switch; (6) THEN the scheduled Fathom→call_connections cutover comes due; (7) reviewer demo-data cleanup (documented SQL, `demo-rv-*`) runs ONLY after review FULLY completes — never before. **Standing state: deauth endpoint live; secret-token rotation declined (accepted risk, logged); Zoom stage WAITING on Zoom — do no Zoom work until approval/notes arrive.**

Fathom is the only recording source for now. Zoom and Google Meet will be added later.

**CRITICAL:** Before Phase 2 (the analysis pipeline) is built, a recording-source abstraction layer must be designed so the analysis pipeline is source-agnostic. The pipeline receives a **transcript object** regardless of where it came from. The transcript object shape MUST be defined before Phase 2 implementation begins. Locking the shape early is the only way to avoid a Zoom/Meet integration becoming an analysis-pipeline rewrite later.

Shape questions to answer at design time: speaker labels and identity resolution, turn-level vs sentence-level granularity, timestamp anchoring (absolute vs relative), confidence per segment, action items / highlights / summary as separate optional fields, transcript-language metadata, and whether each segment carries a back-reference to the source recording's playable URL + offset.

### Phase 2 Architecture — Post-Call Intelligence

**Pipeline (end-to-end, one row at a time):**

```
fathom_calls.sync_status='pending'
    ↓
analysis worker picks up the row
    ↓
fetch transcript + highlights + summary from Fathom with include_*=true
    ↓
normalize timestamps (HH:MM:SS strings + numeric seconds → numeric seconds)
    ↓
identify CLOSER vs PROSPECT (see Speaker Identification below)
    ↓
TWO PARALLEL CLAUDE CALLS:
  1. Section Grader      2. Highlight Extractor
    ↓                      ↓
results upsert into call_analyses + call_highlights
    ↓
fathom_calls.sync_status='processed'
```

**Two new tables (migration 010):**

`public.call_analyses` — one row per analyzed call.
- `id` uuid PK
- `fathom_call_id` uuid FK → `public.fathom_calls(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`
- `user_id` uuid FK → `auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`
- `overall_score` integer (0–100)
- `overall_summary` text
- For each of the 5 sections (`intro` / `discovery` / `pitch` / `objection` / `close`), three columns each: `<section>_grade` text, `<section>_score` integer, `<section>_notes` text. (15 columns total across the 5 sections.)
- `one_thing` text — the single actionable to do differently next time
- `follow_up_email` text — draft email body the closer can copy
- `transcript_stored` jsonb — the normalized turn array (`[{ speaker, role, text, start_seconds }, ...]`) kept on the row so the analysis is reproducible and the review page can render the timeline without re-fetching from Fathom
- `speaker_closer_name` text — what name the closer's voice was identified as in this transcript (free-form match against `recorded_by.name`)
- `analyzed_at` timestamptz
- `status` text — `pending` / `processing` / `done` / `error` (separate from `fathom_calls.sync_status`; this one tracks the *analysis* lifecycle within the row)

`public.call_highlights` — one row per highlight moment (typically 5–8 per call).
- `id` uuid PK
- `fathom_call_id` uuid FK → `public.fathom_calls(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`
- `user_id` uuid FK → `auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE`
- `timestamp_seconds` integer — used in the Fathom deep link (`?t=<seconds>`)
- `speaker` text — `CLOSER` or `PROSPECT`
- `quote` text — exact words spoken at the moment
- `observation` text — one factual sentence describing what happened (not commentary)
- `type` text — `buying_signal` / `objection` / `missed_opportunity` / `strong_moment` / `rapport_moment` / `disqualify_signal`
- `sequence_order` integer — display order within the call (the timeline)

RLS on both tables mirrors `fathom_calls` (own / admin-managed / owner via `current_user_role()`). Backend writes via service-role; no INSERT/UPDATE policies.

**Two parallel Claude calls (one analysis pass = both fire concurrently):**

1. **Section Grader.** Inputs: normalized transcript, closer's uploaded script (if present in KB), top winning-call transcripts from KB. Output: structured JSON with `grade`, `score`, and `evidence` (quoted lines from the transcript) for each of the 5 sections (Intro / Discovery / Pitch / Objection Handling / Close). Plus `overall_score`, `overall_summary`, `one_thing`. Plus `follow_up_email` draft. Uses the Call Analysis Accuracy Requirements section as the prompting contract — every claim must cite a quoted transcript line.
2. **Highlight Extractor.** Input: normalized transcript only (no benchmark). Output: array of 5–8 highlights with `timestamp_seconds`, `speaker`, `quote`, `observation`, `type`. Tone explicitly calibrated as **film coach reviewing tape — factual, not cheerleader or critic.** Must connect patterns across related moments (e.g. "the same 'too expensive' objection appears at 00:12:04 and again at 00:34:51 — second one was harder because the first wasn't fully isolated"). No empty validation, no generic feedback.

**Timestamp handling.**

Per the SDK audit in this CLAUDE.md, Fathom's timestamps are inconsistent across types:
- `TranscriptItem.timestamp` → `"HH:MM:SS"` string
- `ActionItem.recording_timestamp` → `"HH:MM:SS"` string
- `Highlight.start_time` / `end_time` → numeric seconds
- `Meeting.recording_start_time` / `recording_end_time` → ISO datetime string (absolute)

The normalizer runs FIRST in the pipeline and converts everything to **numeric seconds from start of recording**. Numeric chosen because Fathom's own deep-link URL uses it: `https://fathom.video/calls/{recording_url_id}?t={seconds}`. All downstream code (analysis, UI, follow-up email "see at 00:12:04" anchors) reads from the normalized numeric form only.

**Speaker identification.**

The new CLAUDE.md SDK audit confirmed `TranscriptItemSpeaker.display_name` is fragile and `matched_calendar_invitee_email` is "Coming soon!" — currently never populated. Phase 2 resolves CLOSER vs PROSPECT this way:

1. Fetch `recorded_by.name` from the Fathom meeting data (the Fathom user who recorded — usually the closer).
2. Fuzzy-match against the set of `display_name` values present in the transcript turns.
3. Tag the matched speaker as `CLOSER`; all other speakers tagged as `PROSPECT`.
4. Store the matched name in `call_analyses.speaker_closer_name` so re-analysis is reproducible and the review page can label turns confidently.
5. **Fallback** when no fuzzy match clears a threshold: pass through raw `display_name` values to Claude and let it infer roles from conversational cues (who asks discovery questions, who pitches, who handles objections). Store the inferred name in `speaker_closer_name`.

Multi-prospect calls (more than one external participant) are out of scope for Phase 2 — first non-closer voice is treated as "the prospect" and a TODO is left for v1.4.0+ to handle panel calls.

**Trigger.**

Phase 2 fires automatically immediately after `/fathom/sync` completes for a row. Async via the existing fire-and-forget pattern (matches the post-call summary and adaptive-learning flows already in the desktop's `stop-session` handler). **The closer never waits.** Calls show up in the dashboard list as `"analyzing…"` (`status='processing'`) and then populate when done (`status='done'`). Errors flip to `status='error'` with an error message stored, and the dashboard surfaces a retry button.

**Call Review page (dashboard).**

When the user clicks a synced call from the library, the review page renders three sections **in this order** (deliberately — highlights come first, grades support):

1. **Call Highlights** — the lead section, most visual prominence. Timeline of the 5–8 highlights with: clickable timestamp link (`fathom.video/calls/{id}?t={seconds}` deep link, opens in a new tab), speaker label badge, exact quote, Scout's one-sentence observation. Pattern notes inline connect related moments. Footer: **"One thing to do differently"** (the `one_thing` field) called out in an accent box.
2. **Section Grades** — five collapsible cards, one per section, each showing the grade letter + score + evidence quotes. Supporting detail, not the focus — collapsed by default with a "show all" affordance.
3. **Follow-up Email** — generated draft with a copy-to-clipboard button. The closer sends manually; Scout never sends.

### CRM Integration Notes
- **Start with GHL (GoHighLevel) and Close.io.** Both have public APIs with OAuth (same UX pattern we built for Fathom in v1.2.0).
- **Read+write**: pipeline stages, activity logging, contact data, deal notes.
- **Write operations require closer confirmation before executing** — never auto-write without the user seeing exactly what will change. The diff-then-confirm pattern is non-negotiable; one wrong silent overwrite of a pipeline stage in a real CRM destroys trust in the entire product.
- **Closer connects CRM account via OAuth popup** — reuse the Fathom popup pattern (`/auth/<provider>/connect` returns `{url}`, popup posts result to opener, closes itself). The state-JWT scheme in `backend/routes/auth.js` is general enough to reuse with a per-provider secret.
- **HubSpot et al. come later** — only after GHL + Close.io are working in production for at least one paying customer.

### Call Analysis Accuracy Requirements
**This is the single most critical feature in Scout.** A wrong grade on a closer's call is worse than no grade — it teaches the wrong lesson and burns trust. The analysis pipeline must:

- **Know the source of the transcript** (Fathom, Zoom, Meet) and weight confidence accordingly. Fathom diarized transcripts get higher baseline confidence than a Zoom auto-caption export.
- **Flag low-confidence analysis** when transcript quality is poor — never hide uncertainty behind a confident grade. The dashboard surfaces a "transcript quality unclear" badge that adjusts how the grade is presented.
- **Reference specific transcript moments as evidence for every grade** — never make unsupported claims. Every assertion in a grade output ("missed the discovery on budget") must include a quoted prospect/closer line + timestamp anchor.
- **Use the closer's uploaded script** (if present in the KB) **as the benchmark** for what "good" looks like for that closer / offer.
- **Use winning-call transcripts from the KB as comparison examples** — the same three-tier KB diversity model that powers `/kb/search` (learned patterns → user uploads → seeded frameworks) extends naturally to "find the closest winning call to compare this one against."
- **Grade on a spectrum** — not pass/fail. Five-band scoring (or similar) so closers see directional progress over time, not a binary that flattens nuance.

### Teleprompter (Future Architecture Note)
The original live-teleprompter feature **stays as an optional feature** in v2.0 — not deprecated, just no longer the lead product. Future: prompt mode only activates for **specific call sections** (objection handling, closing) — not during intro/rapport when narration interrupts rapport-building. Requires section detection in the live audio path. Not building now. The architecture (suggestion polling timer, prospect-response gate, closer-active guard) must be preserved so the section-aware mode can slot in without a rewrite.

### Desktop deferred work — persistent-login parity (ready-to-build, ships with next DMG)
The v1.3.1 persistent-login fix was applied to the **website only** (shared
`backend/web/js/scout-auth.js`: 401 refresh-retry interceptor + proactive timer +
unix-seconds `expires_at` handling). The Electron app is dormant (teleprompter
optional), so its parity work is deferred but designed and approved — build it
into the next DMG. What's there today: full session persisted to disk
(`userData/token.json`), `ensureFreshToken()` refreshes on launch and on
`get-token` (deduped via `_refreshInFlight`). Three approved additions:
1. **Proactive refresh timer (main process):** every ~4 min, if the stored
   session is within 5 min of `expires_at`, call `ensureFreshToken()` so the app
   never hands the renderer an about-to-expire token. Mirrors the web timer.
2. **401 IPC handling / retry:** the desktop proxy path routes through
   `get-token`→`ensureFreshToken`, but if a proxied request still returns 401
   (token expired mid-flight), the renderer should signal main to
   `ensureFreshToken()` and retry the request once — the desktop equivalent of
   the web fetch interceptor. Add a 401-retry wrapper in `src/lib/proxy-client.js`.
3. **Stale-token re-auth flow — distinguish transient vs genuine:**
   `ensureFreshToken()` currently calls `clearSessionFromDisk()` on ANY non-OK
   `/auth/refresh`. Change it to clear + reopen login ONLY on a genuine auth
   failure (HTTP 401 / `invalid_grant`); on transient failures (network / 5xx)
   keep the session and retry on next launch, so a Wi-Fi blip doesn't log the
   user out. JWT TTL is 3600s; refresh tokens rotate — the disk write after each
   refresh already persists the rotated token.

## Future Plans
- **Deactivation active-check fail-open is DELIBERATE (User Management, 2026-07-27).** `requireAuth` reads `user_profiles.active` after `getUser` and 401s if false — but on a DB error it FAILS OPEN (skips the gate, logs, proceeds). Accepted reasoning (Justin): the Supabase **ban** already blocks new login + token refresh, so a transient DB blip can only extend an ALREADY-ISSUED access token to its natural expiry (≤60 min) — it can never grant NEW access to a deactivated user. Fail-closed would instead risk locking out every legitimate user during a DB hiccup. So fail-open is the correct trade for a best-effort immediate-revoke layer sitting on top of the durable ban.
- **User-management confirms → proper modals (polish follow-up, logged 2026-07-27).** The `#team-members` add-rep flow is a modal, but email-change and delete use `prompt()` (delete requires typing the email). Accepted as a first cut; replace both with styled modals (delete: a typed-confirm modal like a real destructive-action dialog) in a polish pass.
