- **⚠ A CALL WHOSE TRANSCRIPT FATHOM RETURNS EMPTY WILL NEVER SUCCEED, AT ANY SPEED.** One appeared at call 10 of 173; at that rate ~17 would have accumulated and **halted a completely healthy run at around call 80, with nothing actually wrong.** Backing off is the correct response to overload and the *wrong* response to bad data — the two are opposite instructions wearing the same counter.
- **THE FIX: `SATURATION = /429|rate.?limit|too many requests|overloaded|529/i`, aborting at 4.** Permanent per-call failures are still **counted and reported**, just not treated as a reason to slow down. **Proven against both classes before spending on it.**
- **RESULT: two passes, six loops, `saturation=0` in both** — 130 graded in 33 min, then 25 in 14 min, `errored=0` on the cleanup. **289 of 292 graded, ZERO ungraded.**
- **⚠ THE 3 REMAINING ARE NOT RETRY CANDIDATES AND MUST NOT BE REPORTED AS "ZERO ERRORS":** all three are `No transcript turns after normalize … fetched 0 raw turn(s)` — **the fetch SUCCEEDED and returned an empty array**, so there is nothing to grade. **A named, explained casualty is not a silent one; claiming zero would have been the lie.**
- **⚠ A KILLED RUN LEAVES CLAIMS THAT LOOK LIKE WORK.** Killing the first attempt stranded **6 rows in `processing`**, which the second run then SKIPPED as claimed (`skip=20`) until they aged past `CLAIM_STALE_MS`. **A cleanup pass keyed on `status='processing' AND analyzed_at < now() - 11 minutes` recovers them without fighting a live worker** — and `max(analyzed_at)` advancing does NOT prove progress here, because **`analyzed_at` doubles as the claim timestamp**, so a fresh claim looks identical to a completion.

### 🥇 HEALTH SNAPSHOT THEN TECH SUPPORT — RAISED TO THE TOP OF THE QUEUE (Justin, 2026-08-26)
**Above Zoom, the password page, the role hierarchy and the unconnected-source badge.** The justification is this week's own record: **two support tickets from a handful of beta users, and every one required a human to query the database by hand.** Godwin's needed a **server-log read to establish that nothing was broken at all** — an answer no amount of UI inspection could have produced. **That does not scale past a handful of users.**
- **⚠ THE DEPENDENCY ORDER IS THE POINT AND IS UNCHANGED: the SNAPSHOT is built FIRST, because a ticket without it is just a message box.**
- **⚠⚠ THE SNAPSHOT HAS NO UI — Justin's standing correction. It is generated ON DEMAND and ATTACHED TO A TICKET; the ticket list is the only surface a human sees. Do NOT design a health dashboard.**

**⚠⚠ THE SUPPORT CONTROL IS ON EVERY PAGE — *"on EVERY PAGE they see"*. Four consequences, and they decide the design:**
1. **PERSISTENT CHROME: ONE CONTROL, RENDERED ONCE, SHOWN EVERYWHERE — NEVER COPIED PER PAGE.** ⚠⚠ **This is the codebase's most-repeated failure and it would be at maximum scale here.** The objection label lists had to be unified **twice** (the first sweep missed three more); the company picker rendered on **two** surfaces and would have shown **two pickers on one page**; `teamHeaderHtml` split once and silently removed the date picker from three views. **A control copied onto ~15 pages is that mistake fifteen times over.**
2. **THE TICKET RECORDS WHICH PAGE IT WAS RAISED FROM.** **Free context the app already holds** — it turns *"X isn't working"* into *"X isn't working ON THE CALLS PAGE"* at zero cost, and it is exactly the information a support ticket normally has to ask for.
3. **IT MUST NOT OBSTRUCT ANYTHING.** A control on every page is there **forever** — it cannot cover content, capture a click target, or sit over a scroll region.
4. **⚠⚠ EVERY ROLE, NOT ADMINS — closers are the ones who hit problems.** ⚠ **THIS IS THE EXACT OPPOSITE OF THE TICKET LIST, WHICH STAYS ADMIN-ONLY BECAUSE IT EXPOSES OTHER COMPANIES' DATA.** One feature, **two different permission answers**, and conflating them is a data leak — the submit control is universal, the list is not. **The health snapshot still attaches automatically.**

### ⚠⚠⚠ THE GODWIN "ZOOM WON'T SYNC" INCIDENT — NOTHING WAS BROKEN, AND THAT IS THE FINDING (2026-08-26)
**A live beta customer reported that Zoom sync did nothing. The server log settles it in one line:**
```
[zoom] Sync complete for user a99f548b…: fetched=121 inserted=121 malformed=0 pages=2 analysis_dispatched=20
```
**121 cloud recordings fetched, 121 inserted, 0 malformed.** Token present, `external_account_email` correct, `last_sync_at` 18:56:38, rows created 18:53:31, **0 errored**. **His Zoom was configured correctly and the sync worked.**
- **⚠⚠ SO THE ANSWER TO "WHO HAS TO CHANGE SOMETHING" WAS NEITHER — and that is exactly the answer that is hardest to get from a UI report.** The obvious lead (the Josh case: cloud recording not enabled) was **wrong here**, and checking the settings side first would have sent Justin to tell a customer to change something that was already right.
- **THE EXPERIENCE WAS REAL EVEN THOUGH THE SYSTEM WAS FINE. The sync ran for ~3 MINUTES**, and `syncZoomNow` renders its result **only when the request RETURNS** — so the whole wait is one static line, *"Syncing your calls now — this usually takes a few minutes"*, with **no count, no progress, and nothing to distinguish it from a dead button.**
- **⚠ THE ZOOM PATH IS NOT MISSING THE HONEST STATES — I CHECKED BEFORE ASSUMING.** `syncZoomNow` handles `no_recordings` plainly, not-connected, HTTP failure with the reason, network error, and success with a count; the onboarding card renders a distinct syncing state for Zoom exactly as it does for Fathom. **The brief's suspicion that "only Fathom got them" is not what the code says.**
- **⚠⚠ THE REAL RESIDUE IS THE FIRST-SYNC CAP MEETING A LARGE BACKLOG: 20 of 121 were auto-graded, leaving 101 that nothing will ever grade unless a human presses the backfill control — and NOTHING ON HIS SCREEN SAYS SO.** The cap is correct (it stops a connect firing hundreds of analyses); the silence about the remainder is the gap. **A customer who syncs 121 calls and sees 20 graded reasonably concludes it half-worked.**
- **THE 3301 REQUEUE IS LIVE AND VISIBLY WORKING** — repeated `REQUEUED (transcript not ready) … code 3301 "This recording is still being processed"` followed by `re-dispatching N call(s)`. **A Zoom recording is not readable the moment it exists**, and that requeue is what stops those becoming permanent errors.

### ⚠⚠ TELL CLOSERS WHAT ZOOM NEEDS — AND THE GODWIN CASE PROVES *CHECKING* BEATS *TELLING* (filed 2026-08-26, NOT BUILT)
**Scout says "Connected: Zoom" and nothing else. Two people have now raised the same ticket.** Zoom exposes **CLOUD** recordings only, and two settings are easy to confuse: **cloud recording ENABLED** vs **recordings actually GOING to the cloud** rather than the local machine. **Only the second produces anything Scout can read** — a closer recording locally has a perfectly working connection and zero visible recordings.
- **THE QUICK VERSION: say it ON THE SCREEN WHERE THEY CONNECT**, in plain language, before they click Sync and watch nothing happen. Not a help doc, not an email. **Mention auto-record too** — Justin runs it deliberately *"so reps don't forget to record."*
- **⚠⚠ THE BETTER VERSION — CONNECT THEN CHECK, DO NOT INSTRUCT AND HOPE.** Scout can already ask Zoom how many cloud recordings exist, so **it knows within seconds**: *"Zoom is connected, but we can't see any cloud recordings — here's what to switch on."* That turns a support ticket into a sentence on screen.
- **⚠⚠ THE GODWIN INCIDENT IS THE ARGUMENT FOR THE CHECK OVER THE TELL, and it is the opposite of what the ticket suggested. HIS SETTINGS WERE ALREADY CORRECT — 121 recordings synced.** A connect-time instruction would have told him to fix something that was not broken; **a check would have said "121 recordings found" and he would never have raised the ticket at all.** **The check covers BOTH outcomes; the instruction covers only one, and gets the other actively wrong.**
- **⚠ ZERO RECORDINGS AND A FAILED CHECK MUST NOT LOOK THE SAME.** *"We found none"* and *"we could not ask"* are different facts — the standing absent-vs-excluded rule, in its most customer-visible form yet.
- **⚠ CROSS-REFERENCES:** the Godwin entry above · the honest sync states (**Zoom already HAS them — verify before rebuilding**) · Justin's requirement that *"Zoom HAS TO work as good or better than Fathom"*. **RECOMMENDED HIGH, near the unconnected-source badge — Justin decides.**

### ⚠⚠⚠ THE OFFER AXIS — INDEPENDENT OF THE ORG TREE, AND IT IS WHAT MAKES A COMPARISON VALID (filed 2026-08-26, NOT BUILT, NOT A PRIORITY)
**An OFFER is the company a closer is selling FOR. Justin future-proofing for signed agencies — file it, do not build it.**
- **⚠⚠ THE ORG TREE AND THE OFFER ARE INDEPENDENT AXES, and neither implies the other.** A rep can change offer and keep their manager and director; a rep can change manager or director and keep their offer. **Justin's real example: ONE DIRECTOR, 100 CLOSERS, NO MANAGERS, 30 OFFERS.** For that director **the tree says nothing useful and the offer says everything** — which is the case that proves they cannot be collapsed into one hierarchy.
- **⚠⚠ IT IS NOT A REPORTING FILTER, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT.** Scout's value is COMPARISON — *this rep handles spouse objections worse than the team*. **Across 30 offers that comparison is NOISE**: objection mix, qualification criteria and what a good call even looks like are all offer-specific. **Josh's "640 credit score, 10k saved" is a fact about HIS OFFER, not about him** — which the qualification-check work already treats as per-user free text, and which is really per-OFFER.
  **⚠ SO AN OFFER IS THE UNIT THAT MAKES A COMPARISON VALID AT ALL, not a dimension you slice an existing report by.** Filtering by offer after the fact still compares reps who were never comparable.
- **⚠⚠ IT LANDS ON AN ALREADY-DEFERRED PIECE OF WORK — TWO REASONS, ONE MECHANISM.** A call would need **stamping with the OFFER it belonged to**, because reps move between offers. **That is the same mechanism as stamping a call with its TEAM**, already filed and deferred under the history ruling (`fathom_calls` carries only `user_id`; team scope resolves `managed_by` at query time, so moving a rep retroactively moves their whole past). **Whoever builds either should build both — the column, the write at sync time, and the sweep of every scoped query are shared.**
- **⚠⚠ A MANAGER PAGE AND A DIRECTOR PAGE ARE DIFFERENT JOBS, NOT ONE PAGE SCOPED DIFFERENTLY.** A manager with 7 closers **coaches individuals**. A director with 100 closers across 30 offers **manages a portfolio and will never look at a person**. Same underlying data, **different primary axis: per-person vs per-offer.** Building the director view as "the manager view with a wider scope" produces a page nobody can use.
- **⚠ CROSS-REFERENCES: the role hierarchy + inherited-visibility entry above** (the tree axis) **and the history/team-stamp ruling** (the shared mechanism). **NOT A PRIORITY — bottom of the list.**

### ⚠⚠⚠ THE TARGET ORG SHAPE AND THE VISIBILITY RULE — THIS AMENDS THE ROLE-HIERARCHY ITEM (filed 2026-08-26, NOT BUILT)
**The largest agency Justin would sign, in his own framing — recorded as SHAPE, not as work:**
```
Company
  └── Head of Sales / CSO          ⚠ NOT NEEDED YET — the eventual top of the tree, not a request
        ├── Director 1 ── 3 Managers ── ~7 Closers each
        ├── Director 2 ── 3 Managers ── ~7 Closers each
        └── Director 3 ── 3 Managers ── ~7 Closers each
                              ≈ 9 managers, 63 closers under ONE company
```

**⚠⚠ THE VISIBILITY RULE, VERBATIM: *"A manager can only view closers under him. Directors can see all teams below him. Head of sales can see all teams in the company."*** → **EVERY LEVEL SEES EVERYTHING BENEATH IT AND NOTHING BESIDE IT.**
- **⚠⚠ THIS IS NOT AN ADMIN-VIEW RULE, AND READING IT AS ONE IS THE EXPENSIVE MISTAKE.** It governs **every number Scout produces** — team averages, the daily digest, objection drilldowns, needs-work, rankings, EOD, every synthesis. **Each of those has to know where the viewer sits in the tree.** A director's team average spans all their managers' closers; **two directors in the same company must never see each other's numbers.**
- **⚠ SO THE FILED ROLE ITEM IS NO LONGER "A RENAME PLUS ONE NEW TIER". It is A HIERARCHY WITH INHERITED VISIBILITY**, and whoever picks it up reads this entry first.

**⚠⚠ TWO CONSEQUENCES, RECORDED AND DELIBERATELY NOT RESOLVED:**
1. **"A COMPANY IS A RENAMED TEAM" STOPS BEING TRUE AT THIS SCALE — flagged AGAINST that ruling rather than silently contradicting it.** It holds **today**, because a company is one manager's people. In this diagram a company is **the whole tree** with a head of sales at the top. **The ruling is correct now and must be revisited when Director lands.** ⚠ It is the same expiry the `team_name` storage entry already names (*"a client with TWO sales managers appears as TWO companies … a known expiry date"*) — **now with the full shape attached, so the revisit has something concrete to aim at.**
2. **SCOPE RESOLUTION BECOMES A TREE WALK, NOT A LOOKUP.** `resolveTeam` (`routes/team.js:111`) answers *"who does this manager manage"* in **ONE** step off `managed_by`. **A director is TWO steps; a head of sales is THREE.** Doing that on every page load, for 63 closers, is a **different job** from what exists — and every team-scoped query downstream inherits it. ⚠ **Recorded as a design question for whoever builds the roles. Do NOT design it now.**

**⚠ THE PART MOST LIKELY TO BE UNDER-ESTIMATED: the visibility rule is not a filter you add at the end.** Scope is resolved once, near the top, and then flows into every lane — so a change from *lookup* to *tree walk* touches the one place every metric already depends on. That is why this amends the role item rather than sitting beside it.

### ⚠⚠ COMPANIES ARE RENAMED TEAMS — THE STORAGE DECISION, AND WHY IT IS A COLUMN (2026-08-24, `e4679e8`)
**⚠⚠ READ THE ENTRY DIRECTLY ABOVE FIRST (2026-08-26): the target org shape puts a HEAD OF SALES over several DIRECTORS, at which point a company is the WHOLE TREE and "a company is one manager's team" stops being true. This ruling is correct TODAY and has a named expiry — it is flagged, not superseded.**
**Justin's ruling: a COMPANY IS A RENAMED TEAM, admin view only. No new tier, no schema change beyond storage for the name.** Migration 043 adds **`user_profiles.team_name`** — one nullable column on the HEAD's row.
- **⚠⚠ A COLUMN, NOT A `companies` TABLE, AND THE REASONING IS THE DURABLE PART.** A team is ALREADY uniquely identified by the user its members point at via `managed_by`, so there is no second key to invent and a table would add an id, an FK and a join to store one string. **More importantly: creating the entity is the first half of a company TIER — it is exactly how "renamed team" quietly becomes "a thing above teams", which is the ruling this is meant to respect.** Promote it to a table when something else about a company needs storing, with a real reason, not speculatively.
- **NULL IS MEANINGFUL** (never named) — a `''` default would make "unnamed" and "named the empty string" indistinguishable. Applied **inert**, before any code read it.
- **THE FALLBACK IS `"Unnamed company"`, AND IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT DERIVED FROM THE HEAD.** A derived label ("Josh's company") reads as a real name, so nobody questions it and **nobody ever sets the real one**. `companyDisplayName` takes ONLY the stored name — deriving one is impossible rather than merely discouraged, and a test pins its arity to keep it that way. Same governing principle as prospect names: **a WRONG name is worse than NO name.**
- **⚠ IT REPLACED AN EMAIL DRESSED UP AS A COMPANY.** `resolveTeam` rendered **`"josh@scoutsystems.io's team"`** and `/team/context` built the same shape for the picker — **two generators, both wrong in the same way.** Both now call `companyDisplayName`.

### ⚠⚠ A COMPANY IS DEFINED BY HAVING REPS, NEVER BY ROLE (2026-08-24)
**Live data settles it: `joshua@soberlivingriches.com` has role `manager` and ZERO reps. They are a SINGLE USER, not a company of one.** `lib/company.js bucketUsers` keys on reps, matching the standing "has assigned reps (not role) grants the manager experience" rule.
- **EXACTLY-ONE IS GUARANTEED BY CONSTRUCTION, including the shape the schema allows and the data does not yet contain:** a user with **BOTH** reps and a manager. `reps > 0` is checked FIRST, so they head their own company and do **not** also render as a member — double-counting there would inflate every company's user count. That is the future second-tier shape, so heading their own company is the forward-compatible reading. **Decided, not discovered.**
- **ROLE RANKS ARE SPACED (owner 10 / manager 30 / user 50) ON PURPOSE** so a future tier slots in **without renumbering** and **without this file naming a role that does not exist yet**. A test fails if the gaps are compacted.
- **THE GROUPING RUNS SERVER-SIDE** because `admin.html` cannot `require()`. The alternative was a **fourth** mirrored predicate in a codebase that has already paid for duplicated scope rules, two `rankSections` meaning opposite directions, and a manager-membership rule eight endpoints did not have.

### ⚠⚠ `max-height` ON A LIST HID REAL PEOPLE — AND ONLY A SCREENSHOT COULD FIND IT (2026-08-24, `a7ad0c4`)
**`.users-table-wrap` carried `max-height: 320px; overflow: auto`. A 5-member company block is 384px tall, so its last TWO members sat behind an inner scrollbar.** It was **pre-existing** — the 8-row All Users list was already truncated at about 6 — and company blocks only made it visible.
- **⚠⚠ EVERY MEASUREMENT SAID IT WAS FINE.** All five rows were in the DOM (`rowsRendered: 5`), and **`getBoundingClientRect` reported every one of them visible** — because they DO have size. They simply sat inside a clipped container. **The screenshot showed the section ending after row 3 and the numbers did not.**
- **THE RULE, and it is the measurement-is-not-a-composition-test lesson in a new costume: a DOM check cannot see clipping by an ancestor.** `getBoundingClientRect` describes the element, not whether anything is painting it. When a count and a picture disagree, **believe the picture and go looking for the container.**
- **A nested VERTICAL scrollbar inside a page that also scrolls hides content nobody knows to scroll for.** Horizontal is kept for wide tables; vertical is the page's job.

### ⚠ MY OWN GUARD'S SCOPE WAS WIDER THAN ITS CLAIM — AGAIN, INSIDE A GUARD WRITTEN AGAINST DUPLICATION (2026-08-24)
A new test forbade `managed_by === x.user_id` anywhere in `admin.html`, to stop the page re-deriving company membership. **It matched `managedByControlHtml`, which compares exactly those two to mark a dropdown option `selected`** — a legitimate, unrelated use.
- **The claim was "the page must not BUILD the buckets"; the scope was "this string appears nowhere".** Narrowed to assert `companiesArray` is assigned only from the server payload, that no local `bucketUsers` exists, and that the assignment count is exactly two.
- **Nth instance of the scope-vs-claim family, and the second time it has happened INSIDE a guard I was writing to prevent a related failure.** Knowing the rule is demonstrably not the same as applying it.

### ⚠⚠ FATHOM HAS NO IDENTITY ENDPOINT AND ITS TOKEN IS WORKSPACE-SCOPED — PROBED, NOT ASSUMED (2026-08-24)
Justin challenged the identity prompt: *"He logs in to Fathom when he connects it. That's going to be the Fathom email."* **Probed live against a real token:**
```
/me /users/me /user /account /whoami /oauth/userinfo  ->  404
/users                                                 ->  403
/team_members  -> 200 {name,email,created_at}, and NO self-identifying field
/meetings (unfiltered) -> 5 DISTINCT recorded_by identities in ONE page
```
- **The OAuth grant is `scope=public_api`, NOT OIDC** — no `id_token`, no userinfo. **Fathom genuinely does not tell us who authorised**, and without `recorded_by[]` a sync pulls the whole workspace. **The prompt is required and must not be removed.**
- **⚠ BUT IT NO LONGER HAS TO BE THE FIRST RESORT.** `/team_members` lists the workspace, so when the **Scout login email is EXACTLY one member**, that is the answer — captured at connect, picker kept as fallback. **Exact equality only** (same contract as `lib/zoom-identity.js`): a wrong match syncs **a different person's calls** into the account. Plus-addresses are NOT folded, near-misses refuse, ambiguity refuses.
- **The token exchange reads only `access_token`/`refresh_token`/`expires_in`/`scope` and never inspects the rest** — the standing *"a field absent from storage is not absent from the source"* hazard. Probing is what settled it.

### ⚠⚠ "IT ONLY SYNCS 30 DAYS" WAS A CALL-COUNT CAP, NOT A DATE WINDOW (2026-08-24)
```
Fathom page size is HARD-CODED at 10 and IGNORES ?limit   (25 and 100 both -> 10, echoing "limit":10)
MAX_PAGES 20  x  10  =  200 calls, ever, on a first sync
Josh received exactly 200, spanning 38 days      <- the "month" was arithmetic
his real history: 560 meetings / 56 pages / 17.8s, back to 2021-09-16
```
- **Nobody chose 30 days.** The number nobody set was `MAX_PAGES`, and it was silently dropping **360 of his calls**.
- **⚠⚠ AND THE OLDER TAIL WAS PERMANENTLY UNREACHABLE.** Once the first sync stamps `last_sync_at`, every later sync passes `created_after = last_sync_at` and can therefore **only ever fetch NEWER calls**. The UI's *"run sync again to fetch the rest"* was **false** — re-running could never go backwards. That is why the fix is a re-runnable history pull that deliberately **ignores `last_sync_at`**, not merely a bigger cap.
- **THE PAGE CAP MUST SCALE WITH THE CHOICE** or "All time" is a lie: offering it on a 20-page cap returns the same 200 rows and looks like it worked.
- **⚠ NEVER SEND AN EPOCH `created_after` INSTEAD OF OMITTING IT** — Fathom returns ZERO for a 1970 value regardless of other filters. "Just send a very old date" is the obvious wrong fix.

### ⚠⚠ A BACKFILL MUST NOT INHERIT STEADY-STATE GRADING (2026-08-24)
`callIdsToAnalyze` grades **every** new row when `last_sync_at` is set — correct for a normal sync, whose window is bounded by real call volume, and **catastrophic for a backfill: pulling 560 calls would have fired 560 analyses.** History mode passes `null` so the first-sync cap applies.
- **Caught BEFORE clicking it on production**, by asking what the existing selector would do with a 160-row insert. **Pulling calls and grading calls are separate budgets and must stay separate.**
- Verified live: 160 backfilled, **159 with no analysis row**, library full, newest 20 graded.

### ⚠⚠ NAMING RULING — "COMPANY" IN ADMIN, "TEAM" EVERYWHERE ELSE (Justin, 2026-08-24)
He asked for a professional opinion and accepted this one. **The admin console says COMPANY; every other surface keeps saying TEAM.**
- **⚠⚠ THE CAVEAT THAT WILL BITE, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS A TIME BOMB, NOT A NITPICK: a company is currently stored as ONE MANAGER'S TEAM** (`user_profiles.team_name` on the head's row). **A client with TWO sales managers therefore appears as TWO companies.** That is acceptable today and **stops being true the moment the filed Director role lands**, because a Director sits above several managers — at which point one company legitimately spans several teams and the label can no longer live on a head's row.
- **WHEN THAT HAPPENS, "company" needs REAL STORAGE** (its own row, with teams pointing at it) rather than a name on a user. **Do not treat the current column as the design** — it is the cheapest thing that is correct under a one-manager-per-company assumption, and that assumption has a known expiry date.

### ⚠⚠ A COMPANY IS "HAS REPS **OR** IS NAMED" — WIDENED SO ONE CAN BE CREATED (2026-08-24)
The original definition was **has reps**, which makes "Add company" **impossible by construction**: a brand-new company has no members, so its head files under Single Users and the company **vanishes the instant it is made**.
- Now: **reps OR an explicitly-set name.** **It reclassifies nobody** — naming is an explicit owner action, and the one pre-existing named row already had reps. A `manager` with zero reps and no name (a real production row) is **still a single user**. **Role is never the key.**
- **⚠ THIS SUPERSEDED A GUARD SHIPPED THE SAME DAY.** `PATCH /admin/companies/:id/name` refused a target with no reps, reasoning that *"a name stored there is invisible forever"*. **That reasoning is now false** — naming is how a company is created. The guard became a **ROLE** check (a plain `user` may not head a company), and **its HTTP test was CONVERTED, not deleted**: the property that outlived the old rule is the one still asserted.
- **CREATING A COMPANY = PROMOTING AN EXISTING SINGLE USER**, not creating a person and a company together: `+ Create user` already assigns a role and a manager, every real company starts from someone already in Scout, and **promoting the wrong person is one dropdown to undo where a duplicate account is not.**

### ⚠⚠ THE "WHO HAS NOT CONNECTED A RECORDING SOURCE" BADGE — AND WHY SHOWING NOTHING AT ZERO DOES *NOT* CONTRADICT THE ZERO-IS-A-MEASUREMENT RULE (filed 2026-08-26, NOT BUILT)
**A dismissible badge at the top of the Manager view naming reps with no recording source. Justin: HIGH priority — above Zoom and the password page.**
- **⚠⚠ THE DISMISSAL BEHAVIOUR IS THE WHOLE DESIGN, AND IT IS NOT RULED YET. Both obvious options fail, in opposite directions:**
```
dismissed FOREVER          -> a rep onboarded in three weeks never triggers it.
                              THE BADGE QUIETLY STOPS WORKING AND NOBODY NOTICES.
dismissed UNTIL TOMORROW   -> it nags, and a daily nag is ignored on sight.
```
  **ARCHITECT'S RECOMMENDATION (Justin has NOT ruled — record it, do not decide it): dismissal is PER UNCONNECTED PERSON, not per badge.** Gone for people the manager has already been told about; returns for anyone NEWLY unconnected. **That is the only variant where "I have dealt with this" and "something new happened" stay distinguishable.**
- **⚠⚠ A FULLY CONNECTED TEAM MUST SEE NOTHING — and this is NOT a violation of the standing `zero is a measurement, absence is not` rule.** The distinction is the surface's JOB:
  - a **DATA surface** (a category list, a grid, a breakdown) is something the reader COUNTS and RECONCILES, so an omitted row silently changes the total and must render as `0`;
  - an **EXCEPTION surface** (an alert, a badge, a warning) exists only to say *something needs your attention* — and **"0 things need your attention" is not information, it is furniture.** A badge that is always present trains people to stop seeing it.
  **⚠ A future session WILL read these two rules together and think they conflict. They do not: one governs what a measurement omits, the other governs whether an alert should exist at all.**
- **⚠ IT REPORTS A FACT ABOUT OTHER PEOPLE, so it is manager-and-above, ENFORCED SERVER-SIDE — never by hiding it client-side.** Same rule as every other cross-user surface.
- **⚠⚠ IT IS A BADGE ON A PAGE, NOT A NOTIFICATION.** Scout has no notification system and that gap is already filed as blocking EOD manager approval. **This must not pull that work in** — the whole reason it is buildable at high priority is that it needs none of it.

### ⚠⚠ THE ADMIN COMPANY COLUMN SHOWS A DATABASE RELATIONSHIP WHERE A BUSINESS FACT BELONGS (filed 2026-08-26 — NOT BUILT)
**The feature works and Justin could not find it.** The column already moves people between companies; it lists **MANAGER EMAIL ADDRESSES**, so moving someone into Scout Systems means picking `josh@scoutsystems.io` and already knowing that address *is* Scout Systems. **The underlying model — a company is whoever a manager manages — is leaking onto the screen.**
- **JUSTIN'S RULING:** pick a **COMPANY BY NAME** and let Scout resolve the manager. *"If I could drag and drop or have an actual company dropdown that would be better. Then it auto adds them under whatever manager(s) are on that team."* **Drag-and-drop preferred, a name dropdown is the minimum; drag-and-drop is materially more work and that must be said when it is picked up.**
- **✅ THE MULTI-MANAGER QUESTION IS ANSWERED (Justin, same day) — IT WAS NEVER A BLOCKER, IT IS A DEPENDENCY.** *"If a company has more than 1 manager they will have a director to fold up into. That's what that role is for."* **The company points at its DIRECTOR and the managers sit underneath, so picking a company is never ambiguous.**
  - **⚠⚠ SO THE ROLE HIERARCHY IS A PREREQUISITE FOR A CLEAN VERSION OF THIS FEATURE, not an unrelated rename sitting elsewhere on the list.** Without Director there is nothing for several managers to fold into — a company-name picker built first works only while every company has exactly one manager, and needs revisiting the moment one does not. **Build order matters here; the dependency is real rather than nominal.**
  - **⚠ DO NOT CONFUSE THIS WITH THE ADMIN-VIEW REBUILD, which explicitly DROPPED its role-hierarchy dependency** by shipping with today's three roles and slotting Director in later. **That reasoning does not transfer: the rebuild only has to DISPLAY companies, while this feature has to RESOLVE one to a manager — and that is the exact operation Director exists to make well-defined.**
  - **⚠ THIS IS THE SAME EXPIRY ALREADY RECORDED AGAINST THE `user_profiles.team_name` STORAGE DECISION** (*"a client with TWO sales managers appears as TWO companies … that assumption has a known expiry date"*). **It is one problem surfacing in two places, not two problems** — whoever builds either should reconcile both.
- **⚠ SECOND SYMPTOM, SAME CAUSE — FIX BOTH OR NEITHER: the HEAD of a company reads "None — single user".** Josh Pinner heads Sober Living Riches and his own Company cell shows him in no company, because `managed_by` is null for a head. **A head is not a member of their own team in the data, and the screen prints that relationship rather than the fact that he runs the place.**
- **THE TRANSFERABLE SHAPE: a column that renders a FOREIGN KEY where a user expects an ENTITY will read as broken or missing even when it works perfectly.** The give-away here is that the feature was not merely confusing — it was **invisible**: the person who asked for it could not tell it existed.

### ⚠⚠⚠ MOVING A REP RETROACTIVELY MOVES ALL THEIR HISTORY — UNDECIDED, JUSTIN'S CALL (2026-08-24)
```
fathom_calls carries ONLY user_id — no team_id, no company_id, no snapshot
team scope resolves managed_by AT QUERY TIME (repIdsFor)
=> reassigning a rep moves EVERY past quarter's calls, cash, close rate and
   objections from the old company's totals to the new one, retroactively
```
- **Nothing records which team a call belonged to WHEN IT HAPPENED**, so there is no way to answer "what did this company do last quarter" independently of today's org chart.
- **⚠ NOT CHANGED, AND DELIBERATELY SO.** Fixing it means either freezing history (snapshot the team on each call) or accepting the retroactive move — **a reporting decision, not an implementation detail.** Reported and stopped.
- **What WAS added: a cycle guard** so a head cannot be pointed at one of their own reps. ⚠ **It is currently UNREACHABLE in live data** — the pre-existing "managed_by must be manager|owner" check fires first for every member, since all members are plain users. It is defence-in-depth for the reps-AND-a-manager shape, which does not exist yet. **Do not record it as live-exercised.**

### ⚠⚠⚠ REMOVING A CARD DELETED A CAPABILITY FOR THE SECOND TIME — AND THE SECOND ONE WENT UNNOTICED FOR MONTHS (2026-08-24)
`6adb774` removed the healthy "Fathom connected" card. That card hosted THREE controls. Sync Now was noticed and moved to Connections **in the same commit** — and the note written at the time says exactly that, so the risk was understood. **The reanalyze / update-analyses controls were not.** `renderFathomStripConnected` has been **DEFINED AND NEVER CALLED** ever since.
- **CONSEQUENCE: a healthy account had NO reachable way to grade ungraded calls.** Josh's trial account sat at **19 graded of 200** with no control anywhere on the page. The calls were fine; the button was gone.
- **⚠ WHY THE FIRST CATCH DID NOT PREVENT THE SECOND: the audit was of the FUNCTION BEING REMOVED, not of the SURFACE.** `syncFathomNow`'s call sites were enumerated because *it* was the thing under discussion. Nobody asked the more general question — *what else did this card host?* **When deleting a container, enumerate what it CONTAINED, not what you came to change.**
- **AND A DEAD BUILDER LOOKS ALIVE.** It renders nothing, throws nothing, and reads as working code; only `grep -c` on its own name (1 = the definition) reveals it. **A function whose only occurrence is its own declaration is dead** — cheap to check, and worth checking whenever a host is removed.

### ⚠⚠ "19 OF 149" WAS CORRECT AND READ AS BROKEN (2026-08-24)
The grading cap is deliberate: a first sync (or a history pull) grades the newest 20 and leaves the rest, so a 560-call import cannot fire 560 analyses. **But the tile said "19 of 149" with no explanation, to a trial customer.**
- **A NUMBER THAT IS RIGHT CAN STILL BE A DEFECT.** The tile now names the remainder ("6 not graded yet") and the control to fix it sits in Connections. **Correct-but-unexplained is indistinguishable from broken**, and the reader has no way to tell them apart.
- **⚠ THE WINDOW PICKER USED TO REQUIRE `outdated > 0`**, so a pure post-import backlog (pending, nothing outdated) fell through to "Analyze next 10" — **18 clicks for 180 calls**. It now shows whenever `pending + outdated > 0`, because `/fathom/update-analyses` dispatches both in one batch. This mismatch was already recorded once as the reason a 2026-08-01 backfill was abandoned; it is now fixed rather than re-noted.
- **COST OF GRADING A BACKLOG, measured from real transcripts (avg 110k chars ≈ 30k tokens, sent to BOTH the grader and the extractor):** ~**2 Sonnet calls and ~60k input tokens per call**, ~50s each serially. **180 calls ≈ 360 model calls ≈ $43 and ~2.5 hours.** ⚠ And the drain **dies on redeploy**, so a multi-hour run must not overlap a push.

### ⚠⚠ AN UPSTREAM DEFAULT IS NOT A DECISION (2026-08-24)
Justin ruled that a new connection backdates **30 days only**. Checking both providers found two different shapes, and only one was a bug:
- **FATHOM had NO date limit** — a first sync omitted `created_after` and pulled to the 200-call page cap. Genuinely unchosen.
- **ZOOM already pulled ~30 days — by INHERITING Zoom's API default when `from` is omitted.** The behaviour was already right, so the tempting conclusion is "Zoom needs no change".
- **⚠ IT DID. A default that lives in someone else's API is not a decision we have made**: Zoom can change it, and nothing in our code states what we intended, so the day it changes the diff shows nothing and the behaviour moves anyway. **Sending the date explicitly costs one line and converts an accident into a choice.**
- **THE GENERAL RULE: when behaviour depends on an upstream default, STATE IT LOCALLY even when the current default already matches.** Same family as the stale load-bearing comment — the difference between something being true and something being *enforced*.
- **⚠ AND A DEFAULT MUST NOT OVERWRITE A CHOICE.** The Fathom connect handler is an **upsert**, so writing `'30d'` unconditionally would silently narrow anyone **reconnecting** who had chosen 90 days or all time — with nothing on screen to say so. It reads the existing window first.

### ⚠⚠ DELETE-A-COMPANY: THE VERIFICATION, AND THE ONE HAZARD THAT REMAINS (2026-08-24)
Proven on a throwaway seeded with **all seven** data types the ruling names. **Every other company byte-identical before and after; zero residue.**
```
knowledge_base  1134 -> 1132  (-2, exactly the seeded personal rows)
  scope=global   583 ->  583  UNCHANGED — shared content preserved
eod_edits           7 ->    1  (-6)
synthesis cache   452 ->  450  (-2)
calls / analyses / highlights / prospects        all gone via CASCADE
```
- **⚠ THE FIRST ATTEMPT PROVED NOTHING AND SAID IT HAD.** The fixture's KB insert failed silently (`label` is NOT NULL and I did not check `.error`), so `kb_rows_deleted: 0` was reported as a pass when the path had **never been exercised**. **A cleanup count of zero is only evidence if you first proved the rows existed.** The second run seeded 2 and deleted 2.
- **⚠⚠ THE HAZARD, REPORTED AND NOT FIXED: A REP WHO MOVED COMPANIES CARRIES THEIR OLD CALLS.** Calls belong to a PERSON; team totals are computed from who manages whom *right now*. So deleting a company destroys **calls earned at a previous company**, and nothing warns — the confirmation counts them as this company's. **It cannot be detected from the data** either, because nothing records which team a call belonged to when it happened. **This is the history-freeze question wearing its most expensive costume**, and it is Justin's to rule on.
- **⚠ THE FINAL CLICK IS UNVERIFIED.** The confirm + typed-name prompt are native dialogs this harness cannot drive; the endpoint was exercised over HTTP instead. **Do not record the button as click-tested.**

### ⚠⚠ "3 CALLS, HE TOOK 6" — THE NUMBER WAS RIGHT AND THE PAGE WAS STILL WRONG (2026-08-24)
Three separate causes, and the count itself was never one of them.
```
17:0x fathom  present          20:0x fathom  present
18:0x fathom  present          22:0x fathom  NOT SYNCED YET (last sync 22:34)
19:01 ZOOM ONLY — that account has no Zoom connection, so it can NEVER appear
                               23:02 fathom  NOT SYNCED YET
```
- **EOD reads `fathom_calls` and LEFT-joins analyses, so it counts SYNCED calls, not analysed ones.** The grading backlog — the obvious suspect given everything else this week — **is not involved**. Ruled out with evidence rather than assumed away, along with the ET bounds, `eod_edits`, and `not_a_sales_call`.
- **⚠ THE FIX WAS THE WORDING. `3` AND `3 SO FAR` RENDER IDENTICALLY**, and only one is true. Inflating the count would have put calls on the page the data cannot support. EOD now carries sync freshness and says *"still filling up — synced up to X"*.
- **⚠ COMPLETE MEANS "A SYNC RAN AFTER THE DAY ENDED", NOT "THE SYNC IS RECENT".** A historical day synced days later is complete; warning on it would be noise, and noise is how a real warning gets ignored.
- **⚠ BOTH EXITS CARRY IT — the EMPTY-day return most of all.** Zero calls with no explanation is the worst version of this bug, and that early return is the one easiest to forget.

### ⚠⚠⚠ DUAL-SOURCE INGESTION IS NOW VISIBLY DOUBLE-COUNTING A REAL SURFACE (2026-08-24)
On the account with BOTH providers connected, **EOD shows 10 calls for 6 real meetings.** Fathom and Zoom each ingest the same meeting ~1 minute apart. **Proven, not inferred:**
```
17:02 zoom 240s  <-> 17:03 fathom 228s      20:01 zoom 3120s <-> 20:02 fathom 3088s
18:01 zoom 3000s <-> 18:02 fathom 2997s     22:03 zoom    0s <-> 22:04 fathom 1850s
```
- **This is the recorded ONE-ACTIVE-SOURCE-PER-USER ruling, which was never built.** It was filed as inert *"because Fathom's bot creates no Zoom cloud recording"* — **that reprieve has expired**: cloud recording is now on, and every call is arriving twice.
- **⚠ THE DEDUPE KEY CANNOT SOLVE IT.** `UNIQUE (user_id, fathom_call_id)` holds each PROVIDER'S OWN id, so two providers can never collide by construction. **It is the wrong key for the question, not a gap in the logic.**
- **⚠ AND `meeting_id` DOES NOT PAIR THEM EITHER** — a Personal Meeting Room reuses ONE id for every meeting, so all ten rows share `8924530025`. What identifies a pair is **start time within ~60s plus near-identical duration**. Any future dedupe has to key on that, not on an id.
- **Not an EOD bug; EOD is faithfully reporting what was ingested. Filed.**

### ⚠⚠ CROSS-PROVIDER DUPLICATES — THE MATCH, AND WHY OVERLAP BEAT DURATION (2026-08-24)
Josh had both providers connected; **23 duplicate rows** inflated his cash by **$44,100 (16%)**. Tolerances **derived from his whole corpus**, ranked by how much the two recordings OVERLAP as a fraction of the longer one:
```
99 99 99 99 99 98 98 98 98 98 97 97 96 96 95 95 93 92 92 86 78 72 71   genuine
                                                                42     ambiguous
                                                                 0     KNOWN false pair
```
**An EMPTY BAND between 42 and 71**, so the threshold sits inside it rather than on a round number — same discipline as `MIN_CUE_GAP_SECONDS`.
- **⚠⚠ DURATION DELTA WAS THE OBVIOUS METRIC AND IS THE WRONG ONE. Zoom reports WHOLE MINUTES** (every Zoom value in the corpus divides by 60) while Fathom reports exact seconds, so a delta conflates **rounding** with a real difference. It is also **scale-blind**: 90s is fatal on a 4-minute call and meaningless on a 90-minute one. Overlap answers the question actually being asked — *were these recordings of the same stretch of time?*
- **⚠⚠ AND `meeting_id` CANNOT PAIR THEM.** A Personal Meeting Room reuses ONE id for every meeting it ever hosts — all of Josh's rows carry `8924530025`. Anything keyed on it would merge his entire history into one call.
- **FALSE POSITIVE >> FALSE NEGATIVE**, and the rule is tuned for it: two back-to-back calls share **no** time, so they score 0% and are rejected by the gap AND the overlap. A missed duplicate only leaves a count high — visible and reversible.
- **⚠ MY FIRST TEST FIXTURE HAD THE SIGN REVERSED**, which placed the short call INSIDE the long one and made the known false pair overlap 10% instead of 0%. **Direction matters in an interval comparison** — quote the real timestamps rather than reconstructing them.
- **SUPPRESS, NEVER DELETE.** `duplicate_of` points at the row KEPT, `ON DELETE SET NULL` so a duplicate un-suppresses if its survivor ever goes. Fathom wins today as an **ordered preference that can flip** — Justin intends Zoom to replace Fathom.

### ⚠⚠ 21 EXCLUSION SITES, AND THE GUARD ASSERTS THE **PAIRING** (2026-08-24)
`not_a_sales_call` is excluded in 21 places across 13 files. A duplicate is the same kind of row — present, real, must not be counted — so both exclusions now sit **adjacent** at every site.
- **⚠ COUNTING EACH FILTER SEPARATELY WOULD PASS WHILE THEY SAT IN DIFFERENT QUERIES.** The test asserts they appear **together**, so a new counting surface cannot pick up one and forget the other. That is precisely how nine modules have dropped out of lists here before.
- **THE LIST DELIBERATELY DOES NOT FILTER EITHER.** The library is a RECORD; the counts are the aggregate. A suppressed duplicate is **shown with a badge**, exactly like a not-a-sales-call row — hiding it would be data loss wearing a filter's clothes.

### ⚠ "SHOW MORE DOESN'T ALWAYS SHOW UP" — NOT REPRODUCED (2026-08-24)
Checked, and **I could not make it fail.** What was tested: offsets 0/20/40 across all five filters and both sorts on live data — **every page returned a full 20**; the has-more rule (`returned >= limit`) against the rows actually rendered; that filter/range/sort changes reset the offset and null the list; and that the default 7-day range is not simply under one page (it holds 72 calls).
- **⚠ ONE REAL DEFECT FOUND BY READING, NOT THE REPORTED ONE: `loadCallsList` uses `.range(0, 9999)` on the filtered/sorted path** — a silent **10,000-row ceiling**. Nowhere near Josh (596), so it is not this bug, but it truncates without saying so and will bite eventually. **Filed, not fixed.**
- **Reported as unreproduced rather than fixed forward.** An intermittent symptom closed without a reproduction is a guess, and this codebase has already had two faults stacked where fixing one changed nothing.

### ⚠⚠⚠ STANDING RULING — CASH COLLECTED IS EOD-ONLY (Justin, 2026-08-25)
His words: *"we don't track cash collected at all, it's too finicky right now. The only time it's needed is on the EOD report."*
- **DO NOT report cash figures in findings, do not use cash as evidence, do not put it in before/after tables.** It kept being surfaced; this stops it.
- **A DISPLAY RULING, NOT A DATA ONE.** `call_analyses.cash_collected` is still extracted by the grader and still drives the EOD report. **Do not drop the column or stop collecting it.**
- **Removed from every other surface, enumerated by capability:** the team glance tile · the per-rep quick-stats line · that tile's per-rep drilldown · the drilldown metric label · both copy-for-summary payloads · `avg_cash` in the needs-work summary · and the derivations in `lib/team-analytics.js` and `lib/session-analytics.js`.
- **⚠ REMOVED FROM THE PAYLOADS, not merely unrendered** — a field that is still computed is one edit away from reappearing on a tile.
- **Nothing renders blank:** the team stat row simply carries five tiles instead of six, and cash was never the default drilldown metric.

### ⚠⚠ "SHOW MORE" WAS THE FIRST RENDER, NOT THE QUERY (2026-08-25)
Proven the block before that every page position returns full rows, so the data was never the problem. **`renderCallLibrary` only loads when the list is null**, so entering Calls re-rendered whatever was cached earlier in the session — **the rows AND `callLibraryHasMore`, both computed at some earlier moment and never re-checked.**
- **THE SEQUENCE: page to the end once (`hasMore=false`), leave, come back after new calls have synced** — that account syncs every 2 hours — **and the stale list renders with no button and no hint that more exist.** Whether it looked broken depended on what you had done earlier in the session, which is exactly why it read as *random*.
- **⚠ THE FIX IS NOT A LONGER WAIT** (the brief said so, and it was right): entering the view now refetches, so the control's visibility is a function of data fetched when it renders.
- **⚠ `call-review` IS EXCLUDED DELIBERATELY.** Coming back from a call to the list is not a fresh entry; resetting there would drop the user's place — a regression dressed as a fix.
- **⚠ AND IT IS INVALIDATED IN `setView`, NEVER IN THE RENDERER.** `loadCallLibrary` re-renders when it finishes, so nulling the list inside the renderer would wipe it mid-page and paging could never advance.

### ⚠⚠⚠ THE COMMENT-STRIPPER ORDERING BUG WAS IN **ELEVEN** GUARDS, AND ONE CORRECT EDIT DETONATED TWO OF THEM (2026-08-25)
The hazard is already on file — strip LINE comments before BLOCK comments, because a block-comment opener inside a line comment is a false opener that pairs with the next real closer and swallows everything between. **What was not on file is how widespread it was.**
- Adding ONE block comment to `lib/session-analytics.js` made **two separate guards** fail: one reported a missing query, the other a missing import. **Both were plainly present** — the raw greps said 21 and 1, and the syntax check passed.
- **⚠ BOTH FAILURES POINTED AT THE EDIT, NOT AT THE CHECKER**, which is the whole danger: the natural response is to revert a correct change.
- **Fixed the ORDER in all 11 live guards** rather than the two that happened to fire. A latent parser bug held in check by an accident of the input will detonate on ordinary work, and the other nine were one comment away.
- **⚠ AND THE COMMENT EXPLAINING THE FIX TERMINATED ITSELF** by quoting a closing delimiter — the same self-inflicted case already recorded. **Describe the characters; never type them.**

### ⚠ ZOOM-SOURCED CALLS CANNOT BE GRADED FROM A LOCAL RUN (2026-08-25)
`ZOOM_CLIENT_ID` / `ZOOM_CLIENT_SECRET` exist **only on Railway** — they are not in `API Keys.md` (verified: zero matches). So `analyzeCall` on a `source='zoom'` row fails locally with *"Zoom not configured"* no matter how the environment is exported.
- **Fathom rows are unaffected**, so a local backfill still covers the overwhelming majority: 315 of 316 in the 30-day run, with the single failure being the one Zoom-sourced call.
- **⚠ IT IS NOT A DEAD END — it just has to run where the credentials are.** The deployed reanalyze path on Railway has them. **Do not conclude such a call is ungradeable; conclude it is ungradeable HERE.**

### ⚠⚠ A FILTER WHOSE SHAPE YOU ASSUMED WILL SILENTLY SELECT EVERYTHING (2026-08-25)
A run to grade Josh's last 30 days was sized at **134 calls** by SQL. The script that executed it re-derived the set with an embedded `call_analyses(status)` select and reported **315** — it would have re-graded **180 already-done calls**, roughly **$75 of spend nobody approved**.
- **THE EMBED RETURNED NOTHING FOR EVERY ROW**, so `status !== 'done'` was true for all of them. **An empty result and "no match" are indistinguishable to a filter**, and the failure direction was *include everything* — the expensive one.
- **CAUGHT BY THE COUNT DISAGREEING WITH THE SIZING QUERY**, which is the only reason it cost one call instead of two hours. **Killed after 1.**
- **⚠ THE RULE, ALREADY ON FILE FOR DESTRUCTIVE OPS, APPLIES TO EXPENSIVE ONES TOO: PIN TO VERIFIED IDS.** Select with the predicate you actually validated, **print the count**, write the ids to a file, and have the executing script read that list rather than re-deriving it. The rebuilt run took a pinned list of 135 and matched the reconciliation exactly.

### ⚠ A SCHEDULE'S NOMINAL CADENCE IS A CLAIM; ITS OBSERVED INTERVALS ARE THE MEASUREMENT (2026-08-19)
**The cron is `0 */2 * * *`. It has never once run on the hour.** Measured across 8 consecutive successful runs: **1h40m, 1h48m, 1h50m, 1h51m, 1h57m, 2h06m, 2h25m** — GitHub Actions treats scheduled workflows as best-effort and defers them under load.
- **The cost of reading the crontab instead of the history:** a time-boxed push was planned against "~22:00" derived from the expression. The real window was **22:16–23:01** and the run landed at **22:31**. The deadline was met, but the precision claimed was not earned — and the correct number was one `gh run list` away the whole time.
- **⚠ AND A STALLED WATCHER IS NOT EVIDENCE OF A BROKEN CRON.** Forty polls returning "never" were briefly reported as "the cron did not run"; the workflow history showed 8 consecutive successes. **The discriminator is the run history and the OTHER users' `last_sync_at`, not the one row you are waiting on.**
- Generalises: anything time-boxed against a periodic job derives its window from **observed run history**, and states the observed spread rather than the nominal period.

### CLIPS — WHAT THEY ARE TODAY, AND A NAME THAT NEVER EXISTED (2026-08-17)
- **A "clip" in Scout is a TIMESTAMPED DEEP LINK, not a video file:** `recording_url + ?t=<seconds>`, opening the provider's own player. Nothing is cut, stored or hosted by us. Built in six lib modules and rendered on twelve surfaces; the shared rule now lives in **`lib/clip-link.js`**.
- **THE LABEL IS PROVIDER-DEPENDENT.** Fathom's `?t=` is their documented format and **seeks** → **"Clip"**. Zoom share links carry **no timestamp parameter**, so the link opens at 00:00 → **"Open Recording"**. An unknown provider gets the cautious label: **claiming a seek we cannot deliver is the failure mode; claiming less than we deliver is merely modest.**
- **NO RECORDING URL → NO BUTTON, never a disabled one.** *"This call has no recording URL"* is information about our sync, not something a user can act on. A disabled control says "this should work and doesn't"; absence says "there is nothing to open".
- **Measured 2026-08-17: 369 of 369 REAL calls carry a recording_url, and 1,346 of 1,346 real moments are clickable.** The only rows without one are SEEDED. ⚠ A naive query says 20% of calls cannot produce a clip — that is entirely the seed, and reporting it as a product gap would send someone chasing a defect that does not exist.
- **⚠ THERE IS NO BACKLOG ITEM CALLED "FATHOM CLIP UNIFICATION".** The name was carried forward in conversation from an old note and does not appear anywhere in this file or the code. **What actually exists is ZOOM CLIP EXTRACTION (Zoom sub-stage 3): download the recording → ffmpeg-cut the segment → discard the source → store the clip.** It exists *because* Zoom has no deep link, and it is the only part that needs hosted storage. Deep-link clip buttons neither depend on it nor advance it.
- **⚠⚠ THE HABIT, AND IT IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE PART OF THIS ENTRY: WHEN A REQUEST NAMES A BACKLOG ITEM YOU CANNOT FIND, SAY YOU CANNOT FIND IT — DO NOT BIND THE REQUEST TO YOUR NEAREST MATCH.** Here the nearest match was Zoom clip extraction, an unbuilt pipeline needing hosted storage. **Assuming it was meant would have tied a THREE-LINE RENDER FIX to that pipeline** — and the honest answer ("no item exists under that name; here is what does") took one sentence and unblocked the work immediately. The failure mode is not being wrong about the name; it is inheriting the scope, cost and blockers of whatever you matched it to.

### ⚠ A FILTER THAT PARTITIONS ON A NULLABLE FIELD HAS THREE GROUPS, NOT TWO (2026-08-17)
**And the third has to be visible, or the two that show are lying by omission.**
- **The live case:** a Closed / Not Closed control on the Calls view, keyed on `call_analyses.outcome`. Measured across 335 calls: **closed 50 · not-closed 132 · NO ANALYSIS ROW AT ALL 153 (46%)**. Two buttons look like they partition the range. They do not — nearly half the calls are in neither.
- **The tempting resolution is the wrong one.** Folding ungraded calls into "not closed" makes the buttons add up, and **asserts an outcome for a call nobody has graded**. The number would be defensible-looking and false.
- **THE RULE: exclude the unknown group from BOTH sides and PRINT ITS COUNT** — here, *"153 not graded yet — in neither group"*.
- **⚠ WHAT MAKES THAT AN ACCOUNTING RATHER THAN A DISCLAIMER: the three numbers reconcile to the total BY CONSTRUCTION** (50 + 132 + 153 = 335). A reader can add up what is on screen and land on the range they selected. A caveat in prose asks to be believed; three numbers that sum ask to be checked.
- Same family as the `credited (call closed)` fourth bucket on the Objections view: **when a rate or a filter has a group nobody sees, the visible numbers stop reconciling and the page loses trust.** The fix in both cases was to show the missing group, not to explain it.

### ⚠⚠ A NON-VACUITY CHECK MUST ASSERT ITS ANCHOR EXISTS — OR A REMOVAL SILENTLY TURNS IT OFF (2026-08-17, twice in one session)
**The shape:** a guard proves it is not vacuous by breaking a literal and confirming the matcher fires —
```js
const broken = LIVE.replace('<h2>Recent Calls<', '<h2>Recent calls<');   // ← the anchor
```
**When a later commit DELETES that heading, `replace` matches nothing, returns the string unchanged, and the assertion still passes.** The guard now proves nothing and reports success. Nothing errors; the test count does not move; the only trace is a check that has quietly stopped checking.
- **It happened TWICE in one session, and BOTH times under a REMOVAL** — the Title Case guard anchored on `Recent Calls` while item (n) removed that card, and two team-view ordering tests anchored on `What Needs Work` while the objection card was retitled. A removal is exactly the change least likely to make anyone re-read a guard in a different file.
- **THE STANDING SHAPE — assert the anchor before you use it:**
```js
assert.ok(LIVE.indexOf('<h2>Coach Summary<') !== -1, 'non-vacuity anchor is stale');
const broken = LIVE.replace('<h2>Coach Summary<', '<h2>Coach summary<');
```
Now a removal fails **loudly, in the guard, naming the reason** — instead of leaving a passing test that verifies nothing.
- **⚠ A GUARD WHOSE SCOPE IS NARROWER THAN ITS RULE REPORTS SUCCESS OVER THE PART IT NEVER LOOKED AT (2026-08-17).** The Title Case guard inspected `<h2>` and `<button>` only. Scout renders several controls as `<a>` with a button class, so **three lowercase labels sat in the live page while the guard passed** — they were not missed, they were structurally invisible. Widened to cover anchor-buttons, and **re-proven non-vacuous in BOTH scopes** (breaking a heading fails the heading check; breaking an anchor-button fails the anchor check). When a rule says "buttons", enumerate what the codebase actually renders as one.
- **This generalises past non-vacuity to any test that reaches into another file by string:** ordering anchors, slice boundaries, mirror extractions. **The related rule already recorded — "every ordering anchor is asserted present before it is compared", after a conditional check passed vacuously — is the same failure; this is its non-vacuity twin.** If a test's correctness depends on a literal existing somewhere else, the test must say so.

### ⚠ DO NOT DELETE A TEST BECAUSE ITS SCAFFOLDING WENT — CONVERT IT (2026-08-17)
**A test's SUBJECT often outlives its VEHICLE, and deleting it with the feature is how a property silently stops being covered.**
- Removing the What-Needs-Work money math failed 8 tests. Four were genuinely about the removed feature and were archived in place. **Four were converted**, because what they protected still exists:
  - *"linkage Δ counts TRUE objections only"* measured its property through the linkage — which is gone. The property is not: **logistical barriers and disqualifications must stay out of the math even when they sit in closed calls.** Re-expressed through `detail.objections` and bucket totals.
  - *"rate_gap when the linkage groups are too small"* → now pins `rate_gap` as the **only** state, since there is no longer a money state to fall back from.
  - The exports test now asserts the removed constants are **absent**, so reintroducing the money lane trips it rather than passing quietly.
- **THE TEST TO APPLY: is the assertion about the thing being removed, or about a property that merely used it to get at something?** Only the first should go.

### ⚠⚠ A LIFECYCLE BUG CAN BE CAUSED ENTIRELY BY *WHERE A NODE IS EMITTED* — "WHY NOT UPDATE IN PLACE?" CAN HAVE THE ANSWER "IT WAS UNREACHABLE" (2026-08-18)
**The rebuild looked like a CHOICE and was a CONSEQUENCE. That inversion is the finding.**
- **The live case:** the three team graphs were destroyed and rebuilt on every render — measured, 15 Chart constructions and 12 destructions on one visit. The obvious question is *"why not call `update()` instead of destroy-and-recreate?"*, and the obvious answer is *"someone took a shortcut"*. **Neither is right.** Chart.js binds to a **canvas NODE**, and the canvas was emitted **inside the `innerHTML` string**. Every render replaced that node, so the existing instance pointed at a detached canvas. **`update()` was not an option that had been passed over — it was unreachable**, and destroy-and-recreate was the only thing that COULD work.
- **THE GENERAL FORM: a library that binds to a DOM node cannot update in place if the markup path replaces that node.** The defect is not in the update logic, the render logic, or the library. It is in the **emission site** — one decision about whether the node lives in a template string or outside the render cycle — and it dictates the entire lifecycle downstream.
- **⚠ SO THE FIX IS ORDERED, AND THE FIRST STEP IS WHAT MAKES THE SECOND POSSIBLE:** emit a **slot**, keep the node in a host outside the render cycle and re-append it after each assignment, and *only then* can an existing instance be updated. Reversing the order — "just call update()" — fails silently against a dead canvas.
- **The tell, worth reaching for whenever a teardown looks gratuitous: ask what the code COULD have done, not what it should have.** If the answer is "nothing else", the defect is upstream of the code you are reading. Same family as the dead-call-site rule — the component is fine and the thing that reaches it is broken — with the twist that here the *markup* is what reaches it.
- Result: **3 built, 0 destroyed, 12 in-place updates**, using `update('none')` — `'none'` because the default ANIMATES the transition, which is just a slower flash.

### ⚠⚠ A GUARD THAT ASSERTS A **CALL** RATHER THAN THE **SLOT IT NEEDS** WILL PASS ON A SURFACE THAT CANNOT HOST IT (2026-08-18)
**A parity assertion of mine passed while the parity itself was absent — and the mechanism generalises to every "we also do X over here" guard.**
- **The live case:** after preserving the three team canvases, the pivoted rep's own graph needed the same treatment. `drawRepOwnChart()` duly called `mountRepGraphHosts()`, and the guard asserted exactly that — *"PARITY: the pivoted rep graph is preserved too"*. **It passed. It was false.** That surface still emitted its canvas **inline**, so there was no `.rep-graph-slot` for the mount to find: the call ran, did nothing, and the fourth graph went on being destroyed and rebuilt while the other three no longer were.
- **⚠ WHY IT IS INSIDIOUS: the guard describes an INTENTION (we call the function) rather than a CAPABILITY (the surface can receive it).** A no-op call is indistinguishable from a working one at the call site — and the symptom, one graph still flashing while three are fixed, reads as an intermittent bug rather than as a missed call site.
- **THE RULE: assert the thing the call needs to find, not the call.** Here: the markup must contain `data-canvas="repOwnChart"` **and** must contain no inline `<canvas>`. Both are properties of the SURFACE, so they cannot be satisfied by a decorative invocation.
- **Caught by the render probe, not by reading the code** — and only because the probe executes the real path. Then proven by reverting the slot and watching the strengthened guard go red.
- Sits with the non-vacuity family: **an assertion that has never been shown to fail against the thing it names is a claim, not a check.**

### ⚠⚠ THE INK SWEEP CHANGED THE DESIGN INSTEAD OF APPROVING IT — AND "RAISE IT" MADE IT WORSE (2026-08-18)
**That is what the check is for. No amount of reasoning would have predicted the direction.**
- **The live case:** a large brand mark placed behind the login form. At the first position its strokes crossed the **Email label** — the lowest-contrast text on the page — at **4.47:1**, marginally under the 4.5:1 AA line. The obvious correction is *move it up, away from the fields*. **Raising it to 26% made the collision WORSE: 86 → 148 ink pixels**, because the mark's descending dots hang below its centre. **The intuition was backwards, and only a measurement could show it.**
- **⚠⚠ AND THE SWEEP'S OWN INVENTORY WAS SHORT, WHICH IS THE SHARPER HALF.** It checked labels, the forgot link, the button and the wordmark — and **never checked `#cardTitle` or `#cardSub`**, which sit squarely over the mark (**428** and **224** ink pixels). So the "clean" position it found was an artefact of not looking. **The scope-vs-claim rule, third instance in two days, this time inside the very check written to enforce it.**
- **THE CONSEQUENCE WAS A CHANGE OF CRITERION, NOT A NUDGE: zero overlap was never achievable at half-screen size.** Once the inventory was complete, the honest bar for a BACKGROUND is *"the worst exposed text stays readable over it"*, not *"nothing overlaps"*. Solved for opacity instead: `--muted` over a solid stroke gives 4.47:1 at 0.22 and **4.98:1 at 0.18**, so 0.18 ships. A test now computes that contrast rather than trusting the 0.16-0.30 band — **because "in the band" was true of the value that failed.**
- **BUILD THE ELEMENT INVENTORY FROM THE DOM, NOT FROM MEMORY.** The sampling was never the weak part; the **list** was.

### ⚠⚠⚠ AN IDENTITY ARRIVING FROM AN AUTHORITATIVE FIELD DOES NOT MAKE THE JOIN AUTHORITATIVE (2026-08-20)
**The trust belongs to the FIELD, not to the LINK you build from it — and the link is where the error lives.**
- **The live case:** *"the host is always the closer, so use the host instead of inferring from names."* The premise is sound — Zoom's recording carries `host_id`, it is the connected account, and Scout genuinely knows WHO the host is. **But measured on a real 1012-turn call, the VTT carries display names ONLY** — no emails, no `<v>` voice tags, no `id=`. So there is nothing on the transcript side to join an id to, and **joining `host_id` to a transcript line is the SAME NAME MATCH already refused for Fathom**, which recorded the closer as the prospect on **6 of 83 calls**.
- **⚠ THE AUTHORITATIVE SOURCE MAKES IT FEEL LIKE A DIFFERENT MECHANISM. IT IS NOT.** An id from the provider's own account record is unimpeachable; the moment it has to reach a transcript line through a display name, the join inherits the name's error rate and none of the id's certainty. **Ask what the two sides are matched ON, never where each side came from.**
- **⚠ AND NAME THE RATE YOU WOULD BE ACCEPTING, NOT THE SCENARIO.** The risk here was first described as *"occasionally wrong on an unusual setup"* — a manager hosting, a prospect starting the meeting. **That understated it by an order of magnitude:** what was actually on offer was the measured **7% name-collision rate**, with the failure mode being *the closer recorded as the prospect*. **"Host is always the closer" is a second assumption stacked on top; on its own it would be a fine trade, and on a 7%-wrong join it is not.**
- **The general form: when a proposal replaces a heuristic, check whether it removes the heuristic or merely relocates it.** Here it moved the guess from "infer the closer from conversational cues" to "assume the host's name matches this transcript label" — a different sentence, the same class of error.

### ⚠⚠⚠ A PUBLISHED CLAIM ABOUT UNBUILT BEHAVIOUR IS A COMMITMENT YOU HAVEN'T CHOSEN YET (2026-08-20)
**Describing code that does not exist inverts the order of the decision: the sentence stops being a description and becomes a specification nobody agreed to — one that is binding externally and invisible internally.**
- **The live case:** the Zoom participants scope needed a line in the published retention document. I drafted one stating Scout *"stores only a participant count"*. **Nothing was built.** It read as a privacy posture and was in fact **an unmade product decision**, published: it forecloses showing a participant list, matching prospects to Zoom identities, and recording attendance timing — **none of which anyone had ruled on.**
- **⚠ AND IT WOULD HAVE BECOME FALSE SILENTLY.** Whoever implements it later has no reason to read a PDF. The moment the code stores a name, the published claim is untrue — **and a retention statement that has quietly stopped being true is materially worse than one never made**, because it was relied upon.
- **THE ORDER IS: DECIDE WHAT IS STORED → BUILD IT → WRITE THE SENTENCE TO MATCH.** Never the reverse. If a document is needed before the code exists, the honest move is to **request the permission and publish nothing**, not to publish an aspiration.
- **⚠ THE SAME ERROR NEARLY LANDED IN THE SCOPE JUSTIFICATION TEXT**, which described the participant data as used "solely to determine how many people spoke" — true of the design, and equally unbuilt. **Two different documents, same failure**: the reviewer-facing text and the user-facing text both wanted to describe behaviour that did not yet exist.
- **⚠ IT IS THE `data-ahead-of-code` HAZARD WITH THE HALVES SWAPPED, AND THE ASYMMETRY IS WHY IT IS WORSE.** There, data landed before the code that interprets it, and the window closes when you push. Here, a **claim** lands before the code that honours it — **and there is no push that closes it.** It stays open until someone happens to re-read a published document against an implementation, which is to say indefinitely.
- **The generalisation past privacy documents:** API docs, changelogs, README behaviour, error-message wording, a scope justification, a status page. **Anywhere text is published faster than code ships, the text becomes the contract.**

### ⚠⚠⚠ A GATE THAT FAILS OPEN IS WORSE THAN NO GATE — AND YOU ONLY FIND ONE BY RUNNING IT OVER CASES IT SHOULD **FAIL** (2026-08-20)
**THE METHOD IS THE FILING: verifying a check against cases it should PASS tells you nothing about whether it can fail at all.** A green result is equally consistent with "the input was good" and "this check can no longer fail".
- **The live case:** `countBodyEmDashes` exempts the greeting line, because `"Hey Teesha —"` spends an em-dash before the body starts. The strip matched `[^\n]*` — everything up to the first newline. **It therefore swallowed EVERY em-dash on the first line.** ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-20 — the original filing of this lesson over-generalised and the correction matters more than the example.** It said *"every pre-v26 draft is ONE paragraph"*. **Counted, only ONE of the three old drafts is** — the other two have five paragraphs each and pass the paragraph-breaks gate. The accurate statement is about the LINE, not the draft:
```
old regex = strip everything to the first newline
  1-paragraph draft: first line IS the email  -> 4 raw em-dashes counted as 0  (TOTAL loss)
  5-paragraph draft: first line only          -> 4 raw counted as 2  (PARTIAL loss)
  5-paragraph draft: greeting alone on line 1 -> 6 raw counted as 5  (no loss)
```
So the fail-open was **worst on single-paragraph drafts and present on any draft whose first line carried an em-dash beyond the greeting** — a broader class than "one paragraph" and a narrower claim than the one filed. ⚠ **A lesson filed with a wrong generalisation is worse than one filed narrowly**: the example stays verifiable while the rule quietly misleads, and nobody re-checks a rule.
- **⚠ IT WAS INVISIBLE BECAUSE I ONLY EVER RAN THE GATES ON THE THREE NEW DRAFTS — the ones I expected to pass.** They pass legitimately (they have paragraph breaks), so the bug could not show. Running the same gates over the **OLD** drafts, which I expected to FAIL, is what exposed it: a draft with four em-dashes scoring zero is not a pass, it is a broken instrument.
- **⚠⚠ WHY FAIL-OPEN IS THE WORSE DIRECTION: a false FAIL gets investigated, a false PASS gets filed.** A gate that wrongly rejects something is noticed within minutes because someone is blocked. A gate that wrongly accepts reports success, the artefact ships, and nobody returns to it — the failure is indistinguishable from the feature working.
- **THE PRACTICE: for every gate, keep a fixture that MUST fail, and assert that it does.** Not as non-vacuity theatre — as the only evidence the check has any teeth. Here: a single-paragraph email with four em-dashes, pinned at 3 after the greeting.
- **⚠ AND THE FIRST FIX OVER-CORRECTED, WHICH IS ITS OWN LESSON.** Anchoring to "up to the first dash within 40 characters" then ate a genuine body dash from `"Hey Dan I have — one dash."`. **A stripper is a parser, and a parser needs its bounds stated**: greeting word + at most two capitalised name tokens + its own punctuation, or nothing at all. Eight cases pinned, including "no salutation punctuation → strip nothing".

### ⚠⚠ A TEMPLATE ELEMENT CAN SATISFY THE CONSTRAINT, SO THE CONSTRAINT STOPS DESCRIBING THE PROSE (2026-08-20)
**Same shape as the unreachable-bound entry below, seen from the other side: there, the template CONSUMED the whole budget so the rule could never pass; here, the template SATISFIES the rule so it can never fail. Both leave a constraint that no longer measures the writing.**
- **The live case:** `short_sentence` asks whether the email contains a sentence under six words — a proxy for the sentence-length variance that distinguishes human prose from generated prose. Measured naively over the raw email, **the one-word sign-off (`"Joshua"`) splits out as a "sentence" of length 1**. v25 pins a sign-off on every email, so **the check was TRUE for every email ever produced** — always passing, measuring nothing, and reported in a table as though it meant something.
- **THE RULE: any gate over generated text must first ask WHAT THE TEMPLATE ALREADY CONTRIBUTES.** A greeting, a sign-off, a subject line, a boilerplate footer, a required disclaimer — each is fixed, each is not the writing, and each can silently satisfy or exhaust a rule aimed at the writing. **Exclude the fixed parts, then measure.**
- **⚠ THE TELL IS A CHECK THAT IS ALWAYS THE SAME ANSWER.** If a measurement over real, varied inputs never changes value, it is describing the template rather than the content. Here every draft scored `yes`; after excluding greeting and sign-off, one correctly scored `no`.
- Both instances came from the same feature in the same week, which is the argument for asking the question routinely rather than noticing it twice.

### ⚠⚠⚠ A DERIVED VALUE INHERITS THE ASSUMPTIONS OF THE CASE IT WAS DERIVED FROM — AND "THE ARTWORK" IS NOT ONE CASE WHEN IT CONTAINS BOTH STROKES AND FILLS (2026-08-20)
**The number was correct for the element it was derived against, and the binding element was a different one. Nothing in the value says which case produced it.**
- **The live case:** the login mark's background layer sits at `opacity: 0.21`, derived — repeatedly, carefully, across four blocks — by solving *"does low-contrast text stay above 4.5:1 over the mark's ink?"*. **Every one of those solves was text on a 1-3px STROKE.** The mark also contains three solid `fill` discs. Measured at 845px tall: the stroke renders **1.58px** (correctly inside the product's 1-3px line work) while the largest disc is **39.5px across — 25x the stroke width, and fully filled.** Same alpha, 25x the ink per unit area.
- **⚠ THE SYMPTOM WAS NOT A CONTRAST FAILURE, WHICH IS WHY NO CONTRAST CHECK COULD FIND IT.** Contrast cleared AA at 4.58:1. What broke was **perceptual category**: the thin arcs read as TEXTURE and the solid discs read as **UI CONTROLS** — one landing between the password field and "Forgot password?", another under the Log In button, both reading as a status dot or a radio. **A user does not experience "4.58:1"; they experience "is that a button?"**
- **⚠⚠ SO IT MUST NOT BE FIXED WITH THE DERIVED VALUE.** Lowering the layer opacity would dim the strokes (which are correct) in order to fix the discs (which are not) — treating a symptom in the wrong element, and the standing *offering-a-lever-for-a-symptom-whose-cause-is-elsewhere* trap. **The fix is disc-specific alpha**: `fill-opacity 0.3` puts the discs at an effective 0.063, a green delta of +13/255 — at the visibility threshold — while the rings keep their weight.
- **⚠ AND MOVING THE ARTWORK WAS CONSIDERED AND REJECTED, FOR A RECORDED REASON.** *"Place it so no disc lands on an interactive element"* sounds like the permanent fix and is the **one-width trap**: the form shifts with viewport height and grows when an error message appears, so "no overlap" is a RELATIONSHIP between two things that both move and can only be verified at the viewport you checked. It is the same unreachable zero-overlap criterion that already failed the ink sweep below 1600px. **Disc-specific alpha is viewport-independent and needs no re-verification when the form changes.**
- **THE GENERAL RULE: before reusing a derived value, ask WHICH CASE it was derived from and whether the thing you are applying it to is that case.** A value derived for thin ink does not govern thick ink; one derived for a short string does not govern a long one; one derived for a warm cache does not govern a cold one. **The derivation, not the result, is what transfers** — the same lesson as *a criterion is not a constant*, arrived at from inside a single artefact rather than across two surfaces.
- **⚠ IT WAS FOUND BY LOOKING, AND ONLY BY LOOKING.** Four blocks of contrast measurement, an ink sweep, and an analytic opacity derivation all passed. **One screenshot of the form showed it instantly.** File beside *measurement is not a composition test*.

### ⚠⚠ A WHITE-INK RESULT ON A VISIBLY GREEN MARK IS A STATEMENT ABOUT THE INSTRUMENT (2026-08-20)
**Caught before reporting eight AA failures that do not exist — which is the win, and the reason this is filed as a practice rather than as an incident.**
- **The live case:** the owed ink sweep read `getComputedStyle(body, '::before').color` as the mark's ink. That property returns the **inherited TEXT colour** — `[240,244,247]`, near-white — not the artwork's `#09e046`. Composited against near-white, **every low-contrast element came back at 3.85:1 and the sweep reported 8 elements below AA.**
- **THE TELL WAS FREE AND VISIBLE: the mark is unmistakably GREEN in the screenshot taken thirty seconds earlier.** A result asserting white ink contradicted a fact already in hand. Recomputed against the real ink parsed out of the data URI: **4.58:1, clearing AA.**
- **⚠ WHAT ACTING ON IT WOULD HAVE COST — worse than a wasted block: it would have triggered a REAL change to fix a FAKE problem.** Eight AA failures is an unarguable brief; the layer would have been dimmed until the phantom numbers cleared, permanently, on a page whose contrast was already fine — and nobody re-derives a value that was changed for a documented accessibility reason.
- **THE RULE, which is the standing one applied to a property read rather than a search: when a check reports a problem, confirm the CHECK is sound before acting on what it says — and the cheapest confirmation is whether its output contradicts something you can already see.** Same family as the opaque-gradient nav bar (over-reported), the comment-swallowing regex (under-read), and the whole-stylesheet grep (matched outside its subject). **Here the failure was reading the WRONG PROPERTY — a fourth mechanism, one verdict shape.**
- **⚠ AND THE FIX IS TO READ THE SOURCE OF TRUTH, NOT A NEIGHBOURING PROPERTY.** The ink lives in the `background-image` data URI. Parsing it (`decodeURIComponent`, then match `stroke=`/`fill=`) is three lines and cannot be confused with an inherited value.

### ⚠⚠⚠ A FILTER TESTED ON SYNTHETIC INPUT MEASURES YOUR IMAGINATION, NOT THE DATA (2026-08-20)
**Justin's form of it, and it is exact: invented test material would have passed the loose filter, BECAUSE YOU WOULD HAVE INVENTED CLEAN LINES.** The fixture and the filter come from the same head, so they agree by construction — and the agreement reads as validation.
- **The live case:** `lib/closer-voice.js` filter 1 selects the closer's own clean lines to ground the follow-up email. My first draft passed **62%** of Josh's 413 real verified lines, and two of the accepted ones were noise — a self-repair the duplicate-word test missed (*"that's like, you can, that's what you can"*) and a lowercase mid-sentence fragment. Tightened against the real corpus to **30% (~124 lines)**.
- **⚠ NO INVENTED EXAMPLE WOULD HAVE CAUGHT EITHER.** Asked to write a "messy spoken line", you produce `"um, so like, I think maybe"` — the disfluencies you already thought to filter. **Real speech fails in ways you did not think of**, which is the entire reason the filter is hard: `"that's not the, the real estate's not the hard part"` is a stammer, and `"I mean, do you have, do you have room on like a credit card by chance?"` is a self-repair, and neither looks like the caricature.
- **THE RULE: a filter, a classifier, or a validator is calibrated against the REAL POPULATION IT WILL SEE, and the pass rate is reported.** A synthetic suite is fine for pinning *behaviour you have already decided on*; it cannot tell you where the threshold belongs.
- **AND THE PASS RATE IS THE DIAGNOSTIC.** 62% on a corpus of spoken transcript lines should have been implausible on its face — real speech is not two-thirds clean. **A suspiciously generous pass rate is evidence the filter is measuring the fixture.**
- Same family as *measurement is not a composition test* and the minimum-sample rule: **a check that can pass by being shown easy input will be, and it will report success.**
